  - 65 questions over 130 minutes - 70% to pass  - cost $150
  - certification valid for 3 yr
  - need to create an AWS certification account

 to schedule an examine
 https://aws.amazon.com/certification/certified-solutions-architect-associate/

AWS Exam Guide Link:

   file:///C:/Users/pat/AppData/Local/Temp/AWS-Certified-Solutions-Architect-Associate_Exam-Guide.pdf


  - Examine split over 4 domains:
     - Design Resilient Architectures - 30%
       - Design a multi-tier architecture solution
       - Design highly available and/or fault-tolerent architecture solution
       - Design decoupling mechanisms using AWS services
       - Choose appropriate resilient storage

     - Designing Secure Applications and Architectures - 24 %
       - Identify elastic and scalable compute solutions for a workload
       - Select high-performance and scalable storate solutions for a workload
       - Select high-performance networking solutions for a workload
       - Choose high-performance database solutions for a workload

     - Designing High-Performance Architectures - 28 %
       - Design secure access to AWS resources
       - Design secure application tiers
       - Select appropriate data security options

     - Design Cost-Optimized Architectures - 18 %
       - Identify cost effective storage solutions
       - Identify cost effective compute and database solutions
       - Design cost-optimized network architectures


   - Pass Mark:
       - scores from 100 - 1000
       - min passing mark is 720.
       - 65 questions and 130 minutes to complete

Chapter 3 The History of AWS so far
---------------------------------------------------
3.1 The History of AWS so far
  - launched SQS in 2004
  - AWS Officially launched in 2006
  - 180K developers by 2007
  - 2010 all of amazon.com moved to AWS
  - 2012 First Re-Invent Conference
  - 2013 Certifications launched

---------------------------------------------------
3.2 AWS - 10000-foot overview

   - Global Infrastructure
     - compute (e.g.: EC2, lambda)
     - storage (e.g: S3, EFS, ...)
     - Database (e.g. Relational - RDS; non-relational - Dimo DB)

     - migration  & Transfer (e.g. snowball)
     - network & content delivery (e.g. VPC, Cloud Front)
     - developer Tools (not relevant for Solution Arch)

     - Robotics
     - Blockchain
     - Satellite

     - Management & Goverence
     - Media Services
     - Machine Learning (only need to know about)

     - Analytics
     - Security, Identity, & Compliance
     - Mobile

     - AR (augmented reality) & VR (Virtual Reality)
     - Application Integration
     - AWS Cost Management

 - Global Infrastructure
      - 19 Regions & 57 Availability Zones - Dec 2018
         - more regions & 15 more AZs for 2019
      - AZ is a 1 or more datacenters within a short distance (e.g. a mile)
      - A region is geographical area
        - A region consists of 2 or more AZs
      - Edge locations
        - endpoints for AWS which are used for caching content
        - Typically this consists of CloudFront, AWS's Content Delivery Network (CDN)
        - more Edge Locations than Regions
        - in 2019, 150 Edge Locations

    - what do you need to know to pass Solutions Arch Exam?
      - compute (ec2 & lambda), storage (s3), databases (RDS, Dynamo DB, Redshift)
      - network & content delivery (Route 53 [DNS service], VPCs)
      - Security, Identity, & Compliance (Identity Access Management)

    - Exam tips
       - difference between a region, an AZ, and an Edge Location
         - Region
            - physical location in the world which consists of 2 or more AZs
         - an Availabilty Zone
            - 1 or more data centers, each with redundant power, networking and connectivity
           housed in separate facilities
          - Edge locations
            - endpoints for AWS which are used for caching content
            - Typically this consists of CloudFront, AWS's Content Delivery Network (CDN)

Chapter 3 Exam questions:

   1. What is a Amazon VPC - virtual private cloud
   2. Which of the below are factors that have helped make public cloud so powerful:
      - the ease of trying new solutions
      - pay-as-you-go approach for pricing

   3 AWS storage services:
     - EFS and S3
   4. The VPC service is a member of which group of AWS services in the 'All Services' view of the AWS portal
      - networking & Content Delivery
   5. Which of the below are AWS Compute services
      - lambda & EC2
   6. Select the option that shows the correct order from greatest to least
      - Number of Edge locations, Number of  AZ, Number of Regions
   7. Which of the below are database services from AWS
      DynamoDB, Amazon RDS

      DynamoDB is a fast and flexible non-relational database service for any scale.
        DynamoDB enables customers to offload the administrative burdens of operating and scaling
        distributed databases to AWS so that they don’t have to worry about hardware provisioning,
        setup and configuration, throughput capacity planning, replication, software patching, or cluster scaling.

      Amazon Relational Database Service (Amazon RDS) is a managed service that makes it easy to set up, operate,
      and scale a relational database in the cloud. Amazon RDS gives you access to the capabilities of a
      familiar MySQL, MariaDB, Oracle, SQL Server, or PostgreSQL database.

   8 What does an AWS Region Consist of
     - Each AWS Region consists of multiple, isolated, and physically separate AZ's within a geographic area.

   9. What is an AWS Region
     - A region is a geographical area divided into Availability Zones. Each region contains at least two Availability Zones.
   10. In which of the following is CloudFront content cached?
      - Edge Location
   11. Which statement best describes Availability Zones
      -  a distinct location within an AWS Region that are engineered to be isolated from failures.
         Each Region comprises at least two AZs.

   12. Which of the following are part of AWS' Networking & Content Delivery services
      - VPC & CloudFront

3.7 AWS Certified Solution Architect - Associate (SS-C03) 0 Identity Access Management Quiz

Question 4
Why are IAM users considered "permanent" users?

Choices:
 - Because once the password for the account is set, it can never be changed.
 - IAM users aren't permanent; they only last for a few weeks before they "expire."
 - Because once their password, access key, or secret key is set, these credentials don't automatically rotate or change without human interaction.
 - Because once they are created, they can't be deleted.

Answer
 - Because once their password, access key, or secret key is set, these credentials don't automatically rotate or change without human interaction.

These user credentials don't automatically rotate, making them "permanent" without manual human interaction. Reference Documentation: IAM Users


Question 5
What does the "EAR" in a policy document stand for?

Choices
 - Every, Action, Reasonable
 - Ewoks, Always, Romanticize
 - Effects, APIs, Roles
 - Effect, Action, Resource

Answer:
 - Effect, Action, Resource

The elements included in a policy document that make up the "EAR" are effect, action, and resource. Reference Documentation: Policies and Permissions in IAM

SAA-C03 Chapter 2 AWS Fundamentals

2.5 AWS Fundamental Exam Tips

  Building Blocks
    region: is a physical l location containing at least two Availability Zones.
    AZ: is one or more discrete data centers - each with redundant power, networking, and connectivity -
         housed in separate facilities
    Edge Location: are endpoints for aWS that are used for caching content. Typically, this consists of
         CloudFront, AWS's content delivery network (CDN)

  Shared Responsibility Model:
      - asked yourself - can you do this yourself in the AWS Console?
         - yes, then you are likely responsible
            - e.g.: Security Groups, IAM users, patching EC2 OS, patching Databases running on EC2, etc.
         - No, then you are likely responsible
            - e.g.: Management of data centers, security cameras, cabling, patching RDS OS, etc.
     - Encryption is a shared responsibility

  Key Services to know:
     Compute: EC2, Lambda, Elastic Beanstalk
     Storage: S3, EBS, EFS, FSx, Storage Gateway
     Databases: RDS, DynamoDB, Redshift
     Networking: VPCs, Direct Connect, Route 53 (DNS service), API Gateway, AWS Global Accelerator
        API Gateway: fully managed service to create, publish, maintain, monitor secure API at any scale
        Global Accelerator: provides static IP address for different AWS regions and AZ. always routes
               user traffic to optimal endpoints based on performance, reacting to changes in application
               health, your user locations, and policies your configure

  Read the whitepaper "Well Architected Framework Whitepaper
     paper:
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/framework/welcome.html
     webite:
     https://aws.amazon.com/architecture/well-architected/?wa-lens-whitepapers.sort-by=item.additionalFields.sortDate&wa-lens-whitepapers.sort-order=desc&wa-guidance-whitepapers.sort-by=item.additionalFields.sortDate&wa-guidance-whitepapers.sort-order=desc

SAA-C03 Chapter 3 IAM

3.3 IAM Exam Tips

  Steps to Secure your AWS Root Account
    - Enable MFA on the root account
    - Create an admin group for your adminstrators, and assign the appropriate permissions to this group
    - create users accounts for adminstrators
    - add your admin users to the admin group

  Assign Permissions using IAM policy JSON documents
       - policies
          - you apply policies to users, groups, and roles to give or restrict access
          - policies use json, e.g. administratorAccess policy: (allows any access):
                {
                    "Version": "2012-10-17",
                    "Statement": [
                        {
                            "Effect": "Allow",
                            "Action": "*",
                            "Resource": "*"
                        }
                    ]
                }

   Remember the following:
     - IAM is universal
     - Root account: account created when your first setup your AWS account. It has complete
          Admin access. Secure it ASAP, and do not use it to log in day to day
     - New Users: no permissions when first created

   Permanent IAM Credentials:
      - Access key & secret access key are not the same a usernames and passwords
      - you get to view these keys once
      - Always set up password rotation

   Identity Provider (IdP)
     - Your external users sign in through a well-known IdP, such as Amazon, Facebook, or Googgle
     - you give those external indentites permissions to use AWS resources in your account
     - to use IdP, you create an IAM identity provider entity to establish a trust relationship between
       the IdP, and your AWS account
     -  IAM supports IdPs that are comptible with OpenID Connect (OIDC) or SAML 2.0 (Security Assertion
        Markup Language 2.0)

   Note: Identity Provider and Federation are similar concepts, used by different implementations
         (Identity provider - MS Active Directory; Federation -> SAML)?
         AD -> centralized identity source
         SAML -> standard for authentication process (for example to AD)


         Federation is the trust relationship that exists between these organizations; it is concerned
         with where the user’s credentials are actually stored and how trusted third-parties can authenticate
         against those credentials without actually seeing them.

   IAM Federation
     - you can combine your existing your account with AWS
        - for example, when you log on to your PC (using using Microsoft Active Directory), you can use the
          same credentials to log in to AWS if you set up federation
    - Identity Federation
      - Uses SAML (Security Assertion Mark up Language) standard, which Active Directory

    AWS Security Token Service:
      - AWS provides AWS Security Token Service (AWS STS) as a web service that enables you to request temporary,
        limited-privilege credentials for AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) users or for users you
        authenticate (federated users)

SAA-C03 Chapter 4 S3

4.11 S3 Exam Tips

   S3 Overview
     - object based storage
     - Files up to 5TB
     - Not OS or DB storage
     - unlimited storage

     - Files are stored in Buckets
     - S3 is universal namespace
        https://<bucketName>.s3.amazonaws.com/<keyName> (in N. Virginia)

        https://s3-<Region>.amazonaws.com/<bucketName>/<keyName> (all other regions)
        https://s3-eu-west-1.amazonaws.com/acloudguru/cat1.jpeg

        https://<bucketName>.s3-<Region>.amazonaws.com/<keyName>
        https://acloudguru.s3-eu-west-1.amazonaws.com/<keyName>
     - Successfull CLI or API uploads will generated an HTTP 200 status code


    S3 Object Tips:
       - key - object name
       - value - contents/data
       - Version ID - allows you to store multiple versions of the same object
       - Metadata - e.g. content-type, last modified, etc.

    Securing your bucket with S3
      - Buckets are private by default
          - you have to allow public access on both the bucket and its objects in order to make the
            bucket public
      - Object ACLs:
          - you can make individual objects public using object ACLs
      - Bucket Policies:
          - you can make entire buckets public using bucket policies

   Hosting a Static Website
      - bucket policies: you can make entire bucket public using bucket policies
      - Static Content:  you can use S3 to host static content only (not dynamic)
      - Automatic Scaling: S3 Scales automatically with demand
           - no need for load balancer

   Versioning Objects
     - All versions: all versions of an object are stored in S3. This includes all writes and even if
       you delete an object (unless you explicit delete the specific version of the object).
       - when you delete an object with versioning enabled, it place a delete marker on the object,
         but it does not delete the object
     - Backup: can be a great backup tools
     - Cannot not be disabled: once enabled, version cannot be disabled - only suspended
     - Lifecycle Rules: can be integrated with lifecycle rules
     - Supports MFA: can support multi-factor authentication


   Storage Classes                        Avail/Durability     AZ(s)    Use Cases
   ---------------                       -----------------    -------   --------------------------------------------
   s3 standard                           99.99 Availablity     >= 3     Suitable for most workloads (e.g. websites, content distribution,
                                         11 9's Durability              mobile, and gaming applications, & big data analytics
   s3 standared Infrequently Accesss     99.99 Availablity     >= 3     Long term, infrequently accesses critical data (e.g. backups,
                                         11 9's Durability              data stored for disaster recovery file, etc)
   s3 one Zone Infrequently Accesss      99.5  Availablity        1     Long term, infrequently accessed, non-critical data
                                         11 9's Durability
   S3 Glacier Instant Retrieval          99.99 Availablity     >= 3     Archive data that needs immediate access such as medical images,
                                         11 9's Durability              news media  assets, or user generated archives
   S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval         99.99 Availablity     >= 3     Long-term data archiviing that occansionally needs to be
                                         11 9's Durability              accessed within a few hours or minutes
   S3 Glacier Deep Archive               99.99 Availablity     >= 3     Rarely accessed data archiving with a default retrieval time
                                         11 9's Durability              of 12 hours (e.g. financial records for regulatory purposes)
   S3 Intelligent Tiering                99.99 Availablity     >= 3     unknown or unpredicable access patterns
                                         11 9's Durability
   S3 Reduced Redundancy (depreciated)   99.99 Availablity
                                         11 9's Durability

  Lifecycle Management
    - automates moving your objects between the different storage tiers
    - can be used in conjunction with versioning
    - can be applied to current versions and previous versions

  S3 Object Lock and Glacier Vault Lock
    S3 Object Lock:
      - use to store objects using write once, read many (WORM) model
      - can be used on individual objects or applied across the bucket as a whole
      - modes
        - goverance mode
           - user can't overwrite or delete an object version or alter its locks setting unless they have special permissions
        - compliance mode
           - user can't overwrite or delete an object version or alter its locks setting by any user including root user
    S3 Glacier Vault Lock:
      - allows you to deploy and enforce compliance controls for individual S3 Glacier Vaults with a vault lock policy
      - can specify controls, such as WORM, in a vault lock policy from future edits
      - once locked, the policy can no longer be changed

  Encryption with S3
    - Encryption At Rest (server side) is achieved by:
       - S3 Managed Keys - SSE-S3 (server side encryption S3)
       - AWS Key Management Service
         - Managed Keys - SSE-KMS (Server Side Encryption - Key Management Service)
         - User and AWS manage keys together
       - Server Side Encryption with Customer Provided Keys SSE-C
          - (Server Side Encryption - Customer)
          - customer upload file with key, and uses key, then, deletes key
      Note: To configure S3 server-side encryption by using the object creation REST APIs, you must provide the
            "x-amz-server-side-encryption" request header.
    - Encryption At Rest (client side) is achieved by:
       - user encrypts file and then uploads encrypted file


   - Enforcing Encryption with bucket policy
     - a bucket policy can deny all PUT requests that don't include x-amz-server-side-encryption
       parameter in the request header


  Optimizing S3 performance
    S3 prefixes
      prefix example:                                prefix
      mybucketname/folder1/subfolder1/myfile.jpg     /folder1/subfolder1

    S3 Performance
    - you can achieve a high number of requests: 3500 PUT/COPY/POST/DELETE and 5500 GET/HEAD requests per second per prefix
    - you can achieve better performance by spreading reads across different prefixes.
    - For example, if you are using 2 prefixes, you can active 11K GET/HEAD requests per second
      and 7000 PUT/COPY/POST/DELETE requests per sec

   S3 Limitations when using SSE-KMS (Server Side Encryption - Key Management Service)
     - uploading/downloading will count towards the KMS quota
     - Currently, you cannot request a quota increase for KMS
     - Region-specific quota, but it either 5500, 10k, or 30k requests per sec

   Multipart (uploads) and S3 byte-range fetches (downloads)
     - use multipart uploads to increase performance when uploading a file
     - multipart upload recommended for files over 100MB
     - multipart upload required for files over 5GB
     - use S3 Byte-range fetches to increase performance when downloading files from S3


  S3 Replication

    - originally, cross region replication, but now you can also can replicate a bucket in the same region
    - you can replicate objects for one bucket to another
    - objects in existing bucket are not replicated automatically
       - however, when creating replication bucket, it now ask you if you want to replicate exist objects
    - by default, delete markers are not replicate
          - Now, it gives you an option to replicate delete markers when create replication rule
      - deleting individual versions or delete markers will not be replicated

Chapter 4 Identity and Access Management (IAM) and S3
---------------------------------------------------

4.1 Identity Access Management 101
  Resources:
   https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies_job-functions.html

  - What is IAM
    - IAM allows you to manage user and their level of access to the AWS console
      - Create user and grant permissions to those users
      - Create groups and roles
      - Control acces to AWS resources

  - key features of IAM
    - centralized control of your AWS account
    - Shared access to your AWS account
    - Granular permissions
    - identity federation (including Active Directory, Facebook, Linkedin, etc)
      - user could use their same login to their PC to login to AWS
    - Multifactor Authentification
    - Provide temporary access for users/devices and services where necessary
    - allows you to set up your own password rotation policy
    - integrates with many different AWS services
    - Support PCI DSS (Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard) Compliance
       - compliant framework for taking credit card details


  - Key terminology for IAM
    - Users - end users such as people, employees, etc.
    - Groups - A collection of users. Each user in the group will inherit the permissions of the group
             - functions such as administrator, developers, etc - contains users
    - Roles - you create roles then assign them to AWS resources
       - Internal usage within AWS - allow one resource access another resource
    - Policies -  made up of documents called policy documents.
         - in JSON format and they give permissions as to what a user/group//role is able to do


---------------------------------------------------
4.2 Identity and Access Management - Demo

   - go to IAM
   - add MFA to root account
     - do not want to login to root account - instead create user accounts
     - in console upper left corner, select account -> Security Credentials -> MFA
       - Assign MFA device
       - download Google Authenticator APP
            - types Authentificator, Security Key, Hardware TOTP token
            - take a picture so you have a backup of it
            - scan barcode, enter next to codes, accept
   - IAM is global

    - add a user (pat)
       - IAM -> users -> add users -> Pat
         -> add group -> Admin -> AdminAccess policy -> create group
            -> "add user to group" -> next -> review -> create user
            -> add user to Admin group
    - add access keys
      - download access keys CSV

   -> Account Settings -> password policy



   administratorAccess policy: (allows any access)
	{
	    "Version": "2012-10-17",
	    "Statement": [
	        {
	            "Effect": "Allow",
	            "Action": "*",
	            "Resource": "*"
	        }
	    ]
	}

  - Roles
    - allows 1 AWS service use another AWS service
  -> Create Role -> select "AWS Service" -> Use Case "EC2" -> next
     -> Add permissions select "AmazonS3FullAccess" -> next
     -> Role Name "S3_Admin_Access" -> Create role

  - Exam Tips
      - IAM is universal (global)
      - The "root account" is simply the account created when first setup your AWS account.
        It has complete Admin access
      - New users have no permissions when first created
      - Optionally, New user are assigned Access Key ID & Secret Access Keys when 1st created
        for programmatic access
      - only get to view new users password, Access Key, & Secret Access Key once so save
        them in a secure location, else if lost, you have to regenerate them

      - Always setup Multifactor Authentication on your root account
      - can create and customize your own password rotation policy

 -> login to AWS console -> go to "CloudWatch"
    -> in left side -> Alarms -> Billing
      -> Note "Create Alarm" (top left is generic), bottom center is billing Alarm
         -> threshold type: "static", whenever Estimated charge is "greater" than "10"

            email ... topic, alarm name, next, create alarm

 exam tip:
   -> use "cloudwatch" to create billing alarms when account exceeds specified threshold

4.4. S3 101

   What is S3 (Simple Storage Service)
      - secure, durable, highly scalable object storage
      - store and retreive any amount of data
      - data is stored across multiple devices at multiple facilities
      - files can be from 0 to 5 TB
      - unlimited storage
      - files are stored in buckets (file)
      - s3 is a universal namespace - bucket names must be unique globally
      - bucket name
          https://<bucketname.s3.amazonaws.com  (N. Virginia)
          or
          https://<bucketname>.<awsRegion>.amazonaws.com  (other Regions)
      - HTTP 200 code if upload was successful

    S3 Objects:
      - key (name of object)
      - value (object data)
      - version ID
      - metadata
      - subresources
         - access control lists
         - torrent

    S3 data consistency
      - strong read after write consistency for PUTS of new objects. This behavior applies to both writes to new objects
        as well as PUT requests that overwrite existing objects and DELETE requests.
      - before, but not now: eventual consistency of overwrite PUTS and DELETES (can take time to propagate)
      - Note: Bucket configurations have an eventual consistency model. If you delete a bucket and immediately
        list all buckets, the deleted bucket might still appear in the list.


    S3 guarantees
      - built for 99.99% availability
      - AWS guarantees 99.9% availability
      - AWS guarantees 99.999999999% durability (11 x 9s)

    S3 features
      - tiered storaage
      - lifecycle management (change tier after time periods)
      - versioning
      - encryption at reset
      - MFA deletes (when versioning is enabled)
      - Secure your data using Access Control Lists and Bucket Policies

    S3 Storage classes
       - S3 Standard
         - 99.99% availability
         - 99.999999999% durability (11 x 9s)
         - designed to sustain the lost of 2 facilities concurrently
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 IA (infrequently accessed)
         - for data accessed less frequently, but requires rapid access when needed
         - lower fee than S3, but charged a retrieval fee
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 One Zone IA (infrequently accessed)
         - lower cost option for infrequently accessed data,
           but do not require multiple availability & data resilience
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 Intelligent Tiering
         - uses machine learning to optimize your storage costs
         - optimize costs by automatically moving data to the most cost-effective access tier
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 Glacier
         - secure, durable, and lower cost storage classes for data archiving
         - retrieval times configurable from minutes to hours
       - S3 Glacier Deep Archive
         - lowest cost storage class
         - retrieval time of 12 hours is acceptable

    S3 Charges:
      - Storage
      - requests
      - storage management pricing
      - data transfer pricing
      - transfer acceleration
      - cross region replication


     S3 Cross Region replication
      - if enable, bucket data is automatically replicated in specified other region

     S3 transfer acceleration
       - enables fast, easy, and secure transfers of files over long
         distances btween your end users and an S3 bucket
       - use CloudFront globally distributed edge locations
       - as the data arrives at edge endpoint, data is routed to S3 over optimized network path

  Exam Tips:
    - S3 is object based
    - filed can be from 0 bytes to 5 TB
    - unlimited storage
    - files are stored in buckets
    - S3 is a universal namespace - bucket names must be globally unique
    - buckets use DNS name:
          https://<bucketName>.s3.amazonaws.com (in N. Virginia)
          https://<bucketName>.<awsRegion>.amazonaws.com (anywhere else)
    - not suitable to install an operating system or database on (need block base storage)
    - Successfull uploads will generate a HTTP 200 status code
    - you can turn MFA delete (when versioning is enabled)

    S3 Key fundamentals:
      - key (name of object)
      - value (object data)
      - version ID
      - metadata
      - subresources
         - access control lists
         - torrent
    S3 data consistency
       - read after write consistency for PUTS of new objects
       - eventual consistency of overwrite PUTS and DELETES (can take time to propagate)

    S3 Storage classes
       - S3 Standard
         - 99.99% availability
         - 99.999999999% durability (11 x 9s)
         - designed to sustain the lost of 2 facilities concurrently
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 IA (infrequently accessed)
         - for data accessed less frequently, but requires rapid access when needed
         - lower fee than S3, but charged a retrieval fee
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 One Zone IA (infrequently accessed)
         - lower cost option for infrequently accessed data,
           but do not require multiple availability & data resilience
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 Intelligent Tiering
         - uses machine learning to optimize your storage costs
         - optimize costs by automatically moving data to the most cost-effective access tier
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 Glacier
         - secure, durable, and lower cost storage classes for data archiving
         - retrieval times configurable from minutes to hours
       - S3 Glacier Deep Archive
         - lowest cost storage class
         - retrieval time of 12 hours is acceptable

       S3 RRS (Reduced Redundancy Storage) - phased out
          - similar to S3 One Zone IA


       Read S3 FAQ before taking the exam
         e.g.; https://aws.amazon.com/s3/faqs/

4.5 Create an S3 Bucket Demo


 -> S3 console -> S3 -> create bucket -> Bucket name "pathall1stbucket" use defaults -> create bucket
    -> upload some pics
    -> examine objects in bucket -> each has a URL
    https://<bucketName>.s3.amazonaws.com/<objectName>
    -> examine storage classes -> edit storage class
    -> bucket -> Permission -> "block public access" -> edit -> unselect "Block all public access" -> save changes
    -> now select object -> Permissions -> Edit Object Ownership -> ACL Enabled
    -> select object -> Actions -> Make a public using ACLs -> make public


  - by default, public access of your buckets are blocked
  - ownership - ACL disabled, ACLs enabled (default: ACLs disabled)
  - Public Access options - default: block all public access
  - bucket option
     - bucket versioning (default: disabled)
     - default encryption [default: Server-side encryption with Amazon S3 managed keys (SSE-S3)]
     - bucket key (default: enabled)

   -> Exam Tips

        - cannot have a same bucketname as someone else
     - When you view your buckets you view them globally but
       you can have buckets in individual regions
     - you can replicate the contents of one bucket to another bucket automatically
       by using "cross region replication"
     - you cna change storage classes and encryption of your objects on the fly

     Restricting bucket access
      - bucket policies - Applies across the whole bucket
      - object Policies - Applies to individual files
      - IAM policies to Users & Groups - Applies to Users & Groups

     S3 transfer acceleration
       - enables fast, easy, and secure transfers of files over long
         distances between your end users and an S3 bucket
       - use CloudFront globally distributed edge locations
       - as the data arrives at edge endpoint, data is routed to S3 over optimized network path

4.7 S3 Security & Encryption

 S3 Basics
   - by default, all newly create buckets are PRIVATE.
   - You can setup access to your buckets using:
      - Bucket policies (work at bucket level)
      - Access Control Lists (work at object levels)
   - buckets can configured to create access logs
      - log all requests made to the S3 bucket

      - can be sent to another bucket and even another bucket in another account

   - Encryption in transit is achieved by:
      - SSL/TLS (https) (Secure Socker Layer / Transport Layer Security)
         - Note: TLS is a replacement for SSL
   - Encryption At Rest (server side) is achieved by:
       - S3 Managed Keys - SSE-S3 (server side encryption S3)
       - AWS Key Management Service
         - Managed Keys - SSE-KMS (Server Side Encryption - Key Management Service)
         - User and AWS manage keys together
       - Server Side Encryption with Customer Provided Keys
          - (Server Side Encryption - Customer)
   - Encryption At Rest (client side) is achieved by:
       - user encrypts file and then uploads encrypted file


  - Encryption be set a bucket and object level
    - by default, bucket SSE-S3 encryption was enabled (was not previously the case)

  Exam tips:
    - can encrypt individual objects and set encryption at bucket level
---------------------------------------------------
4.8 S3 Versioning - Demo

Using Versioning in S3 buckets:
   https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/Versioning.html

   Versioning with S3
     - stores all versions of an object (including all writes and even if delete an object)
        - For delete requests, S3 stores a delete marker instead of deleting the object permantently
     - great backup tool
     - once enabled, Versioning cannot be disabled, only suspended
     - integrates with Lifecycle rules
     - by default, versioning is disabled

   -> Lab
      -> create s3 bucket (s3versiondemobucket) and a text file
         - enable public access and enable versioning
         - enable ACLs so object can be made public
         - upload file version 1 and make public
         - upload file version 2 and make public
         - upload file version 3 and make public
         Note: each object's version is private until individual version is made public
         - select object and go to "version" tab to see the 3 versions

         - delete object from bucket
           - object is gone, but if you enable "show versions", the 3 versions are still there
             - instead object has a "delete marker"
        - to restore the object, "delete" the "delete marker"

        - to permanently delete, you need to delete each version individually

  Exam tips:
    Versioning
      - stores all versions of an object (including all writes and even if you delete an object)
     - great backup tool
     - once enabled, Versioning cannot be disabled, only suspended
     - integrates with Lifecycle rules
     - Version's MFA Delete capability, which uses multi-factor authentication, can be used to provide
       an additional layer of security
          - MFA delete: requires MFA login before deleting an object

4.9 Lifecycle Management with S3 - Lab

   - use same bucket as versioning lab
      - under management, create lifecycle rule
         - lifecycle rule name "main lifecycle rule"
           - Choose scope "apply to all objects in bucket"
              Note: "Limit the scope of this rull using one or more filters where filter can be file prefix, tags, object sizes,

  - lifecycle rule action options:
     - Move current versions of objects between storage classes
     - Move noncurrent versions of objects between storage classes
     - Expire current versions of objects
     - Permanently delete noncurrent versions of objects
     - Delete expired object delete markers or incomplete multipart uploads
       These actions are not supported when filtering by object tags or object size.

  Exam tips:
     lifecycle (Policies) management:
       - automates moving your objects between the different storage tiers
       - can be use in conjunction with versioning
       - can be applied to current versions and previous versions

4.10 S3 Object Lock and Glacier Vault lock [SAA-CO2]

   S3 Object Lock:
     - use to store objects using a write once, read many (WORM) model
     - can help you prevent objects from being deleted or modified for a fixed amount of time
       or indefinitely
     - can be used to meet regulatory requirements that require WORM storage, or add an
       extra layer of protection against object changes and deletions

   S3 Object Lock Modes:
     Goverance Mode:
       - users can't overwrite or delete an object version or alter its lock settings
         unless they have special permissions.
       - you can project objects being deleted by most users, but you can still grant some users
         permissions to alter the retention settings or delete the object if necessary
     Compliance Mode:
       - a protected object version can't be overwritten or deleted by ANY user including the "root" users
       - when an object is locked in compliance mode, its retention mode can't be changed and its retention
         period can't be shortened
       - ensures an object version can't be overwritten or deleted for the duration of the retention period

     Retention Periods:
       - projects an object version for a fixed amount of time
       - S3 stores a timestamp in the object's metadata to indicate when the retention period expires
       - after retention period expires, the object version can be overwritten or deleted unless you
         also place a legal hold on the object version

     Legal Holds
       - enables you to place a legal hold on an object version
       - a legal prevents object version from being overwritten or deleted
       - a legal holde does not have an associated retention period and remains in effect
         until removed.
       - legal holds can be freely placed and removed by any user who has s3:PutObjectLegalHoldPermissions


   Glacier Vault Lock
     - allows you to deploy and enforce compliance controls for individual S3 Glacier vaults and vault lock policy
     - you can specify controls such as WORM (write once, read many) in a vault lock policy and lock the policy for
       future edits
     - once locked, the policy can no longer be changed


   Exam Tips:
    S3 Object lock:
      - use S3 Object lock to store objects using a write once, read many (WORM) model
      - objects locks can be on individual objects or applied across the bucket as a whole
      - S3 object lock come in 2 modes:
         goverance mode
           - users can't write or delete an object version or alter its lock settings unless they have special permissions
         compliance mode
           - a protected object version can't be overwritten or deleted by any user include the "root" user

     Glacier Vault Lock
       - allows you to deploy and enforce compliance controls for individual S3 Glacier vaults and vault lock policy
       - you can specify controls such as WORM (write once, read many) in a vault lock policy and lock the policy for
         future edits
       - once locked, the policy can no longer be changed

---------------------------------------------------
4.11 S3 Performance

 Organizing objects using prefixes [and delimitors]
   https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/using-prefixes.html

 Organizing objects in the S3 console using folders:
   https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/using-folders.html


  S3 Prefixes:
    Example:                                        Prefix:
     mybucketname/folder1/subfolder1/myfile.jpg     /folder1/subfolder1
     mybucketname/folder2/subfolder1/myfile.jpg     /folder2/subfolder1
     mybucketname/folder3/myfile.jpg                /folder2
     mybucketname/folder4/subfolder4/myfile.jpg     /folder4/subfolder4

  S3 Performance:
    - S3 has extremely low latency
    - you can get the first bytes out from S3 within 100 - 200 milliseconds
    - you can achieve a high number of requests: 3500 PUT/COPY/POST/DELETE and 5500 GET/HEAD requests per second per prefix

  S3 Prefix for performance:
    YOu can get bettter performance by spreading your reads across different prefixes.
    For example, if you are using 2 prefixes, you can active 11K GET/HEAD requests per second
    For example, if you are using 4 prefixes, you can active 22k GET/HEAD requests per second

  S3 Limitations when using SSE-KMS (Server Side Encryption - Key Management Service)
     - if your SSE-KMS to encrypt your objects, you keep in mind KMS Limits
     - when you upload a file, you generate a GeneratDataKey in the KMS API
     - when you download a file, you call Decrypt in the KMS API

  S3 limitations when using KMS:
     - uploading/downloading will count toward the KMS quota
     - Currently, you cannot request a quota increase for KMS
     - Region-specific quota, but it either 5500, 10k, or 30k requests per sec

  S3 Performance - multipart Upload
    - recommended for files over 100MB
    - required for files over 5GB
    - parallelize uploads (increase efficiency)
       - splits file into parts and uploads parts in parallel

  S3 Performance - downloads - S3 Byte-range fetches
    - parallelize downloads by specifying byte ranges
    - if there is a failure in the download, it's only for a specific byte range
    - can be used to speedup downloads
    - can also be used to just download partial amounts of the file (e.g. header)

  Exam Tips:
     prefix example:                                prefix
     mybucketname/folder1/subfolder1/myfile.jpg     /folder1/subfolder1

    S3 Performance
    - you can achieve a high number of requests: 3500 PUT/COPY/POST/DELETE and 5500 GET/HEAD requests per second per prefix
    - you can achieve better performance by spreading reads across different prefixes.
    - For example, if you are using 2 prefixes, you can active 11K GET/HEAD requests per second
      and 7000 PUT/COPY/POST/DELETE requests per sec

   S3 Limitations when using SSE-KMS (Server Side Encryption - Key Management Service)
     - uploading/downloading will count towards the KMS quota
     - Currently, you cannot request a quota increase for KMS
     - Region-specific quota, but it either 5500, 10k, or 30k requests per sec

   Multipart and S3 byte-range fetches (downloads)
     - use multipart uploads to increase performance when uploading a file
     - multipart upload recommended for files over 100MB
     - multipart upload required for files over 5GB
     - use S3 Byte-range fetches to increase performance when downloading files from S3

4.12 S3 Select and Glacier Select

   S3 Select:
     - enables applications to retrieve only a subset of data from an object by using simple SQL expressions
     - By using S3 Select to retrieve only the data needed by your application, you can
       achieve drastic performance increases - in many cases, you can get as much as a 400% improvement

   S3 Select Example:
     - assume all your data is stored in S3 in zip files that contain CSV files
     - without S3 Select, you would to download, decompress, and process the entier CDS to get the data you need


   Glacier Select:
    - some companies write data directly to Amazon Glacier to satisfy compliance needs like SEC Rule 17a or HIPAA
    - many S3 users have lifecycle policies designed to save on storage costs, by moving their data into Glacier
      when they no longer need to access it on a regular basis
    - Glacier select allows you to run SQL queries against Glacier directly

   Exam tips:
     - S3 Select enables applications to retrieve only a subset of data from an object by using simple SQL expressions
       - get data by rows or columns using simple SQL expressions
       - save money on data transfers and increase speeds


    S3 Select limitations
      - you can only perform a query on one object at a time
      - only supports the "SELECT" clause in SQL (no joins, no groupings, and no other sophisticated SQL operations

4.13 AWS Organizations and Consolidated Billing

   AWS Organizations:
     - an account management service that enables you to consolidate multiple AWS accounts into an organization
       that you create and centrally manage
       - policy can be applied to each Organizaion Unit, and inherited by sub organization units
       - example policy for OU could limit/restrict AWS services that they can use

   Consolidated Billing
     - all your accounts are linked for billing purposes
     - Since the more you use an AWS service, the lower rate your pay for that service
     - consolidate billing lets you take the aggregate of all your accounts
     - paying account is independent. Cannot access resources of the other accounts
     - Linked accounts - all are independent

   Advantages of Consolidated Billing
     - one bill per AWS account
     - very easy to track charges and allocate costs
     - volume pricing discount


   Lab
      - requires 2 AWS accounts
      - go to AWS Organizations in 1st account
         - Create an Account (root) account
         - Add an AWS account
            - need to include account name, account owner email, ...
            - email nees to be verified
      - go to AWS Organizations in 2nd account
         - accept invite
      - go to AWS Organizations in 1st account
         - now you will see 2 organizations
         - can apply policies to each organization to restrict AWS services used by the OU

   Exam Tips:
     Some best practices with AWS Organizations
       - always enable MFA on root account
       - always use a strong complex password on root account
       - paying account should be used for billing purposes only. Do not deply resources into the paying account
       - Enable/Disable AWS services using Service Control Policies (SCP) either on OU or on individual accounts

    S3 Cross Account Access
       - 3 different ways to share S3 buckets across accounts

           - Using Bucket ACLs & IAM (individual Objects)
             Programmatic Access Only

     -> in 2nd Account, go to IAM -> Roles

             - add AWS Management Console access, custom password, add user to group Admin -> Admin access policy

              - now there is a switch role button at top left side
                 -> select switch role and paste "role link from other account"
                 -> now you are in the account with just S3 permissions

           - Using Bucket ACLs & IAM (individual Objects) -  Programmatic Access Only
           - Cross-account IAM roles. Programming and Console Access

https://docs.aws.amazon.com/general/latest/gr/rande.html#s3_region

AWS S3 Replication Configuration
https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/replication-add-config.html

Amazon S3 Replication adds support for replicating delete markers
https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2020/11/amazon-s3-replication-adds-support-for-replicating-delete-markers/


 -> go to S3 -> create a new bucket in a different region (Tokyo)
    - unblock public access -> enable ACLs -> leave versioning disabled
    -> create bucket

  -> select your original versioning bucket (s3versiondemobucket)
     -> management -> Replication Rules
     -> IAM Role -> Create a new role
     -> source bucket -> apply to all objects in the bucket
     -> destination bucket -> select tokyo bucket
         -> reports "Replication requires versioning to be enabled
         -> enable bucket versioning
     -> destination Storage Class
       -> "change the storage class for the replicated objects
       -> select "Standard-IA"
       -> Save (replication rule)
  -> it queries:
       Replicate existing objects?
             select "no, do not replicate existing objects"
              other option: "Yes, replicate existing objects"


  Note: previously, only new objects were replicated. Now, it gives
        you are choice.
    -> Examine Tokyo bucket, it is empty

    -> in original versioning bucket, upload 4th version of "versiondemo.txt"
       -> Make public

   -> examine Tokyo bucket to verify "4th version" doc
      show up (make take up to 15 min)
      -> public permission changes in source bucket, does not make
         it public in destination bucket

   -> Example use case:
      - replicate all AWS service logs from production account to audit account
        Note: production will not be able to delete audit account logs


  Exam Tips
    Cross Region Replication
      - version must be enabled on both the source and destination buckets
      - by default, files in existing in source bucket are not replicated
        automatically (now, in console when creating replicate rule, it
        queries you to see if want to replicate exiting files)
      - All subsequent updated files will be replicated automatically
      - by default, delete markers are not replicate
          - Now, it gives you an option to replicate delete markers when
            create replication rule
      - deleting individual versions or delete markers will not be replicated
      - understand what Cross Region Replication is at a high level

AWS S3 Transfer acceleration
https://s3-accelerate-speedtest.s3-accelerate.amazonaws.com/en/accelerate-speed-comparsion.html


S3 Transfer Acceleration:
   - utilizes the clouldFront Edge Network to accelerate you uploads to S3
   - instead of uploading directly to your S3 bucket, your users can use a distinct URL to upload directly
     to an edge location which will then transfer that file to S3
   - You will get a distinct URL to upload to, e.g.:
         acloudguru.s3-accelerate.amazonaws.com

    - Users upload to edge location closest to them, then it transferred to the S3 Bucket
    - AWS has a tool to test it out
    https://s3-accelerate-speedtest.s3-accelerate.amazonaws.com/en/accelerate-speed-comparsion.html

    -> takes 5 or 6 min to compare S3 bucket uploads in different regions from your location

     S3 Transfer acceleration
      - instead of uploading directly to your S3 bucket, your users can use a distinct URL to
        upload directly to an edge location which will then transfer that file to S3

4.17 AWS DataSync


     - Allows you to move a large amount of data to AWS

     AWS DataSync Agent
     - Install DataSync agent on a server that connects to your NAS (Network Attached Storage or Server) or the file system
       to copy data to AWS and and write data from AWS

     Network Transfers:
       - DataSync automatically encrypts data and accelerates transfers over the WAN .
       - DataSync performs automatic data integrity checks in-transit and at rest.

     AWS DataSync
       - DataSync seamless and securely connects to AWS S3, AWS EFS, or AWS FSx for Windows File Server to copy
         data and metadatay to and from AWS

    Exam Tips:
      AWS DataSync
        - Used to move large amounts of data from on-premise to AWS
        - Used with NFS and SMB-compatible file systems
        - Replication can be done, hourly, daily, weekly
        - Install the DataSync agent to start the replication

     AWS DataSync EFS to EFS:
       - can be used to replicate EFS to EFS
       - For this, DataSync sync agent would be installed on EC2 instance

4.18 CloudFront

AWS CloudFront Key Features
https://aws.amazon.com/cloudfront/features/?whats-new-cloudfront.sort-by=item.additionalFields.postDateTime&whats-new-cloudfront.sort-order=desc

   What is CloudFront:
     - a content delivery network (CDN) is a system of distributed servers (networks) that
       deliver webpages and other web content to a user based on the geographic locations
       of the users, the origin of the webpage, and a content delivery server

   Edge Location
     - location where content will be cached
     - it will be cached for the TTL (Time to Live)
   Origin
      - the origin of all the files that the CDN will distribute.
      - this can be an S3 bucket, an EC2 Instance, and Elastic Load Balancer, Route53, HTTP server,
        AWS Elemental MediaPackage endpoint, AWW Elemental Media Store container
   Distribution:
     - name given the CDN which consists of a collection of Edge Locations

    CloudFront
      - can be used to deliver your entire website, including dynamic, static, steaming, and
        interactive content using a global network of edge locations
      - requests for your content are automatically routed to the nearest edge location, so
        content is delivered with the best possible performance

     CloudFront distribution types:
       - Web Distribution - typically used from Websites
       - RTMP (Real-Time Messaging Protocol) - used for media streaming


   Exam Tips:
      Edge Location:
         - the location where content will be cached. This is seperate from AWS Regions/AZs
         - edge location are not just read only - you can write to them too (place an object on them).
         - objects are cached for the life of the TTL (time to live)
         - you can clear cached objects (invalidate cache), but you will be charged
      Origin
        - the origin of all the files that the CDN will distribute.
        - this can be an S3 bucket, an EC2 Instance, and Elastic Load Balancer, Route53, HTTP server,
          AWS Elemental MediaPackage endpoint, AWW Elemental Media Store container
     Distribution:
       - name given the CDN which consists of a collection of Edge Locations
     CloudFront distribution types:
       - Web Distribution - typically used from Websites
       - RTMP (Real-Time Messaging Protocol) - used for media streaming

        - location where content is cached
        - not just read only - you can write to them too (put an object on to them)
        - objects are cached for the life of the TTL (time to live)
        - you can invalidate cached objects, but you will be charged

      Origin
        - the origin of all the files that the CDN will distribute.
        - this can be an S3 bucket, an EC2 Instance, and Elastic Load Balancer, Route53, HTTP server,
          AWS Elemental MediaPackage endpoint, AWW Elemental Media Store container
     Distribution:
       - name given the CDN which consists of a collection of Edge Locations
     CloudFront distribution types:
       - Web Distribution - typically used from Websites
       - RTMP (Real-Time Messaging Protocol) - used for media streaming (no longer supported)

4.20 CloudFront Signed URLs and Cookies

Cloud Front Signed URL or S3 signed URL or cookie?
- How to restrict content access?

   Use cloudfront signed URLs or signed cookies
     - a signed URL is for individual files. 1 file = 1 URL (Uniform Resource Locators)
     - A signed cookie is for multiple files. 1 cookie = multiple files

   Signed URL Policies
       - when we create a signed URL or signed cookie, we attach a policy
          - the policy can include:
             - URL expiration
             - IP ranges (limit to your end customer IP address range)
             - trusted signers (which AWS accounts can create signed URLs)

     How signed URLs work:
          cloudFront/edge location <- OAI (Origin Access Identity) -> S3

          client <-> application <- use SDK to generate signed URL -> cloudfront/edge

                 Client login to application:
                 -> login returns Signed URL to client
                 -> application uses SDK to generate signed URL to cloudfront

       CloudFront Signed URLs
          - can have different origins (does not have to be EC2)
          - key-part is account wide and managed by root user
          - can utilize cache features
          - can filter by date, path, IP address, expiration, etc

       S3 Signed URL
         - user have direct access to S3 (not using cloudfront)
         - issues request as the IAM user who creates the presigned URL
            - have same permissions has the IAM user
         - limited lifetype

   Exam tips:
     - use signed URLs/cookies when you want to secure content so that only the people you
       authorize are able to access it

     Use cloudfront signed URLs or signed cookies
       - a signed URL is for individual files. 1 file = 1 URL (Uniform Resource Locators)
       - A signed cookie is for multiple files. 1 cookie = multiple files

      Origin:
         - if your origin is EC2, then use CloudFront
         - if your origin is S3 and have a single file, then you want use an S3 signed URL


---------------------------------------------------
4.21 Snowball [snowball edge, snowmobile]

AWS Snowball Quotas
https://docs.aws.amazon.com/snowball/latest/ug/limits.html

  What is snowball:
    - a big secure disk
    - petatype-scale data transport solution that uses secure appliances to transfer large
      amounts of data into and out of AWS
    - address common challenges with large scale data transfers including high network costs
      long transfer times, and security concerns
    - transferring data with Snowball is simple, fast, secure, and can be as little as one-fifth
      cost of high-speed internet

    - either 50TB or 80TB in US regions or 80TB in non-US regions
    - uses multiple layers security designed to project your data including tamper-resistant enclosures,
      256-bit encryption, and industry standard Trusted Platform Module (TPM) design to encure
      both security and full chain-of-custody of your data.
    - once the data transfer job has been processed and verified, AWS performs a software
      erasure of the snowball appliance

    - supports 10Gb/s Ethernet (RJ45), 25 Gb/s SFP (small form factor pluggable) connection, and
      SFP+Optical connection

  What is snowball edge:
    - is 100TB data tranfer device with on-board storage and compute capabilities.
    - you can use Snowball edge to move large amounts of data into and out of AWS, as a temporary
      storage tier for large local datasets, or to support local workloads in remote of offline
      locations
    - can run lambda functions from snowball edge
    - example: An Airline use snowball edge for testing of its aircraft

    - connects to your existing applications and infrastructure using standard storage interfaces,
      streamlining the data transfer process and minimimizing setup and integration
    - can cluster together to form a local storage tier and process your data on-premises, helping
      ensure your applications continue to run even when they are not able to access the cloud

    - supports 10Gb/s Ethernet (RJ45), 25 Gb/s SFP (small form factor pluggable) connection, and
      40 Gb/s QSFP (quad small form factor pluggable) connection

  What is snowmobile:
    - exabyte-scale data transfer service used to extremely large amounts of data to AWS
    - you can transfer up to 100PB per snowmobile
    - it is a 45-foot long ruggedized shipping container, pulled by a semi-trailer truck
    - makes it easy to move massive volumes of data to the cloud, including video libraries,
      image repositories, or even a complete data center migration.
    - transferring data with snowmobile is secure, fast, and cost effective

   What is Snowcone
     - 8TB of storage
     - small weighing 4.5 lbs (2.1kg)
     - ruggized, secure, and purpose-built for use outside of a traditional data center
     - small form factore makes it a perfect fit for tit spaces or where portability is
       necessity and network connectivity is unreliable

    Internet connection         Min Days to transfer 100TB at 80%       when to consider
                                     utilization                           Import/Export Snowball
        T3 (45Mbps)                  269 days                              2TB or more
        100 Mbps                     120 days                              5TB or more
       1000 Mpbs                      12 days                             60TB or more

    Exam Tips
      - understand what is snowball
         - snowball can import to S3 and export from S3

4.22 Snowball demo

4.23 Storage Gateway

   what is storage gateway:
     - a service that connects an on-premises software appliance with cloud-based storage to
       provide seamless and secure integration between an organization's on-premise IT environment
       and AWS storage infrastructure.
     - enables you to securely store data to the AWS cloud for scalable and cost-effective storage

     - original a Virtual Gateway, but now there is a physical gateway option
     - software appliance is available for download as a virtual machine image that you install
       on a host in your datacenter.
     - support VMware ESXi or Microsoft Hyper-V as the hypervisor (what you run VM on)
     - once you've installed your gateway and associated it with your AWS account through the
       activiation process, you can use the AWS management console to create the storage
       gateway option that is right for you.


   Storage Gateway types:
     - File Gateway (NFS & SMB [server message block]) - for sharing files
       Note: Microsoft renamed SMB to Common Internet File System (CIFS) so sometimes refer to as SMB/CIFS
     - Volume Gateway (iSCSI) - for sharing volumes (copies of your hard disk drives)
        - stored volumes
        - cached volumes
     - Tape Gateway (Virtual Tape Library)


    File Gateway:
      - files are stored as objects in your S3 buckets, accessed through a Network File System (NFS)
        mount point.
      - Ownership, permissions, and timestampls are durably store in S3 in the user-metadata of the
        object associated with the file
      - Once the objects are transferred to S3, they can be managed as native S3 objects, and
        bucket policies such as versioning, lifecycles management, and cross-region replication
        apply directly to the objects stored in your bucket

    Volume Gateway:
      - a way of storing virtual hard disk drive in AWS
      - presents your applications with disk volumes using iSCSI block protocol
      - Data written to these volumes can be asynchronously backed up as point-in-time
        snapshots of your volume, and stored in the cloud as AWS EBS snapshots
      - Snapshots are incremental backups that capture only changed blocks
      - All snapshots storage is also compressed to minimize your storage charges

    Volume Gateway - Stored Volumes:
      - lets you store your primary data locally, while asynchronously backing up that data to AWS
      - stored volumes provide your on-premises applications with low-latency access to their
        entire datasets, while providing durable, off-site backups
      - you can create storage volumes and mount as iSCSI devices from your on-premise
        application servers
      - data written to your stored volumes is stored on your on-premises storate hardware
      - this data is asynchronously backed up to AWS S3 in the form of AWS EBS snapshots
      - 1 GB to 16 TB in size for stored volumes

    Volume Gateway - Cached Volumes:
      - lets you uses S3 as your primary data storage while retaining frequently accessed data
        locally in your storage gateway
        - cached volumes minimize the need to scal your on-premise storage infrastructure,
        while still providing your applications with low-latency access to their frequently
        accessed data.
        - you can create store volumes up to 32 TB in size and attach to them as iSCSI devices
        that your write to these volumes in S3 an retain recently read data in your on-premises
        storage gateway's cached and upload buffer storage
        - 1 GB - 32 TB in cached volumes

     Tape Gateway
       - a way of getting rid of tape backups
       - durable, cost-effective solution to archive your data in AWS cloud
       - the VTL (Virtual Tape Library) iSCSI interface it provides lets you leverage your
         existing tape-based backup application infrastructure to store data on Virtual Tape
         cartridges that you create on your tape gateway
       - each tape gateway is preconfigured with a media changer and tape drives,
         which are available to your existing client backup applications as iSCSI devices
       - you add tape cartridges as you need to archive your data
       - supprt by NetBackup, Backup Exec, Veeam, etc.

   Exam Tips:

   Storage Gateway types:
     File Gateway
       - for flat files, store directly on S3
     volume gateway
        Stored Volumes - Entire dataset is stored on site and is asynchronously backup to S3
        Cached Volumes - Entire dataset is stored on S3 and most frequently accessed data is cached on site
     Gateway Virtual Tape Library (tape gateway)
        tape backup is replaced with AWS S3 backups


     Originally only a Virtual Appliance, but now also has a hardware appliance option

---------------------------------------------------
4.24 Athena vs Macie

   Athena:
     - interactive query service which enable you to analyze and query data located in S3
       uinsg standard SQL (Structured Query Language)
         - serverless, nothing to provision, pay per query / per TB scanned
         - no need to set up complex Extract/Transform/Load (ETL) processes
         - works directly with data stored in S3
         - essentially, allows you to turn S3 into a database

   Athea Uses Cases:
     - used to query log files stored in S3 (e.g. Elastic Load Balancer [ELB] logs, S3 access logs, etc.
     - Generate business reports on data stored in S3
     - Analyze AWS cost and usage reports
     - Run queries on click-stream data (user click data on your website)
     - can perform SQL against any number of objects, or even entire bucket paths. Its not uncommon for folks
       to perform queries on TERABYTES of data.

   Before What is Macie:
     What is PII (Personally Identifiale Information):
        - personal data used to establish individual's identity
        - home address, email address, SSN
        - Passport number, driver license number
        - D.O.B, phone number, bank account, credit card number

   What is Macie:
     - Security service which uses Machine Learning and NLP (Natural Language Processing) to
       to discover, classify, and protect sensitive data stored in S3
       - Uses AI to recognize if your S3 objects contain sensitive data such as PII
       - Dashboards, reporting, and alerts
       - Works directly with data stored in S3
       - can also analyze CloudTrail logs
       - Great for PCI-DSS (Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard) and preventing ID theft

  Athena Exam Tips:
    - Athena is an interactive query service
    - It allows you to query data located in S3 using standard SQL (Structured Query Language)
    - Serverless
    - Commonly used to analyze log data stored in S3
    - can perform SQL against any number of objects, or even entire bucket paths. Its not uncommon for folks
      to perform queries on TERABYTES of data.

  Macie Exam Tips
    - uses AI to analyze data in S3 and helps identify PII (Personally Identifiable Information)
    - Can also be used to analyse CloudTrail logs for suspicious API activity
    - includes Dashboards, Reports, and Alerting
    - Great for PCI-DSS (Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard) and preventing ID theft

---------------------------------------------------
4.25 S3 and IAM Summary

   Identity Access Management (IAM)
     - consists of the following:
       - users
       - groups
          - policies applied to group are inherited by users in the group
       - roles
       - policies
          - you apply policies to users, groups, and roles to give or restrict access
          - policies use json, e.g. administratorAccess policy: (allows any access):
                {
                    "Version": "2012-10-17",
                    "Statement": [
                        {
                            "Effect": "Allow",
                            "Action": "*",
                            "Resource": "*"
                        }
                    ]
                }

     - IAM is univeral (apply to all regions)
     - the "root account" is simply the account created when your first setup your AWS account
        - it has complete Admin access
        - always setup Multifactor Authentication (MFA) on root account
     - New Users have NO Permissioins when first created
        - Least privilege is a common AWS theme
     - New User are [optionally] assigned an Access Key ID & Secret Access Keys when 1st created
       - these keys are not the same as passwords.
       - cannot be used to login to the console
       - used to access AWS via the API and Command Line Interface (CLI)
       - you only get to view these keys once (need to download them to a secure location).
       - if you lose these keys, you have to regenerated them
     - You can create and customize your own password rotation policies

   S3:
     - S3 is Object-based (allows you to upload files)
     - not suitable to install an operating system on or database
     - files (objects) can be from 0 to 5 TB
     - there is unlimited storage
     - files are stored in buckets
     - S3 is univeral namespace - names must be unique globally - buckets must be uniquely named
     - example URL:
        https://<bucketName>.s3.amazonaws.com (in N. Virginia)

        https://s3-<Region>.amazonaws.com/<bucketName> (all other regions)
        https://s3-eu-west-1.amazonaws.com/acloudguru

        https://<bucketName>.s3-<Region>.amazonaws.com/
        https://acloudguru.s3-eu-west-1.amazonaws.com/
     - Successful uploads with generate a HTTP 200 status code
     - By default, all newly created buckets are PRIVATE.
     - You can setup Access Control to your bucket using:
        - Bucket policies (bucket wide - but can limit access to folders in a bucket )
        - Access Control Lists (ACL) (can be on individual objects)
     - S3 buckets can be configured to create access logs which log all requests made to the S3 bucket
        - access logs can be sent to another bucket or even another bucket in another account

   S3 Key Fundamentals
       - Key (name of the object)
       - value (data/contents of the object)
       - Version ID (important for versioning)
       - metadata
       - subresources
         - Access Control Lists
         - torrents
            - a torrent file is a metadata file type that primarily stores the URLs of the host
              server that manages the communicatioin, data transferring, and other protocol
              specific admin in the file-sharing downloading process.
       - Read after Write Consistentcy for PUTS of new objects
       - Eventual Consistency for overwrites PUTS and DELETES (can take time to propagate)

   S3 Storage classes
      https://aws.amazon.com/s3/storage-classes/
       - S3 Standard
         - 99.99% availability
         - 99.999999999% durability (11 x 9s)
         - designed to sustain the lost of 2 facilities concurrently
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 Standard IA (infrequently accessed)
         - for data accessed less frequently, but requires rapid access when needed
         - lower fee than S3, but charged a retrieval fee
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 One Zone IA (infrequently accessed)
         - lower cost option for infrequently accessed data,
           but do not require multiple availability & data resilience
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 Intelligent Tiering
         - uses machine learning to optimize your storage costs
         - frequent, infrequent, & Archive Instant Access tiers with Deep Archive option
         - optimize costs by automatically moving data to the most cost-effective access tier
         - 1st byte latency: millisec
       - S3 Glacier Instant retrevial
         - secure, durable, and lower cost storage classes for data archiving
         - 99.999999999% durability (11 x 9s)
         - retrieval time in milliseconds
         - 128 KB min object size
       - S3 Glacier Flexible retrevial (formally S3 Glacier)
         - secure, durable, and lower cost storage classes for data archiving
         - 99.999999999% durability (11 x 9s)
         - retrieval times configurable from minutes to hours
       - S3 Glacier Deep Archive
         - lowest cost storage class
         - 99.999999999% durability (11 x 9s)
         - retrieval time of 12 hours is acceptable

       S3 RRS (Reduced Redundancy Storage) - phased out
          - similar to S3 One Zone IA

   S3 Encryption
     - encryption In Transit is achieved by:
        - SSL/TLS (via https) (Secure Socker Layer / Transport Layer Security)
     - Encryption At Rest (server side) is achieved by:
         - S3 Managed Keys - SSE-S3 (server side encryption S3)
         - AWS Key Management Service
           - Managed Keys - SSE-KMS (Server Side Encryption - Key Management Service)
           - User and AWS manage keys together
         - Server Side Encryption with Customer Provided Keys
            - (Server Side Encryption - Customer)
     - Encryption At Rest (client side) is achieved by:
         - user encrypts file and then uploads encrypted file

   AWS Organizations
      - Organizations best practices
        - always enable multi-factor authentication on root account
        - always use strong and complex password on root account
        - paying account  should be used for billing purposes only - do not
          deploy resources in paying account
        - Enable/disable AWS services using service control policies (SCP) on
          either OU (Organization Units) or on individual accounts

    3 ways to Share S3 buckets across accounts
       - Using Bucket Policies & IAM (applies across the entire Bucket) -  Programmatic Access Only
       - Using Bucket ACLs & IAM (individual Objects) -  Programmatic Access Only
       - Cross-account IAM roles. Programming and Console Access

    Cross Region Replication
      - replication of bucket data across region (bucket in one region to bucket in 2nd region)
      - version must be enabled on both the source and destination buckets
      - by default, files in existing in source bucket are not replicated
        automatically (now, in console when creating replicate rule, it
        queries you to see if want to replicate exiting files)
      - All subsequent updated files will be replicated automatically
      - by default, delete markers are not replicate
          - Now, it gives you an option to replicate delete markers when
            create replication rule
      - deleting individual versions or delete markers will not be replicated
      - understand what Cross Region Replication is at a high level

    Lifecycle Policies (management):
      - automates moving your objects between the different storage tiers
      - can be used in conjunction with versioning
      - can be applied to current versions and previous versions

    S3 Transfer Acceleration
      - instead of uploading directly to your S3 bucket, your users can use a distinct URL
        to upload directly to an edge location which will then transfer that file to S3

    CloudFront
      Edge Location:
         - the location where content will be cached. This is seperate from AWS Regions/AZs
         - edge location are not just read only - you can write to them too (place an object on them).
         - objects are cached for the life of the TTL (time to live)
         - you can clear cached objects (invalidate cache), but you will be charged
      Origin
         - the origin of all the files that the CDN will distribute.
         - this can be an S3 bucket, an EC2 Instance, and Elastic Load Balancer, Route53, HTTP server,
           AWS Elemental MediaPackage endpoint, AWW Elemental Media Store container
      Distribution:
        - name given the CDN which consists of a collection of Edge Locations
      CloudFront distribution types:
        - Web Distribution - typically used from Websites
        - RTMP (Real-Time Messaging Protocol) - used for media streaming (no longer supported)

    Snowball
    - a big secure disk
    - used to transfer/import large amounts of data into S3 and export out from S3 AWS
    - either 50TB or 80TB in US regions or 80TB in non-US regions

   Storage Gateway types:
     File Gateway
       - for flat files, store directly on S3 - NFS
     volume gateway
        Stored Volumes - Entire dataset is stored on site and is asynchronously backup to S3
        Cached Volumes - Entire dataset is stored on S3 and most frequently accessed data is cached on site
     Gateway Virtual Tape Library (tape gateway)
        - used for backup and uses popular backup applications like NetBackup, Backup Exec, Veeam, etc.
        - tape backup is replaced with AWS S3 backups

   Athena:
    - Athena is an interactive query service
    - It allows you to query data located in S3 using standard SQL (Structured Query Language)
    - Serverless
    - Commonly used to analyze log data stored in S3

   Macie:
    - uses AI to analyze data in S3 and helps identify PII (Personally Identifiable Information)
    - Can also be used to analyse CloudTrail logs for suspicious API activity
    - includes Dashboards, Reports, and Alerting
    - Great for PCI-DSS (Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard) and preventing ID theft

    S3 - Read the S3 FAQs before taking the exam
         e.g.; https://aws.amazon.com/s3/faqs/
