4.26 Create Static Website using Amazon S3


-------
Additional Resources:

Note: When creating the bucket, uncheck all four checkboxes on step 3 - Set Permissions. If you skip this step,
you will not be allowed to create the bucket policy later. If you made a mistake and created the bucket without
unchecking the checkboxes, you may go to Bucket > Permissions > Public access settings > Edit, and uncheck all
four restrictions. Then add a bucket policy, go to Bucket > Permissions > Bucket Policy, and add the
following JSON statement (replacing <MY_BUCKET> with your bucket name):
{
    "Version": "2012-10-17",
    "Id": "Policy1645724938586",
    "Statement": [
        {
            "Sid": "Stmt1645724933619",
            "Effect": "Allow",
            "Principal": "*",
            "Action": "s3:GetObject",
            "Resource": "<BUCKET_ARN>/*"
        }
    ]
}

>**Note:** Ensure the trailing `/*` is present so the policy applies to all objects within the bucket.

    Click Save changes.

Ensure the trailing /* is present so the policy applies to all objects within the bucket.

-------
Learning Objectives

Optional: Run progress checks to confirm you've completed the objectives
1. Create S3 Bucket

Create an S3 bucket that begins with the name my-bucket (e.g., my-bucket-<ACCOUNT ID>) in the us-east-1 region. When creating the bucket, remember to uncheck the S3 Block Public Access settings. Upload the code for the static site. You will find the code (index.html and error.html) in the following GitHub repository: https://github.com/ACloudGuru-Resources/Course-Certified-Solutions-Architect-Associate/tree/master/labs/creating-a-static-website-using-amazon-s3
Enable Static Website Hosting

You can use Amazon S3 to host a static website. On a static website, individual web pages include s"tatic content. They might also contain client-side scripts.

To host a static website on Amazon S3, configure an Amazon S3 bucket for website hosting, and then upload your website content to the bucket.

When you configure a bucket as a static website, you must enable website hosting, set permissions, and create and add an index document.

You must ensure that the S3 Block Public Access settings are disabled on the bucket, so that the files in the bucket can be made publicly readable.


   -> S3 -> create bucket -> name: my-bucket-pathall
    -> ACLs enabled, unselect "Block all public access -> Create Bucket
    -> select bucket -> copy ARN  arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket-pathall
        -> upload "error.html" & "index.html"
        -> Properties  -> Static Website Hosting -> enable
                       -> Index Document "index.html"
                       -> Error Document "error.html" -> save changes

             -> Static Website hosting now has Bucket Website endpoint
                -> click, but 403 error

        -> go to permissions tab -> edit "bucket policy"
           -> added provided bucket policy but replace <BUCKET> with ARM:
          {
    "Version": "2012-10-17",
    "Id": "Policy1645724938586",
    "Statement": [
        {
            "Sid": "Stmt1645724933619",
            "Effect": "Allow",
            "Principal": "*",
            "Action": "s3:GetObject",
            "Resource": "arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket-pathall/*"
        }
    ]
}

  -> after applying bucket policy, the web site is visible


2. Apply Bucket Policy

To make objects in your bucket publicly readable, write a bucket policy that grants everyone s3:GetObject permission.

Note that AWS will not permit you to create the policy if the S3 Block Public Access settings are still enabled on the bucket.

After disabling the Block Public Access settings, you can add a bucket policy to grant public read access to your bucket. When you grant public read access, anyone on the internet can access your bucket.


   -> S3 -> create bucket -> name: my-bucket-pathall
    -> ACLs enabled, unselect "Block all public access -> Create Bucket
    -> select bucket -> copy ARN  arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket-pathall
        -> upload "error.html" & "index.html"
        -> Properties  -> Static Website Hosting -> enable
                       -> Index Document "index.html"
                       -> Error Document "error.html" -> save changes

             -> Static Website hosting now has Bucket Website endpoint
                -> click, but 403 error

        -> go to permissions tab -> edit "bucket policy"
           -> added provided bucket policy but replace <BUCKET> with ARM:
          {
    "Version": "2012-10-17",
    "Id": "Policy1645724938586",
    "Statement": [
        {
            "Sid": "Stmt1645724933619",
            "Effect": "Allow",
            "Principal": "*",
            "Action": "s3:GetObject",
            "Resource": "arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket-pathall/*"
        }
    ]
}

  -> after applying bucket policy, the web site is visible

    user-3 should be in the EC2-Admin group.

2. Use the IAM Sign-In Link to Sign In as a User

Copy the IAM user sign-in link in the AWS console, open an incognito window, and sign in as user-1, user-2, and user-3 with the passwords provided on the lab page.

Ensure you are using the N. Virginia (us-east-1) Region.

    user-1 password: 3Kk6!AY36^5h1rolJYb@C
    user-2 password: 3Kk6!AY36^5h1rolJYb@C
    user-3 password: 3Kk6!AY36^5h1rolJYb@C


 -> go to IAM ->
    -> examine "user-1" -> no permissions / no groups
    -> has an "ARN" (Amazon Resource Name) -> can be used for login
    -> examine Groups -> 3 pre-created groups
       -> "EC2-Admin" Group has
           -> "Customer inline" policy (customer created policy)
           -> can attach a "Managed Policy" (standard AWS pre-created policies) -> Add Permissions -> attach policies
       -> "EC2-Support" Group has
          -> has managed policy "AmazonEc2ReadOnlyAccess"
       -> "S3-Support" Group has
          -> has managed policy "AmazonS3ReadOnlyAccess"

     -> add users to each group
        -> S3-support  -> add user "user-1"
        -> EC2-support -> add user "user-2"
        -> EC2-Admin   -> add user "user-3"
          -> note: you can modify the Group inherited policy to be more restrictive for the specific user


   -> in separate private window
      -> login as user-1
          -> create bucket -> fails (due to permissions)
          -> go ec2 -> many api errors to permissions
      -> login as user-2
          -> go ec2 -> no api errors  -> stop running instance -> fails due to permissions
      -> login as user-3
          -> go ec2 -> no api errors  -> stop running instance -> stops instance

4.28 AWS Certified Solution Architect Identity Access Management & S3 Exam Questions

1. What statement best decribes IAM
IAM allows you to manage users, groups, roles, and their corresponding level of access to the AWS Platform.

2. What is an additional way to secure the AWS accounts of both the root account and new users alike?
ans: Implement Multi-Factor Authentication for all accounts.

3. A new employee has just started work, and it is your job to give her administrator access to the AWS console. You have given her a user name, an access key ID, a secret access key, and you have generated a password for her. She is now able to log in to the AWS console, but she is unable to interact with any AWS services. What should you do next?
ans: Grant her Administrator access by adding her to an Administrators' group.

4. ...
You cannot log in to the AWS console using the Access Key ID / Secret Access Key pair. Instead, you must generate a password for the user, and supply the user with this password and your organization's unique AWS console login URL.


5. Which of the following is not a component of IAM?
-> includes Users, roles, & groups, but not Organization Units

6. Power User acccess:

info: A Power User Access provides Administrator Access without the capability to manage the users and permissions. In other words, a user with Power User Access can create, delete, modify or see the resources, but he cannot grant permissions to other users.

ans: Access to all AWS services except the management of groups and users within IAM.

10. What is the default level of access a newly created IAM User is granted?

ans: No access to any AWS services.

12. What level of access does the "root" account have?
ans: Administrator Access

13.  Your company has launched a new app. To access the app files, the development team needs access to a bucket that is located within your team's AWS account. The development team is using a different account and requires programmatic and console level access to your team's S3 bucket. How would you share this bucket with the development team's account?

ans; Setting up a cross account IAM Role

14. You are a solutions architect working for a large engineering company that are moving from a legacy infrastructure to AWS. You have configured the company's first AWS account and you have set up IAM. Your company is based in Andorra, but there will be a small subsidiary operating out of South Korea, so that office will need its own AWS environment. Which of the following statements is true?

ans: You will need to configure Users and Policy Documents only once, as these are applied globally.

18: A __ is an object in AWS stored as a JSON document that provides a formal statement of one or more permissions.
ans: Policy

Chapter 5 Elastic Compute Cloud (EC2)
---------------------------------------------------
5.1 EC2 Overview

  Introducting EC2
    - VM hosted in AWS
    - only pay for what you use
    - the capacity you want when you need it
    - provision instance in minu

  On-premise infrastructure
    - long-term investments, 3 - 5 years

  EC2 pricing options
    on-demand instances
      - pay by the hour or second depending on the type of instance you run
    reserved instances
      - reserve capacity for 1 to 3 years
      - up to 72% discount on the hourly charge
      - the long the contract and the more you pay up front, the greater the discount
    spot instances
      - purchase unused capacity at a discount of up to 90%
      - prices fluctuate with supply and demand
    dedicated hosts
      - a physical EC2 server dedicated for your use
      - the most expensive option


  EC2 Use Cases
    on-demand instances
      - Flexible: low cost and flexible
      - short term: applications with short term, spikey, or unpredictable workloads that
        cannot be interrupted
      - Testing the water:
    reserved instances  (RI)
      - Predictable Usages: applications with steady state or predictable usage
      - Specific Capacity Requirements
      - Pay up front - pay upfront to reduce the total computing costs even further
      - reserved instances operate at a regional level
      - Standard RIs:
         - up to 72% off the on-demand prices
      - Convertible RI:
         - up to 54% off the on-demand prices.
         - Has option to change to a different RI type of equal or greater value
      - Scheduled RIs:
         - launched with the time window you define
         - match your capacity reservation to predictable recurring schedule that only requires a
           fraction of a day, week, or month
    spot instances
      - Flexible: applications that have flexible start and end times
      - Cost Sentive: applications that are only feasible at very low compute prices
      - Urgent Capacity: users with an urgent need for large amounts of additional computing capacity
      - examples: image rendering, genomic sequencing, algorithmic trading engines
    dedicated hosts
      - Compliance
        - regulatory requirements that may not support multi-tenant virtualization
      - Licensing
        - licensing that does not support multi-tenancy or cloud deployments
      - on-demand
         - dedicated hosts can be purchased on-demand
      - reserved
         - can be purchased as a reservation for up to 70% off the on-demand price

   AWS Calculator
      https://calculator.aws
        - use to estimate AWS cost

  Exam Tips

    EC2 Overview
      - EC2 is like a VM, hosted in AWS instead of your own data center
      - select the capacity your need right now
      - grow and shrink when you need
      - pay for what you use
      - to provision, wait for minutes, not months to access

    EC2 pricing options
      on-demand instances
        - pay by the hour or second depending on the type of instance you run
        - great for flexiblity
      reserved instances
        - reserve capacity for 1 to 3 years
        - up to 72% discount on the hourly charge
        - the long the contract and the more you pay up front, the greater the discount
        - great if you have known, fixed requirements
      spot instances
        - purchase unused capacity at a discount of up to 90%
        - prices fluctuate with supply and demand
        - great for applications with flexible start and end times
        - If EC2 interrupts your Spot Instance in the first hour, before a full hour of running time,
          you're not charged for the partial hour used.
      dedicated hosts
        - a physical EC2 server dedicated for your use
        - the most expensive option
        - great if you have server-bound licenses to reuse or compliance requirements

---------------------------------------------------
5.2 Demo: Launching an EC2 instance
 -> demo showing launching ec2 t2.micro instance with Amazon Linux 2 OS, etc.

---------------------------------------------------
5.3 AWS Command Line

  AWS Management Console

  Command Line Interactions
    - interactive using AWS CLI, e.g. "aws s3 ls"

  Lesson Objectives
    1. Launch an EC2 instance via console
       - use AWS CLI in EC2 instance

         -> EC2 Launch -> AL2 OS -> t2.micro -> use new security group with ssh/http/https access -> create key -> launch

          -> install AWS on Windows
          -> in Cygwin terminal
             ssh -i <pem> ec2-user@<publicIp>
             sudo yum update
             # aws cli is installed on AL2 OS, but not configured

    2. Create an IAM User
       - give the user permissions to access and create S3 resources

       -> IAM -> user groups -> create group -> name: s3Access -> policy: AmazonS3FullAccess -> Create
              -> users -> Add user -> Name: johnsmith, group s3Access, no console access -> create
              -> users -> johnsmith -> Access Key -> create access key -> name: johnsmithkey -> create -> download

    3. Configure the AWS CLI
       - configure the CLI using the IAM user's credentials.
       - Use the CLI to create an S3 bucket and upload a file

       -> on ec2 instance
       $ aws configure (enter keys, region, output format)
       $ aws s3 ls  # now works
       # create/make a bucket
       $ aws s3 mb s3://s3clidemo-pathallfree2
       $ aws s3 ls  # shows the new bucket
       # create a file
       $ echo "Hello" > hello.txt
       # upload the file to S3
       $ aws s3 cp hello.txt s3://s3clidemo-pathallfree2

  Exam Tips

    Least Privilege
      - always give your users the minimum amount of access required to do their job

    Use Groups
      - Create IAM groups and assign your users to groups
      - group permissions are assigned using IAM policy documents
      - users will automatically inherit the permissions of the group
    AWS CLI
      - Secret Access keys
         - only get to view user Access Key, & Secret Access Key once so save
           them in a secure location, else if lost, you have to regenerate them
         - need to run "aws configure" add or update access keys
      - Don't share key pairs
         - each developers should have their own access key ID and secret access key
      - Supports Linux, Windows, MacOS
        - can install CLI on your Mac, Linux, or Windows PC
        - you can also using on EC2 instances


---------------------------------------------------
5.4 Using Roles

   What is an IAM Role
     - a role is an identity you can create in IAM that has specific permissions
     - a role is similar to a user, as it is an AWS identity with permission policies that determine
       what the identity can and cannot do in AWS
     - Instead of being uniquely associated with one person, a role is intended to be assumable by
       anyone who needs it

   Roles are Temporary
     - a role does not have the standard long-term credentials the same way passwords or access keys do
     - instead, when you assume a role, it provides you with temporary security credentials for your role session

   What else can Roles do
     - roles can be assumed by people, AWS architecture, or other system-level accounts
     - roles can allow cross-account access.
       - this allows one AWS account the ability to interact with resources in other AWS accounts

   Console Demo
     Create an IAM Role
       - ensure it has S3 acccess

       -> IAM -> Roles -> create role -> Use Case: EC2 -> Permission Polices: AmazonS3FullAccess -> Next ->
          Role Name:  S3_Admin_Access_For_Ec2 -> create role

      create an EC2 instance
        - attach the role we just created
         -> EC2 Launch -> AL2 OS -> t2.micro -> security group: launch-wizad-1 -> advanced details
            -> IAM Instance Profile: S3_Admin_Access_For_Ec2


      create an S3 bucket from EC2 instance using CLI
      Access the S3 bucket for EC2 instance using CLI
          -> in Cygwin terminal
             ssh -i <pem> ec2-user@<publicIp>
             # use CLI to make S3 bucket
             $  aws s3 mb s3://s3cliroledemo-pathallfree2
             # create a file
             $ echo "Hello World" > hello_world.txt
             # copy file to S3 bucket
             $ aws s3 cp hello_world.txt s3://s3cliroledemo-pathallfree2


   Exam Tips

     Roles
       1. the preferred option
         - Roles are preferred from a security perspecitive
       2. Avoid Hard-coding your Credentials
         - Roles allow you to provide access with the use of access key IDs and secret keys
       3. Policies
         - policies control a role's permissions
       4. Updates
         - you can update a policy attached to a role, and it will takey immediate effect
       5. Attaching and Detaching
         - you can attach and detach roles to running EC2 instances without have to stop or terminate these instances


---------------------------------------------------
5.5 Security Groups and Bootstrap

   How Computers Communicate
      Linux: ssh (port 22)
      Windows: RDP (port 3389) -> when use "remote desktop protocol" to remotely access your windows machine
      HTTP : Web Browsing (Port 80)
      HTTPS : Encrypted Web Browsing (SSL) (Port 443)

   Security Groups
     - virtual firewalls for your EC2 instances
     - By default, everything is blocked
     - to open a port to everything, use: 0.0.0.0/0  (in production env, not only should be used for 80 and 443)

   Bootstap Scripts (User data script)
     - a script that runs when the instance first runs
     - e.g.:

	#!/bin/bash
	yum update -y
        # install apache web service
	yum install httpd -y
        # enable/start apache web service
	systemctl start httpd
	systemctl enable httpd
        # create web service "index.html" file
	cd /var/www/html
	echo "<html><body><h1>Hello Cloud Gurus</h1></body></html>" > index.html

   Console Demo
         -> security group -> Create Name: WebDMZ -> open HTTP, HTTPS, ssh to everywhere -> create
         -> EC2 Launch -> AL2 OS -> t2.micro -> Name: WebServer -> security group: WebDMZ -> advanced details
            -> User Data -> paste in above "bootstrap.sh" script contents
            -> keys: <keyName> -> create instance

         In Web Browser, go to the <Public Address> -> it will show "Hello Cloud Gurus

  Exam Tips

    Security Groups
      - change to SG take effect immediately
      - you can have any number of EC2 instances within a security group
      - you can have multiple SGs attached to EC2 instance
      - All inbound traffic is block by default
      - All outbound traffic is allowed

    Bootstrap Scripts (User data)
      - a script that runs when the instance first runs
      - it passes user data to the EC2 instance and can be used to install applications
        (like web servers and databases) as well as do updates and more

---------------------------------------------------
5.6 EC2 Metadata and User Data

  What is EC2 Metadata
    - data about your ec2 instance
    - can include private IP address, public IP address, hostname, security groups, etc
  Retrieving Metadata
  Using User Data to Save Metadata
    - using the curl command, we can query metadata about our ec2 instance
    - e.g.:
       # get local IP address
       curl http://169.265.169.254/latest/meta-data/local-ipv4
       # list meta-data directory contents
       curl http://169.265.169.254/latest/meta-data/
    - using User Data to save Metadata
       - in below simple bootstrap (user data) script, the curl command is used to save
         IPv4 Address metadata
       #!/bin/bash
       curl http://169.265.169.254/latest/meta-data/local-ipv4 > myIP.txt
  Demo
       -> EC2 Launch -> AL2 OS -> t2.micro -> Name: WebServer1 -> security group: WebDMZ -> advanced details
          -> User Data -> paste in provided "metadata.sh" script contents
          -> keys: <keyName> -> create instance

          metadata.sh script:
           #!/bin/bash
           yum update -y
           # install apache web server
           yum install httpd -y
           # start apache web server
           service httpd start
           # create index.html with  displaying its public IP address
           cd /var/www/html
           echo "<html><body><h1>My IP is" > index.html
           curl http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/public-ipv4 >> index.html
           echo "</h1></body></html>" >> index.html

        -> in Web browers, go to public IP address, it will display: My IP is <public IP>

        -> SSH to instance

        ssh -i .ssh/<PEM>.pem ec2-user@<public IP>

        # show the meta-data directory:
        $ curl http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/
           ami-id
           ami-launch-index
           ami-manifest-path
           block-device-mapping/
           events/
           hostname
           identity-credentials/
           instance-action
           instance-id
           instance-life-cycle
           instance-type
           local-hostname
           local-ipv4
           mac
           metrics/
           network/
           placement/
           profile
           public-hostname
           public-ipv4
           public-keys/
           reservation-id
           security-groups
           services/


          # display user data script (in curl path, just change "meta-data" to "user-data"):
          curl http://169.254.169.254/latest/user-data
            #!/bin/bash
            yum update -y
            yum install httpd -y
            service httpd start
            cd /var/www/html
            echo "<html><body><h1>My IP is" > index.html
            curl http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/public-ipv4 >> index.html

  Exam Tips

    User Data vs Metadata
       User Data is simply the bootstrap scripts
       - metadata is data about your EC2 instances
       - you can use bootstrap scripts (user data) to access metadata

---------------------------------------------------
5.7 Networking with EC2

  Different Virtual Networking Options
    - you can attach 3 different types of virtual networking cards to your EC2 instances

    ENI (Elastic Network Interface)
      - for basic, day-to-day networking
    EN  (Enhanced Networkimg)
      - uses single root I/O virtualization (SR-IOV) to provide high performance
    EFA (Elastic Fabric Adapter)
      - accelerates High Performance Computing (HPC) and machine learning applications

  ENI
    - default EC2 Virtual Network

    - ENI is a virtual network card that allows:
      - private IPv4 address
      - public IPv4 address
      - many IPv6 addresses
      - MAC address
      - 1 or more security groups
    - Common ENI Use Cases
      - create a management network
      - use network and security applications in you VPC
      - Create dual-homed instances with workloads/roles on distinct subnets
         - separate subnets for workloads and for management, etc.
      - Create a low-budget, high-availability solution

  Enhanced Networking
    - for High-performance between 10 Gpbs - 100 Gbps
    Single Root I/O Virtualization (SR-IOV):
      - SR-OV provides higher I/O performance and lower CPU utilization
    Performance
      - provides higher bandwidth, higher packet per second (PPS) perfomance, and
        consistently inter-instance latency

  ENA vs VF
    Depending on your instance type, EN (enhanced networking can be enabled using:

      - Elastic Network Adaptor (ENA)
          - supports networks speeds of up to 100 Gbps for supported instance types
      - Intel 82599 Virtual Function (VF) Interfae
          - supports networks speeds of up to 10 Gbps for supported instance types
          - typically used on older instances
      Always choose ENA over VF interfaces

  EFA (Elastic Fabric Adaptor)
    - a network device that you can attach to your EC2 instance to accelerate High Performance
      Computing (HPC) and machine learning
    - provides lower and more consistent latency and high throughput than TCP transport
    - traditionally used in cloud-based HPC systems

    EFA can use OS-Bypass
      - An EFA is an ENA with added OS-bypass capabilities
        - instead of using TCP/IP to communicate, they uses Libfabric to bypass the kernel
          and communicate director with the EFS device
      - makes it a lot faster with much lower latency
      - OS-Bypass enables HPC and machine learning applications to bypass the OS kernel
        and communicate directly with EFS device. Not currently supported with Windows - only Linux
      - EFA OS-bypass traffic is limited to a single subnet. That EFA traffic cannot be set from
        one subnet to another

  Exam Tips

    ENI (Elastic Network Interface)
      - for basic, day-to-day networking
      - perhaps you need a separate management network from your production network or
        a separate logging network, and you to do this at low costs.
         - in this scenario, using multiple ENIs for each network
    EN  (Enhanced Networkimg) or ENA (Enhanceed Network Adaptor)
      - uses single root I/O virtualization (SR-IOV) to provide high performance
      - when using need speeds between 10 Gbps and 100 Gbps
      - anywhere you need reliable, high throughput
    EFA (Elastic Fabric Adapter)
      - An EFA is an Elastic Network Adapter (ENA) with added capabilities. It provides all of the functionality
        of an ENA, with additional OS-bypass functionality.
      - when you need to accelerates High Performance Computing (HPC) and machine learning applications
      - or if you to do an OS-bypass
      - if you a scenerio mentions HPC or ML, choose EFA network adaptor

---------------------------------------------------
5.8 Optimizing with EC2 Placement

  3 types of placement groups:
    - cluster, spread, & partition
    - there is no charge for placement group
    - need to create a placement group, before you can use it

  Cluster Placement Group
    - packs instances close together inside an AZ
    - Grouping of instances within a single AZ
    - recommended for applications that need low network latency, high network throughput, or both
    - only certain types of instances can be launched into a cluster placement group
       - supported types: current generation except for burstable performance. Some previous generatation
         instances: A1, C3, cc2, ...
   - can span peered virtual private networks (VPCs) in the same Region (? AZ)

  Spread Placement Group
    - a group of instances that are each placed on distinct underlying hardware
    - recommended for applications that have a small number critical instance that should be
      kept separate from each other
    - example use case: primary database and backup database

  Partition Placement Group
    - each partition placement group has its own set of racks
    - each rack has its own network and power source
    - no 2 partitions within a placement group share the same racks, allowing you to
      isolate the impact of hardware failures within your application
    - EC2 divided each group into logical segments called partitions

  Exam Tips

     3 types of placement groups
        cluster placement group
          - low latency, high network throuphput
          - can't span multiple AZs
          - AWS recommends homogenous instances with cluster PGs
        Spread placement group
          - individual critical EC2 instances that need to be on their own dedicate HW
          - recommended for applications that have a small number critical instance that should be
            kept separate from each other
          - example use case: primary database and backup database
        Partition placement group
          - each partition placement group has its own set of racks
          - multiple EC2 instances, HDFS, HBase, and Cassandra
             - HDFS ([Apache] Hadoop Distributed File System): distributed file system that handles large data
               sets running on commodity Hardware
             - [Apache] HBase: column-oriented, non-relational database
             - Cassandra is a No-SQL distributed database
          -  can have partitions in multiple AZ in the same Region

      Multiple AZs:
        - cluster PGs cannot span multiple AZs, whereas spread PGs and partition PGs can
      Instance Types
        -  only certain instance types can be launched in a placement group (compute optimized,
           GPU, memory optimized, storage optimized)

      You can't merge placement groups

      You can move an existing instance into a placement group. Before you move the instance, the
        instance must be in the stopped state. You can move or remove an instance using the AWS CLI
        or AWS SDK, but you can't do it via the console yet.

---------------------------------------------------
5.9 Solving Licencing Issues with with Dedicated Hosts

    EC2 pricing options
      on-demand instances
        - pay by the hour or second depending on the type of instance you run
        - great for flexiblity
      reserved instances
        - reserve capacity for 1 to 3 years
        - up to 72% discount on the hourly charge
        - the long the contract and the more you pay up front, the greater the discount
        - great if you have known, fixed requirements
      spot instances
        - purchase unused capacity at a discount of up to 90%
        - prices fluctuate with supply and demand
        - great for applications with flexible start and end times
        - If EC2 interrupts your Spot Instance in the first hour, before a full hour of running time,
          you're not charged for the partial hour used.
      dedicated hosts
        - a physical EC2 server dedicated for your use
        - the most expensive option
        - great if you have server-bound licenses to reuse or compliance requirements

    Dedicated Hosts
      - Compliance
        - regulatory requirements that may not support multi-tenant virtualization
      - Licensing
        - licensing that does not support multi-tenancy or cloud deployments
        - e.g.: Microsoft licensing, Oracle licencing, ...
      - on-demand
         - dedicated hosts can be purchased on-demand
      - reserved
         - can be purchased as a reservation for up to 70% off the on-demand price

    Exam Tips

       Any question that talks about special licensing requirements think of dedicated hosts

      dedicated hosts
        - a physical EC2 server dedicated for your use
        - allows you to use your existing per-socket, per-core, or per-VPM software licenses,
          including Windows Server, Microsoft SQL Server, and SUSE Linux Enterprise Server

---------------------------------------------------
5.10 Timing Workloads with Spot Instances and Spot Fleets

  EC2 Spot Instances
    - let you take advantage of unused EC2 capacity
    - Spot instances are available at up to a 90% discount compared to On-Demand prices

  When to Use Spot instances
    - Stateless, fault-tolerant, or flexibe applications
    - applications such as big data, containerized workloads, CI/CD (Continuous Integration /
      Continuous Delivery), high performance computing (HPC) and other test and development workloads

  Spot Prices
    - must 1st decide on you maximum spot price
    - the instance will be provisioned so long as the Spot price is below your max spot price
    - the hourly spot price varies depending on the capacity and the region and even the AZs
    - if the spot price goes above you maximum, you have 2 min to choose whether to stop or
      terminate you instance

  Spot Blocks
    - you may use a Spot Block to stop your Spot instances from being terminated even if the Spot
      price goes over you max spot price.
    - you can set spot blocks for between 1 to 6 hours currently
    - Pricing is based on the requested duration and the available capacity, and is typically
      30% to 45% less than On-Demand, with an additional 5% off during non-peak hours for the region.

  Spot Pricing History
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/using-spot-instances-history.html
     - can get 3 hours, 1 day, 1 week, 1 month, 3 month spot price history by instance type/ region/AZ
     -> AWS Console -> EC2 -> Spot Requests -> Pricing History
     -> AWS CLI: describe-spot-price-history

  Spot Uses Cases
    - big data and analytics
    - containerized workloads
    - CI/CD (Continuous Integration / Continuous Delivery) and testing
    - Image and media rendering
    - high performance computing (HPC) -> mostly likely for Exam

  Spot Instances are NOT good for
    - persistent workloads (e.g. web servers, database servers)
    - Critical Jobs
    - databases

  Terminating Spot Instances
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/spot-requests.html
    - spot request includes
        - maximum price
        - desired number of instances
        - launch specification (e.g. AMI, ...)
        - request type: one time | persistent (if persistent includes request is valid from/to)

    - A one-time spot instance request remains active until EC2 launches the spot instance,
      the request expires, or you cancel the request. If capacity is not available, your spot
      instance is terminated and the spot instance request is closed.

    - A persistent spot instance request remains active until it expires or you cancel it,
      even if the request is fulfilled. If capacity is not available, your spot instance is interrupted.
      After your instance is interrupted, when capacity becomres avaiable again, the Spot instance
      is started if stopped or resume if hibernated.

    - How to terminate spot instances start by a persistent spot request:
       1. cancel spot request
       2. terminate spot instances

  Spot Fleets
    - a collection of Spot Instances and (optionally) on-demand instances
    - the spot fleet attempts to launch the number spot instances and on-demand instances to
      meet the target capacity you specified in the spot fleet request
    - The request for Spot instances is fulfilled if there is available capacity and the max
      price   you specified in the request exceeds the current spot price.
    - you can specify the max per hour that you are willing to pay for your fleet, and Spot
      instance launch until it reaches that amount.

  Spot Fleet Request types:
    request:
      - one time request for desired capacity.
      - if capacity is diminished because of spot interruptions, the fleet does not attempt
        replenish Spot instances, nor does it submit requests in alternative spot capacity pools
    maintain:
    - The spot fleet also attempts to maintain its target capacity fleet if your spot instances
      are interrupted by automatically replenishing any interrupted Spot instances

  [Spot Fleet] Launch Pools
    - spot fleets will try and match the target capacity with your price restraints
    1. set up different launch pools. Define things like EC2 instance type, operating system, and AZs
    2. You can have multiple pools, and the fleet will choose the best way to implement depending
       on the strategy you define
    3. spot fleets will stop launching instances once you reach your price threhold or capacity desire


  Spot Fleet Strategies
     capacityOptimized
       - spot instances come fom the pools with optimal capacity for the number of instances launching
     diversified
       - Spot instances are distributed across all pools
     lowestPrice
       - Spot instances come from the pool with the lowest price. Default strategy
     InstancePoolsToUseCount
       - Spot instances are distributed across the number spot instance pools you specify.
       - The parameter is valid only wne used in combination with lowestPrice

  Exam Tips
     Spot Instances
       - Spot instances save up to 90% of the cost of on-demand instances
       - Useful for any type of computing where you don't need persistent storage
     Spot block
       - You can block spot instances from terminating by using a spot block
     Spot Fleet
       - A spot fleet is a collection of spot instances and (optionally) on-demand instances

---------------------------------------------------
5.11 Deploying vCenter in AWS with VMware Cloud on AWS

   Why use VMware on AWS
     - VMware is used by orgainizations around the world for private cloud deployments
     - Some organizations opt for a hybrid cloud strategy and would like to leverage AWS services

   Use Cases
     hybrid Cloud
       - connect your on-premises cloud to AWS public cloud, and manage a hybrid workload
     Cloud Migration
       - migrate your existing cloud env to AWS using VMware built-in tools
     Disaster Recovery
       - VMware is famous for its disaster recovery technology.
       - Using hybrid cloud, you can have inexpensive disaster recovery env on AWS
     Leverage AWS
         - us over 200 AWS services to update your applications or to create new ones

   Deployment of VMware cloud on AWS
     - it runs on dedicated hardware hosted in AWS using a single AWS account
     - each host has 2 sockets with 18 cores per socket, 512 GB RAM, and 15.2 TB
       Raw SSD storage
     - Each host is capable of running multiple VMware instances (up to hundreds)
     - clusters can start with 2 hosts up to a max of 16 hosts per cluster
     - these hosts are yours vCenter clusters

   Exam Tips
     - so far, not seen on exams
     - you can deploy vCenter on AWS Cloud using VMware
     - perfect for extending your private VMware cloud into the AWS public cloud
     - need to order vCenter from VMware
     Note: Now: Customers can buy VMware Cloud on AWS directly through AWS and AWS Partner Network (APN)

---------------------------------------------------
5.12 Extending AWS Beyond the cloud with AWS outposts

https://aws.amazon.com/outposts/

  What is Outposts
    - Outposts brings AWS data center directly to you, on-premise.
    - Allows you to have the large variety of AWS services (e.g. S3 buckets, ec2 instances)
      in your data center
    - You can have outposts in sizes as 1U and 2U servers all the way up to 42U
      racks and multiple-rack deployments

  Outposts Benefits
    Hybrid Cloud
      - Create a hybrid cloud where you can leverage AWS services inside your own data center
    Fully Managed Infrastructure
      - AWS can manage the infrastructure for you.
      - you do not need a dedicated team to look after your Outposts infrastructure
    Consistency
      - Brings the AWS Management Console, APIs, and SDKs into your data center, allowing
        uniform consistency in you hybrid environment

  Note: 42U rack holds 42 1U devices, or any combination of 1U, 2U, 3U or other rack units
        adding up to 42.

  Outpost Family Members
    Outposts Rack
      - whole racks installed in your data centers or co-location space
      hardware: available with a single 42U (U: unit or rack unit) rack and scale up to a 96 racks
      servers: provides AWS compute, storage, database, and other services locally
      results: gives the same AWS infrastructure, services, and APIs in your data center

    Outposts Servers
      - individual servers
      Hardware: individual servers in 1U or 2U form factor
      Use Cases: Useful for small space requires, such as retail store, branch office, healthcare
         locations, of factory floor
      Results: Provides local compute and networking services

  Process
    Order:
      - log in to AWS management console and order your outposts configuration
    Install:
      - AWS staff will come on-site to install and deploy the hardware, including power, networking,
        and connectivity
    Launch:
      - Using the AWS Management console, you can launch instances on your Outpost on-site
    Build:
      - start building your on-site AWS enviroment

  Exam Tips

      Scenario about extending AWS to your data center -> think AWS Outposts
      AWS Outposts rack: for large deployment in your data center
      AWS Outposts sever: for smaller deployments


---------------------------------------------------
5.13 EC2 Exam Tips

    EC2 Overview
      - EC2 is like a VM, hosted in AWS instead of your own data center
      - select the capacity your need right now
      - grow and shrink when you need
      - pay for what you use
      - to provision, wait for minutes, not months to access

    EC2 pricing options
      on-demand instances
        - pay by the hour or second depending on the type of instance you run
        - great for flexiblity
      reserved instances
        - reserve capacity for 1 to 3 years
        - up to 72% discount on the hourly charge
        - the long the contract and the more you pay up front, the greater the discount
        - great if you have known, fixed requirements
      spot instances
        - purchase unused capacity at a discount of up to 90%
        - prices fluctuate with supply and demand
        - great for applications with flexible start and end times
      dedicated hosts
        - a physical EC2 server dedicated for your use
        - the most expensive option
        - great if you have server-bound licenses to reuse or compliance requirements
          (e.g. requirements that cannot run on multi-tenant HW)


    Least Privilege
      - always give your users the minimum amount of access required to do their job

    Use Groups
      - Create IAM groups and assign your users to groups
      - group permissions are assigned using IAM policy documents
      - users will automatically inherit the permissions of the group

    AWS CLI
      - Secret Access keys
         - only get to view user Access Key, & Secret Access Key once so save
           them in a secure location, else if lost, you have to regenerate them
         - need to run "aws configure" add or update access keys
      - Don't share key pairs
         - each developers should have their own access key ID and secret access key
      - Supports Linux, Windows, MacOS
        - can install CLI on your Mac, Linux, or Windows PC
        - you can also using on EC2 instances

     Roles
       1. the preferred option
         - Roles are preferred from a security perspecitive
       2. Avoid Hard-coding your Credentials
         - Roles allow you to provide access with the use of access key IDs and secret keys
       3. Policies
         - policies control a role's permissions
       4. Updates
         - you can update a policy attached to a role, and it will takey immediate effect
       5. Attaching and Detaching
         - you can attach and detach roles to running EC2 instances without have to stop or terminate
           these instances

    Security Groups
      - change to SG take effect immediately
      - you can have any number of EC2 instances within a security group
      - you can have multiple SGs (up to 5) attached ot EC2 instance
      - All inbound traffic is block by default
      - All outbound traffic is allowed

    Bootstrap Scripts (User data)
      - a script that runs when the instance first runs
      - runs a root level
      - it passes user data to the EC2 instance and can be used to install applications
        (like web servers and databases) as well as do updates and more

    User Data vs Metadata
       User Data is simply the bootstrap scripts
       - metadata is data about your EC2 instances
       - you can use bootstrap scripts (user data) to access metadata


    Networking with EC2:
      ENI (Elastic Network Interface)
        - for basic, day-to-day networking
        - perhaps you needa separate management network from your production network or
          a separate logging network, and you to do this at low costs.
           - in this scenario, using multiple ENIs for each network
      EN  (Enhanced Networkimg)
        - uses single root I/O virtualization (SR-IOV) to provide high performance
        - when using need speeds between 10 Gbps and 100 Gbps
        - anywhere you need reliable, high throughput
      EFA (Elastic Fabric Adapter)
        - when you need to accelerates High Performance Computing (HPC) and machine learning applications
        - of if you to do an OS-bypass
        - if you a scenerio mentions HPC or ML, choose EFA network adaptor

    Placement Groups:
      3 types of placement groups
         cluster placement group
           - low latency, high network throuphput
           - can't span multiple AZs
           - AWS recommends homogenoush instances with cluster PGs
         Spread placement group
           - individual critical EC2 instances that need to be on their own dedicate HW
         Partition placement group
           - multiple EC2 instances, HDFS, HBase, and Cassandra
              - HDFS ([Apache] Hadoop Distributed File System): distributed file system that handles
                large data sets running on commodity Hardware
              - [Apache] HBase: column-oriented, non-relational database
              - Cassandra is a No-SQL distributed database

       Multiple AZs:
         - cluster PGs cannot span multiple AZs, whereas spread PGs and partition PGs can
       Instance Types
         -  only certain instance types can be launched in a placement group (compute optimized,
            GPU, memory optimized, storage optimized)

       You can't merge placement groups

       You can move an existing instance into a placement group. Before you move the instance, the
         instance must be in the stopped state. You can move or remove an instance using the AWS CLI
         or AWS SDK, but you can't do it via the console yet.

   Dedicated Hosts
       Any question that talks about special licensing requirements compliance requirements,
       think of dedicated hosts

      dedicated hosts overview
        - a physical EC2 server dedicated for your use
        - allows you to use your existing per-socket, per-core, or per-VPM software licenses,
          including Windows Server, Microsoft SQL Server, and SUSE Linux Enterprise Server

  Spot Instances, Spot Blocks, and Spot Fleets

     spot instances:
       - save up to 90% of the cost of on-demand instance
       - useful for any type of computing where you don't need persistent storage

     spot blocks:
       - you may use a Spot Block to stop your Spot instances from being terminated even if the Spot
         price goes over you max spot price.
       - you can set spot blocks for between 1 to 6 hours currently
       - Pricing is based on the requested duration and the available capacity, and is typically
         30% to 45% less than On-Demand, with an additional 5% off during non-peak hours for the region.

     spot fleet:
       - a collection of spot instances and optionally on-demand instances

   VMware vCenter
     - you can deploy vCenter on AWS using VMware
     - perfect solution for extending your private VMware Cloud into AWS public cloud
     AWS VMware use cases (where you want to use the VMware tools)
        - cloud migration
        - disaster recover

   AWS Outposts
      Scenario about extending AWS to your data center -> think AWS Outposts
      AWS Outposts rack: for large deployment in your data center
      AWS Outposts sever: for smaller deployments

Learning objectives
 1. Manually Install Software on webserver-01

 2. Use a Bootstrap Script to Build webserver-02 and Debug Issues
 3. Use a Fixed Bootstrap Script to Build webserver-03

5.15 Using EC2 Roles and Instance Profiles in AWS Lab

Learning Objectives
 1. Create a Trust Policy and Role Using the AWS CLI
 2. Create Instance Profile and Attach Role to an EC2 Instance
 3. Test S3 Permissions via the AWS CLI
 4. Create an IAM Policy and Role Using the AWS Management Console
 5. Attach IAM Role to an EC2 Instance Using the AWS Management Console


1. Create a Trust Policy and Role Using the AWS CLI
Obtain the labreferences.txt File
    Navigate to S3.
    From the list of buckets, open the one that contains the text s3bucketlookupfiles in the middle of its name.
    Select the labreferences.txt file.
    Click Actions > Download.
    Open the labreferences.txt file, as we will need to reference it throughout the lab.

Log in to Bastion Host and Set the AWS CLI Region and Output Type
    Navigate to EC2 > Instances.
    Copy the public IP of the Bastion Host instance.
    Open a terminal, and log in to the bastion host via SSH:
    ssh cloud_user@<BASTION_HOST_PUBLIC_IP>

    For more information on how to connect to a Linux instance using SSH, please refer to the AWS Documentation. For more information on how to connect to a Linux instance using Putty, please refer to Connect to your Linux instance from Windows using PuTTY.

    Enter the password provided for it on the lab page.
    Run the following command:
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ aws configure
    Press Enter twice to leave the AWS Access Key ID and AWS Secret Access Key blank.
    Enter us-east-1 as the default region name.
    Enter json as the default output format.

Create IAM Trust Policy for an EC2 Role
    Create a file called trust_policy_ec2.json:
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ vim trust_policy_ec2.json

    To avoid adding unnecessary spaces or hashes, type :set paste and then i to enter insert mode.

    Paste in the following content:
    {
      "Version": "2012-10-17",
      "Statement": [
        {
          "Effect": "Allow",
          "Principal": {"Service": "ec2.amazonaws.com"},
          "Action": "sts:AssumeRole"
        }
      ]
    }

    Save and quit the file by pressing Escape followed by :wq!.

Create the DEV_ROLE IAM Role
    Run the following AWS CLI command:
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ aws iam create-role --role-name DEV_ROLE --assume-role-policy-document file://trust_policy_ec2.json

4. Create an IAM Policy Defining Read-Only Access Permissions to an S3 Bucket
    Create a file called dev_s3_read_access.json:
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ vim dev_s3_read_access.json
    To avoid adding unnecessary spaces or hashes, type :set paste and then i to enter insert mode.
    Enter the following content, replacing <DEV_S3_BUCKET_NAME> with the bucket name provided in the labreferences.txt file:
    {
        "Version": "2012-10-17",
        "Statement": [
            {
              "Sid": "AllowUserToSeeBucketListInTheConsole",
              "Action": ["s3:ListAllMyBuckets", "s3:GetBucketLocation"],
              "Effect": "Allow",
              "Resource": ["arn:aws:s3:::*"]
            },
            {
                "Effect": "Allow",
                "Action": [
                    "s3:Get*",
                    "s3:List*"
                ],
                "Resource": [
                    "arn:aws:s3:::<DEV_S3_BUCKET_NAME>/*",
                    "arn:aws:s3:::<DEV_S3_BUCKET_NAME>"
                ]
            }
        ]
    }

    Save and quit the file by pressing Escape followed by :wq!.

    Create the managed policy called DevS3ReadAccess:
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ aws iam create-policy --policy-name DevS3ReadAccess --policy-document file://dev_s3_read_access.json
    Copy the policy ARN in the output, and paste it into the labreferences.txt file - we'll need it in a minute.

2. Create Instance Profile and Attach Role to an EC2 Instance
Attach Managed Policy to Role
    Attach the managed policy to the role, replacing <DevS3ReadAccess_POLICY_ARN> with the ARN you just copied:
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ aws iam attach-role-policy --role-name DEV_ROLE --policy-arn "<DevS3ReadAccess_POLICY_ARN>"
    Verify the managed policy was attached:
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ aws iam list-attached-role-policies --role-name DEV_ROLE

Create the Instance Profile and Add the DEV_ROLE via the AWS CLI
    Create instance profile named DEV_PROFILE:
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ aws iam create-instance-profile --instance-profile-name DEV_PROFILE
    Add role to the DEV_PROFILE called DEV_ROLE:
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ aws iam add-role-to-instance-profile --instance-profile-name DEV_PROFILE --role-name DEV_ROLE
    Verify the configuration:
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ aws iam get-instance-profile --instance-profile-name DEV_PROFILE

Attach the DEV_PROFILE Role to an Instance
    In the AWS console, navigate to EC2 > Instances.
    Copy the instance ID of the instance named Web Server instance and paste it into the labreferences.txt file — we'll need it in a second.
    In the terminal, attach the DEV_PROFILE to an EC2 instance, replacing <LAB_WEB_SERVER_INSTANCE_ID> with the Web Server instance ID you just copied:
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ aws ec2 associate-iam-instance-profile --instance-id <LAB_WEB_SERVER_INSTANCE_ID> --iam-instance-profile Name="DEV_PROFILE"
    Verify the configuration (be sure to replace <LAB_WEB_SERVER_INSTANCE_ID> with the Web Server instance ID again):
    [cloud_user@bastion]$ aws ec2 describe-instances --instance-ids <LAB_WEB_SERVER_INSTANCE_ID>

    This command's output should show this instance is using DEV_PROFILE as an IamInstanceProfile. Verify this by locating the IamInstanceProfile section in the output, and look below to make sure the "Arn" ends in /DEV_PROFILE.

3. Test S3 Permissions via the AWS CLI
    In the AWS console, copy the public IP of the Web Server instance.
    Open a new terminal.
    Log in to the web server instance via SSH:
    ssh cloud_user@<WEB_SERVER_PUBLIC_IP>
    Use the same password for the bastion host provided on the lab page.
    Verify the instance is assuming the DEV_ROLE role:
    [cloud_user@webserver]$ aws sts get-caller-identity
    We should see DEV_ROLE in the Arn.
    List the buckets in the account:
    [cloud_user@webserver]$ aws s3 ls
    Copy the entire name (starting with cfst) of the bucket with s3bucketdev in its name.

    Attempt to view the files in the s3bucketdev- bucket, replacing <s3bucketdev-123> with the bucket name you just copied:
    [cloud_user@webserver]$ aws s3 ls s3://<s3bucketdev-123>
    We should see a list of files.

Create an IAM Policy and Role Using the AWS Management Console
Create Policy
    In the AWS console, navigate to IAM > Policies.
    Click Create policy.
    Click the JSON tab.
    Paste the following text as the policy, replacing <PROD_S3_BUCKET_NAME> with the bucket name provided in the labreferences.txt file:
    {
        "Version": "2012-10-17",
        "Statement": [
            {
              "Sid": "AllowUserToSeeBucketListInTheConsole",
              "Action": ["s3:ListAllMyBuckets", "s3:GetBucketLocation"],
              "Effect": "Allow",
              "Resource": ["arn:aws:s3:::*"]
            },
            {
                "Effect": "Allow",
                "Action": [
                    "s3:Get*",
                    "s3:List*"
                ],
                "Resource": [
                    "arn:aws:s3:::<PROD_S3_BUCKET_NAME>/*",
                    "arn:aws:s3:::<PROD_S3_BUCKET_NAME>"
                ]
            }
        ]
    }

    Click Next: Tags.
    Click Next: Review.
    Enter ProdS3ReadAccess as the policy name.
    Click Create policy.

Create Role
    Click Roles in the left-hand menu.
    Click Create role.
    Under Choose a use case, select EC2.
    Click Next: Permissions.
    In the Filter policies search box, enter ProdS3ReadAccess.
    Click the checkbox to select ProdS3ReadAccess.
    Click Next: Tags.
    Click Next: Review.
    Give it a Role name of PROD_ROLE.
    Click Create role.
5. Attach IAM Role to an EC2 Instance Using the AWS Management Console
    Navigate to EC2 > Instances.
    Select the Web Server instance.
    Click Actions > Security > Modify IAM role.
    In the IAM role dropdown, select PROD_ROLE.
    Click Save.

Test the Configuration
    Open the existing terminal connected to the Web Server instance. (You may need to reconnect if you've been disconnected.)
    Determine the identity currently being used:
    [cloud_user@webserver]$ aws sts get-caller-identity
    This time, we should see PROD_ROLE in the Arn.
    List the buckets:
    [cloud_user@webserver]$ aws s3 ls

    Copy the entire name (starting with cfst) of the bucket with s3bucketprod in its name.
    Attempt to view the files in the s3bucketprod- bucket, replacing <s3bucketprod-123> with the bucket name you just copied:
    [cloud_user@webserver]$ aws s3 ls s3://<s3bucketprod-123>
    It should list the files.
    In the aws s3 ls command output, copy the entire name (starting with cfst) of the bucket with s3bucketsecret in its name.
    Attempt to view the files in the <s3bucketsecret-123> bucket, replacing <s3bucketsecret-123> with the bucket name you just copied:
    [cloud_user@webserver]$ aws s3 ls s3://<s3bucketsecret-123>

    This time, our access will be denied - which means our configuration is properly set up.


Note:
   Roles
     - CLI create role steps:
       create assume role with assume role trust entites policy document (what resource or account/user can assume the role)
       Note: in AWS console, this can be automatically created from "Selected trust entity" options selected
       $ aws iam create-role --role-name DEV_ROLE --assume-role-policy-document file://trust_policy_ec2.json
       if need create role policy document (if not using aws managed policies)
       $ aws iam create-policy --policy-name DevS3ReadAccess --policy-document file://dev_s3_read_access.json
       Attach Policy ARN to role
       $ aws iam attach-role-policy --role-name DEV_ROLE --policy-arn "<DevS3ReadAccess_POLICY_ARN>"

     Instance Profiles (create ec2 instance profile, an assign role to it)
       Note: if creating Role for EC2 from console, instance profile is automatically created,
             but with CLI, it needs to be separately created
       # create instance profile
       $ aws iam create-instance-profile --instance-profile-name DEV_PROFILE
       # assign Role to instance profile
       $ aws iam add-role-to-instance-profile --instance-profile-name DEV_PROFILE --role-name DEV_ROLE
       # verify instance profile
       $ aws iam get-instance-profile --instance-profile-name DEV_PROFILE

       # associate instance profile to webserver instance
       $ aws ec2 associate-iam-instance-profile --instance-id <LAB_WEB_SERVER_INSTANCE_ID> --iam-instance-profile Name="DEV_PROFILE"
       # verify webserver instance has been associated with ROLE via instance profile
       $ aws ec2 describe-instances --instance-ids <LAB_WEB_SERVER_INSTANCE_ID>
       # show identity currently being used (verify it is expected role)
       $ aws sts get-caller-identity

5.16 AWS Certified Solution Architect Elastic Compute Cloud (EC2) Quiz

Question 3
True or False? If you reboot your EC2 instance, the user data will rerun automatically.
aws: False

Correct Answer:
By default, user data runs one time and one time only. Reference Documentation: Work with Instance User Data
