Chapter 6 Elastic Block Storage (EBS) and Elastic File Storage (EFS)
---------------------------------------------------
6.1 EBS Overview

  EBS Volumes
    - different types of EBS volumes and Use cases for each type
    - storage volume that you can attach to your EC2 instances
    - virtual disk in the cloud

  Mission Critical
    - Production workloads
       - designed for mission-critical workloads
    - Highly available
      - automatically replicated within a single AZ to protect against HW failures
    - scalable
      - dynamically increase capacity and change the volume type with no downtime or
        performance impact to your live systems

  EBS Volume Types: Solid State Disk
    General Purpose SSD (gp2)
      - balance of price and performance
      - 3 IOPS per GB, up to 16K IOPS per volume
      - gp2 volumes smaller than 1 TB can burst up to 3K IOPS
      - Good for boot volumes or development and test applications that are not latency sensitive
    General Purpose SSD (gp3)
      - balance of price and performance
      - Predicatable 3K IOPS baseline perfomrance and 125 Mb/s regardless of volume size
      - Ideal for applications that require high performance at a low cost, such as MySQL, Cassandra,
        virtual desktops, and Hadoop analytics
      - customers  looking for higher performance can scale up to 16K IOPS and 1000 Mb/s for additional fee
      - top performance of gp3 is 4 times faster than max throughput of gp2
      Note: should not be need to memorize IOPS or throughput for exam
            should not be need to select between gp2 and gp3 for exam
    Provisioned IOPS SSD (io1)
       - legacy high performance option and the most expensive
       - 50 IOPS per GB, up to 64K IOPS per volume
       - use if you need more than 16K IOPS
       - used for I/O-intensive applications, large databases, and latency-sensitive workloads
    Provisioned IOPS SSD (io2)
       - 500 IOPS per GB, up to 64K IOPS
       - 99.999 durability instead of up to 99.9
       - higher durability and more IOPS than io1
       - io2 is the same price as io1
       - used for I/O-intensive applications, large databases, and latency-sensitive workloads.
         Applications that need high levels of durability
    Provisioned IOPS SSD (io2 Block Express)
       - up to 256K IOPS
       - 99.999 durability
       - latest generation - availabl only with some instance types and in some regions
       - same durability as io2 and more IOPS than io2
       - io2 Block Express is the same price as io2
       - used for most I/O-intensive applications, mission critical deployments of NoSQL & Relational Databases
         Applications that need high levels of durability


  EBS Volume Types: Hard Disk Drives (Mb/s-Intensive)
    Throughput Optimized HDD (st1)
      - low-cost HDD volume
      - baseline throuphput of 40 Mb/s per TB
      - Ability to burst up to 250 Mb/s per TB
      - max throughput of 500 Mb/s per volume
      - Used for Frequently accessed, throughput-intensive workloads
      - use cases: big data, data warehousing, ETL (Extract/Tranform/Load), and log processing
      - cost-effective way to store mountains of data
      - cannot be a boot volume
    Cold HDD (sc1)
      - low cost options
      - baseline throughput of 12 Mb/s per TB
      - Ability to burst up to 80 Mb/s per TB
      - max throughput of 250 Mb/s per volume
      - a good choice for colder data requiring fewer scans per day
      - good for applications that need the lowest cost and performance is not a factor
      - cannot be a boot volume

  IOPs vs Throughput
    IOPS
      - measures the number of read and write operations per second
      - important metric for quick transactions, low-latency apps, and transactional workloads
      - ability to action reads and writes very quickly
      - for IOPS, choose provisioned IOPS SSD (io1 or io2)
    Throughput
      - measures the number of bits read or written per sec (Mb/s)
      - important metric for large datasets, large I/O sizes, complex queries
      - the ability to deal with large datasets
      - For thoughput, Choose Throughput Optimized HDD (st1)

  Note:
    Amazon Data Lifecycle Manager
      - use to automate the creation, retention, and deletion of EBS snapshots and EBS-backed AMIs
      - When combined with the monitoring features of CloudWatch Events and AWS CloudTrail, Amazon Data Lifecycle
        Manager provides a complete backup solution for Amazon EC2 instances and individual EBS volumes at no additional cost.
      - it helps you:
        - Protect valuable data by enforcing a regular backup schedule.
        - Create standardized AMIs that can be refreshed at regular intervals.
        - Retain backups as required by auditors or internal compliance.
        - Reduce storage costs by deleting outdated backups.
        - Create disaster recovery backup policies that back up data to isolated accounts.

  Exam Tips

    EBS volume type differences
      https://aws.amazon.com/premiumsupport/knowledge-center/ebs-volume-type-differences/

    EBS SSD Volume types
      Note: if you want more than 16K IOPS, then io1/io2
      General Purpose SSD (gp2)
        - suitable for boot disks and general applications
        - 16K IOPS per volume
        - up to 99.9 durability
        - Good for boot volumes or development and test applications that are not latency sensitive
      General Purpose SSD (gp3)
        - suitable for high perfomance applications
        - Predicatable 3K IOPS baseline perfomrance and 125 Mb/s regardless of volume size
        - up to 99.9 durability
      Provisioned IOPS SSD (io1)
        - suitable for OLTP (On-Line Transaction Processing) and latency-sensitive applications
        - 4K MB/s throughput per volume, up to 64K IOPS per volume
        - use if you need more than 16K IOPS
        - up to 99.9 durability
        - used for High perfomance and most expensive
      Provisioned IOPS SSD (io2)
        - suitable for OLTP (On-Line Transaction Processing) and latency-sensitive applications
        - 500 IOPS per GB, up to 64K IOPS
        - 99.999 durability instead of up to 99.9
      Provisioned IOPS SSD (io2 block express)
        - used for most I/O-intensive applications, mission critical deployments of NoSQL & Relational Databases
          Applications that need high levels of durability
        - up to 256K IOPS
        - 99.999 durability
        - latest generation - available only with some instance types and in some regions
        - io2 Block Express is the same price as io2


    EBS HDD Volume types
      Throughput Optimized HDD (st1)
        - use cases: big data, data warehousing, ETL (Extract/Tranform/Load), and log processing
        - max throughput of 500 Mb/s per volume
        - cannot be a boot volume
      Cold HDD (sc1)
        - max throughput of 250 Mb/s per volume
        - used for less frequently accessed data
        - low cost options
        - cannot be a boot volume
        - up to 99.9 durability

---------------------------------------------------
6.2 Volumes and Snapshots

    Volumes
      - volumes are simply virtual hard disks
      - you need a minimum of 1 volume per EC2 - root volume
    Snapshots
      - snapshots exists on S3
      - snapshots are at a point in time
         - a copy of the virtual disk / volume at a point in time
      - Snapshots are incremental
      - the 1st snapshot
        - it may take some time to create as there is no previous point-in-time copy

    3 Tips for Snapshots
      - Consistent snapshots
        - it is recommended that you stop the instance and take a snapshot
          - ensures files are not cached in memory at time of snapshot
      - Encrypted snapshots
        - if you take a snapshot of an encrypted EBS volume, the snapshot will be encrypted automatically
      - sharing snapshots
         - you can share snapshots, but only in the region in which they were created
         - to share to other regions, you will need to copy them to the destination region first

    What to know about EBS Volumes
      Location:
        - EBS volumes will always be in the same AZ as your EC2 which it is attached
      Resizing:
        - resize on the fly
           - you can resized EBS volumes on the fly. You do not need to stop or start the instance.
             However, you will nned to extend the filesystem in the OS so the OS can see the resized volume
      Volume type:
        - switch volume types
          - you can change volume types on the fly (e.g. go from gp2 to io2). You do not need to stop or
            restart the instance

    Console Demo
      -> create ec2 with 2 volumes (root: gp2, 2nd: sc1)
          -> changed 2nd from sc1 to io2
          -> detached 2nd
          -> deletec 2nd volume
       -> select root volume -> action -> snapshot
       # copy snapshot to another region
        -> select snapshot -> action -> Copy snapshot -> set destination region
           -> create snapshot

        -> select new snapshot -> action -> create image

    Exam Tips

      EBS Volumes and snapshots
        - volumes exist on EBS, where as snapshots exist on S3
        - you can resize EBS volumes on the fly as well as changing the volume types

      Snapshots
        - snapshots exists on S3
        - snapshots are at a point in time
           - a copy of the virtual disk / volume at a point in time
        - Snapshots are incremental
        - the 1st snapshot
          - it may take some time to create as there is no previous point-in-time copy

      Sharing snapshots
         - you can share snapshots, but only in the region in which they were created
         - to share to other regions, you will need to copy them to the destination region first

---------------------------------------------------
6.3 Protecting EBS Volumes

  EBS Encryption
    - EBS encrypts your volume with a data key using industry-standand AES-256 (Advanced Encryption Standard)
    - uses AWS key management service (AWS KMS) and customer master keys (CMK) when creating encrypted volumes and
      snapshots
  What happens when you encrypt
    - data at rest is encrypted inside the volume
    - data in flight moving between instance and volume is encrypted
    - all snapshots [created for encrypted volumes] are encrypted
    - all volumes created for encrypted snapshots are encrypted

  Encryption Explored
    handled transparently
    - encryption and decryption are handled transparently (you don't need to do anything)
    Latency:
    - has a minimal impact on latency
    Copying
    - copying and unencrypted snapshot allows encryption
    Snapshots
    - snapshots of encrypted volumes are encrypted
    Root Device Volumes
    - you can no encrypt root device volumes upon creation

  4 Steps to Encrypt unencrypted [root] Volume
    - create a snapshot of the unencrypted root device volume
    - create a copy of the snapshot and select the encrypt option
    - Create an AMI from the encrypted snapshot
    - Use encrypted AMI to launch new encrypted instances

  Console Demo
    -> launch EC2 with root volume unecrypted
        Note: You can choose to encrypt the root volume during launch steps
    # steps to create encrypted image of the unencrypted root volume
    -> create snapshot of root volume
       -> select volume -> Action -> create snapshot
     # create encrypted snapshot from snapshot
     -> select snapshot -> copy snapshot -> select "encrytion" -> copy
     # create an image from the encrypted snapshot
     -> select encrypted snapshot -> action -> create image -> create

  Exam Tips
    Encrypted Volumes
      - data at rest is encrypted inside the volume
      - data in flight moving between instance and volume is encrypted
      - all snapshots [created for encrypted volumes] are encrypted
      - all volumes created from encrypted snapshots are encrypted

    How to encrypt exiting Volumes that are unencrypted
      - create a snapshot of the unencrypted root device volume
      - create a copy of the snapshot and select the encrypt option
      - Create an AMI from the encrypted snapshot
      - Use encrypted AMI to launch new encrypted instances

---------------------------------------------------
6.4 EC2 Hibernation

  EBS Behavioral Reviewed
    - can stop and terminate EC2 instances
    - if we stop the instance, the data is kept on the disk (with EBS) and will
      remain o the disk until the EC2 instance started
    - if we terminate the instance, by default the root volume will also be terminated
  When our EC2 instance is started:
    - OS boots up
    - user data script is run (bootstrap scripts)
    - Applications start

  EC2 Hibernation
    - when your hibernate an EC2 instance, the operating system is told to perform hibernation
      (suspend-to-disk).
    - save the contents from the instance memory (RAM) to your EBS root volume
    - it persists the EBS root volume and any attached EBS data volumes

  When your start your instance out of hibernation
    - the EBS root volume is restarted to its previous state
    - RAM contents are reloaded
    - the processes that were previously running on the instance are resumed
    - previously attached data volumes are reattached and the instance retains its instance ID

  EC2 Hibernation in Action
    EC2 Hibernation
      - instance boots much faster
      - OS does not need to reboot because the in-memory (RAM) is preserved
      - this is useful for:
         1. long-running processes
         2. services that take time to initialize

  Exam Tips

    What to Know about EC2 hibernation
      - EC2 hibernation preserves the in-Memory RAM on persistent storage (EBS)
      - much faster to boot up because you do not need to reload the OS
      - Instance RAM must be less than 150 GB
      - supporting instance families include: c3, c4, c5, m3, m4, r3, r4, & r5
      - available for Windows, Amazon Linux 2 AMI, and Ubuntu
      - instances cannot be hibernated for more than 60 days
      - available for on-demand instances and reserved instances

---------------------------------------------------
6.5 EFS Overview

  What is EFS (Elastic File System)
     - managed NFS (Network File system) that can be mounted on many EC2 instances
     - works with EC2 instances in multiple AZs
     - highly available and scalable. However, it is expensive

  Use Cases
    Content Management
      - Great fit for content management systems, as you can easily share content between EC2 instances
        (e.g. wordpress logs)
    Web Server
       - great fit for web servers. Have just a single folder structure for you website

  Overview
    - uses NFSv4 protocol
    - compatible with Linux-based AMI (Windows not support at this time)
    - encyption at reset using KMS
    - Files system scales automatically; no capacity planning required
    - pay per use

  Performance
    - 1000s concurrent connections
    - 10Gbps throughput
    - petabytes scaling

  Controlling Performance
    - when creating an EFS, you can set what performance you want
    General Purpose
      - used for things like web servers, CMS (content management systems - used build and manage web server), etc
    Max I/O
      - used for big data, media processing, etc

  Storage Tiers
    - comes with storage tiers and lifecycle management, allowing you to move your data from one
      tier to another after X number of days
    Standard storage tier
      - for frequently accessed files
    Infrequently Accessed storage tier
      - for infrequently accessed files

  Console Demo
   -> EFS -> create files system ->
       -> have regional or one-zone options
       -> enable backup enabled by default unless you pick customized
          -> customized lets: disable backups, set/disable lifecycle management, set performance options
             (bursting or Enhanced), set performance mode (General Purpose or Max I/O)
  Exam Tips
    EFS
      - supports the NFS version 4 (NFSv4) protocol
      - only pay for the storage you use (no pre-provisioning required)
      - can scale up to petabytes
      - can support thousands of concurrent NFS (EC2) connections
      - Data is store across multiple AZs within a region
      - Read-after-write consisteny

     if you have a scenario-based question around highly scalable shared storage using NFS, think EFS
---------------------------------------------------
6.6 FSx Overview

  FSx for Windows
    - a fully managed native Microsoft Windows File System
    - AWS FSx is built on Windows Server
    - scenarios such as Microsoft sharepoint migratition to AWS,
      think FSx for Windows
      Note: Sharepoint is used for Website data storage

  FSx for Windows vs EFS
     FSx for Windows
       - a managed Windows Server that runs Windows Server Message Block (SMB)
         based file services
       - Designed for Windows and Windows applications
       - Supports AD (Active Directory) users, access control lists, group,
         and security policies, along with Distributed File System (DFS)
         namespaces and replication
     EFS
       - a managed NAS (Network Attached Storage)  filer for EC2 instances based
         on Network File System (NFS) version 4
       - NFS: one of the first network file sharing protocols native to Unix and Linux

  FSx for Lustre
    - a fully managed file system that is optimized for compute intensive workloads
    - only supports Linux (Not Windows)
    - use cases:
       - high-performance computing
       - machine learning
       - media data processing workloads
       - EDA
  FSx For Lustre Performance
    - you can launch and run a lustre file system that can process massive datasets at
      to hundreds of gigabytes per sec of throughput, millions of IOPS, and sub-milisecond
      latencies

  Exam Tips
     EFS vs FSx for Windows vs FSx for Lustre
        EFS: when you need distributed, highly resilent storage for Linux instances
             and linux based applications
        FSx for Windows: When you need centralized storage for Windows-based applications,
             such as Sharepoint, Microsoft SQL server, Workspaces, IIS Web Server, or any
             native Microsoft application
             Note: IIS (Internet Information Services) Web Server is a Microsoft created webserver
        FSx for Lustre: When you need high-speed, high-capacity, distributed storage. This
             will be for applications that do high performance computing (HPC), financial
             modeling, etc. Remember that FSx for Lustre, when linked to an S3 bucket (data lake), it
             can access data directly on S3 (NOT store, but it may be export changes, deletion back to S3)

---------------------------------------------------
6.7 Amazon Machine Images (AMI): EBS vs Instance Storage

  What is an AMI
    - AMI provides the information required to launch an instance
    - you must specify an AMI when you launch an instance

  5 things you can base your AMI on
    - region
    - Operating System
    - Architecture (32-bit or 64-bit)
    - Launch permissions
    - Storage for root device (root device volume)
  EBS vs Instance Store
    - all AMIs are categorized as either backed by EBS or Instance store
    For EBS:
      - the root device for an instance launched from the AMI is EBS volume created from
        and EBS snapshot
    For Instance store:
      - the root device for an instance launched from the AMI is an instance store volume created
        from a template stored in the S3
  Instance Store Volumes
    - ephemeral (temporary) storage
    - instance store volumes cannot be stopped
    - if the underlying host fails, you lose your data
    - you can, however, reboot the instance without losing your data
    - if you delete the instance, you will lose the instance store volume
  EBS Volumes
    - EBS-backed instances can be stopped
    - You will not lose  the data on this if it is stopped
    - you can also reboot an EBS volume and not lose your data
    - by default, the root device volume will be deleted on termination. However, you
      can tell AWS to keep the root device volume with EBS volumes
  Console Demo
      -> launch instance -> more AMI options -> Community AMIs -> Refine filter -> select instance store
       -> select OS: Amazon Linux (no AL2) -> instance type c1.medium (note: few choices c1,c2,c3,m1,m2,m3, ...)
       -> volume: 350GB HDD option

  Exam Tips

    Instance Store Volumes
      - ephemeral (temporary) storage
      - instance store volumes cannot be stopped
      - if the underlying host fails, you lose your data
      - you can, however, reboot the instance without losing your data
      - if you delete the instance, you will lose the instance store volume
    EBS Volumes
      - EBS-backed instances can be stopped
      - You will not lose the data on this if it is stopped
      - you can also reboot an EBS volume and not lose your data
      - by default, the root device volume will be deleted on termination. However, you
        can tell AWS to keep the root device volume with EBS volumes
    AMI
      - just a blueprint for an EC2 instance

---------------------------------------------------
6.9 AWS Backup

  What is AWS backup
    - allows you to consolidate your backups across multiple AWS services such as
      EC2, EBS, EFS, FSx for Lustre, FSx for Windows File Server, and AWS Storage Gateway
    - it can include other services, such as database technologies like RDS and DynamoDB

  AWS Backup with Organizations
    - backup can be used with AWS Organizations to backup multiple AWS accounts in your
      organziation
    - gives you centralized control accross all AWS services, in multiple AWS accounts
      across the entire AWS organization

  Benefits of AWS Backup
    Central Management
      - use a single, central backup console, allowing you to centralize your backups across
        multiple AWS services and multiple AWS accounts
    Automation
      - can create automated backup schedules and retention policies
      - can create lifecycle policies, allowing you to expire unnecessary backups after a
        period of time
    Improved Compliance
      - Backup polices can be enforced while backups can be encrypted both at reset and in
        transit, allowing alignment to regulatory compliance.
        - Auditing is made easy due to a consolidated view of backups across many AWS services


  Exam Tips

    AWS Backup
      Consolidation: Use AWS backup to back up AWS Services, such as EC2, EBS, EFS,
          FSx for Lustre, FSx for Windows File Server, and AWS Storage Gateway
      Organizations: You can use AWS Organizations in conjunction with AWS Backup to backup
          you different AWS services across multiple AWS accounts
      Benefits: Backup gives you centralized control, letting you automate your backup and
          define lifecycle policies of your data. You get better compliance, as you can enforce
          your backup policies, ensure your backups are encrypted, and audit them once completed

---------------------------------------------------
6.10 EBS Exam Tips

    EBS:
      - highly available and scalable storage volumes that you can attach to an EC2 instance

    EBS SSD Volume types
      General Purpose SSD (gp2)
        - suitable for boot disks and general applications that are not latency sensitve
        - 16K IOPS per volume
        - up to 99.9 durability
      General Purpose SSD (gp3)
        - suitable for high perfomance applications
        - Predicatable 3K IOPS baseline perfomrance and 125 Mb/s regardless of volume size
        - up to 99.9 durability
      Provisioned IOPS SSD (io1)
        - suitable for OLTP (On-Line Transaction Processing) and latency-sensitive applications
        - 50 IOPS per GB, up to 64K IOPS per volume
        - use if you need more than 16K IOPS
        - up to 99.9 durability
        - used for High perfomance and most expensive
      Provisioned IOPS SSD (io2)
        - suitable for OLTP (On-Line Transaction Processing) and latency-sensitive applications
        - 500 IOPS per GB, up to 64K IOPS
        - 99.999 durability instead of up to 99.9
        - latest generation Provisioned IOPS volume
        - io2 is the same price as io1

    EBS HDD Volume types
      Throughput Optimized HDD (st1)
        - use cases: big data, data warehousing, ETL (Extract/Tranform/Load), and log processing
        - max throughput of 500 Mb/s per volume
        - cannot be a boot volume
        - up to 99.9 durability
      Cold HDD (sc1)
        - max throughput of 250 Mb/s per volume
        - used for less frequently accessed data
        - low cost options
        - cannot be a boot volume
        - up to 99.9 durability
        - lowest cost option

      EBS Volumes and snapshots
        - volumes exist on EBS, where as snapshots exist on S3
        - you can resize EBS volumes on the fly as well as changing the volume types

      Snapshots
        - snapshots exists on S3
        - snapshots are at a point in time
           - a copy of the virtual disk / volume at a point in time
        - Snapshots are incremental
        - the 1st snapshot
          - it may take some time to create as there is no previous point-in-time copy

      Sharing snapshots
         - you can share snapshots, but only in the region in which they were created
         - to share to other regions, you will need to copy them to the destination region first

      Instance Store Volumes
        - ephemeral (temporary) storage
        - instance store volumes cannot be stopped
        - if the underlying host fails, you lose your data
        - you can, however, reboot the instance without losing your data
        - if you delete the instance, you will lose the instance store volume

      EBS Volumes
        - EBS-backed instances can be stopped
        - You will not lose the data on this if it is stopped
        - you can also reboot an EBS volume and not lose your data
        - by default, the root device volume will be deleted on termination. However, you
          can tell AWS to keep the root device volume with EBS volumes
      AMI
        - just a blueprint for an EC2 instance

      Encrypted Volumes
        - data at rest is encrypted inside the volume
        - data in flight moving between instance and volume is encrypted
        - all snapshots [created for encrypted volumes] are encrypted
        - all volumes created for encrypted snapshots are encrypted

      How to encrypt exiting Volumes that are unencrypted
        - create a snapshot of the unencrypted root device volume
        - create a copy of the snapshot and select the encrypt option
        - Create an AMI from the encrypted snapshot
        - Use encrypted AMI to launch new encrypted instances

      What to Know about EC2 hibernation
        - EC2 hibernation preserves the in-Memory RAM on persistent storage (EBS)
        - much faster to boot up because you do not need to reload the OS
        - Instance RAM must be less than 150 GB
        - supporting instance families include: c3, c4, c5, m3, m4, r3, r4, & r5
        - available for Windows, Amazon Linux 2 AMI, and Ubuntu
        - instances cannot be hibernated for more than 60 days
        - available for on-demand instances and reserved instances

      EFS
        - supports the NFS version 4 (NFSv4) protocol
        - only pay for the storage you use (no pre-provisioning required)
        - can scale up to petabytes
        - can support thousands of concurrent NFS (EC2) connections
        - Data is store across multiple AZs within a region
        - Read-after-write consisteny

        - if you have a scenario-based question around highly scalable shared storage using NFS, think EFS

     EFS vs FSx for Windows vs FSx for Lustre
        EFS: when you need distributed, highly resilent storage for Linux instances
             and linux based applications
        FSx for Windows: When you need centralized storage for Windows-based applications,
             such as Sharepoint, Microsoft SQL server, Workspaces, IIS Web Server, or any
             native Microsoft application
             Note: IIS (Internet Information Services) Web Server is a Microsoft created webserver
        FSx for Lustre: When you need high-speed, high-capacity, distributed Linux storage. This
             will be for applications that do high performance computing (HPC), financial
             modeling, etc. Remember that FSx for Lustre can store data directly on S3

     Storage Options Use Cases
        S3
         - used for serverless object storage
        Glacier
          - used for archiving objects
        EFS
          - Network File System (NFS) for Linux instances. Centralized storage solution across
            multiple AZs
        FSx for Lustre
          - File storage for high performance computing Linxu file systems
        EBS Volumes
          - persistent storage for EC2 instances
        Instance Store
          - ephemeral storage for EC2 instances
        FSx for Windows
          - File storage for Windows instances. Centralized storage solution across multiple AZs


     EBS Multi-Attach
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/ebs-volumes-multi.html
      - EBS Multi-Attach enables you to attach a single Provisioned IOPS SSD (io1 or io2) volume to
        multiple instances that are in the same Availability Zone.
      - can be attached to up to 16 Linux instances built on the Nitro System that are in the same AZ.
      - You can attach a volume that is Multi-Attach enabled to Windows instances, but the operating system
        does not recognize the data on the volume that is shared between the instances, which can result in
        data inconsistency.
      - Multi-Attach is supported exclusively on Provisioned IOPS SSD (io1 and io2) volumes.
      - region and other limitations

     AWS Backup
       Consolidation: Use AWS backup to back up AWS Services, such as EC2, EBS, EFS,
           FSx for Lustre, FSx for Windows File Server, and AWS Storage Gateway
       Organizations: You can use AWS Organizations in conjunction with AWS Backup to backup
           you different AWS services across multiple AWS accounts
       Benefits: Backup gives you centralized control, letting you automate your backup and
           define lifecycle policies of your data. You get better compliance, as you can enforce
           your backup policies, ensure your backups are encrypted, and audit them once completed

6.12 AWS Certified Solutions Architect - Elastic Block Storage (EBS) and Elastic File System (EFS) Quiz

Question 2
True or False? Amazon FSx for Lustre can't store data directly on Amazon S3.
Choices:
 - False
 - True

The statement is not true because Amazon FSx for Lustre file systems CAN be linked to Amazon S3 buckets.
Correct Answer

Amazon FSx for Lustre file systems can be linked to Amazon S3 buckets, allowing you to access and process data concurrently from both
a high-performance file system and from the S3 API.


Question 3
What type of storage does EFS offer?
 - Object
 - Block
 - File
 - System

Block-level storage solutions are provided by EBS, rather than EFS. While EBS can be set up to be used
with any kind of operating system, EFS can only be used natively with Linux-based operating systems.
It can also be helpful to remember the acronym of EFS as the 'Elastic File Sytem'. Reference Documentation:
When to Choose Amazon EFS

Correct answer:
EFS is a file-level storage solution. Reference Documentation: When to Choose Amazon EFS


Question 5
You've been tasked with creating a highly performant database on your EC2 instance. Which type of EBS volume will support the high level of IOPS that you require?
Choices:
 - Cold HDD
 - General Purpose
 - Throughput Optimized
 - Provisioned IOPS

Correct Answer
Provisioned IOPS would be the best option in this instance. Reference Documentation: Amazon EBS Provisioned IOPS Volume

Note: Provisioned IOS includes SSD io1 & io2, Throughput Optimized is HDD st1, Cold HDD is sc1, and General Purpose are SDD gp1 & gp2


Question 5
What is the purpose of an Amazon Machine Image (AMI)?
Choices:
 - It's a type of database structure that can be deployed to RDS.
 - It provides you with an always-up-to-date OS for your EC2 instance.
 - It's a type of EC2 instance that you'll select during creation of an instance.
 - It's a template to create a new EC2 instance from.

Correct Answer:
 - It's a template to create a new EC2 instance from.

AMIs are templates for EC2 instances. Reference Documentation:
   https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/AMIs.html

Chapter 7 Databases
---------------------------------------------------
7.1 Relational Databases Service (RDS) Overview

  Relational Databases
   Tables
     - data is organized in tables
     Rows: stores the data items
     Columns: The field in the database

  Relational Database Engines with RDS
    SQL Server
    Oracle
    MySQL
    PostgreSQL
    MariaDB
    AWS Auroa

  RDS Advantages
    - up and running in minutes
    - multi-AZ (e.g. primary in one AZ and backup in another AZ)
    - Failover capability
    - Automated backups
    - A manual install of a database in your own data center could take 8 days or longer

  When to use an RDS database
    - generally used for online transaction processing (OLTP) workloads

  OLTP (OnLine Transaction Processing) and OLAP (OnLine Analytical Processing)
    OLTP
      - processes data from transactions in real time (e.g. customers orders, backing transactions,
        payments, and booking systems)
    OLAP
      - processes complex queries to analyze historical data (e.g. analyzing net profit figures from the
        past 3 yrs and sales forecasting)
      - OLAP is all about data analysis using large amounts of data, as well as complex queries that
        take a long to complete

   OLAP Example
     Net Profit Analyze: you been asked to produce a report comparing net profits for car sales in 3
       different regions
         large amount of data
             - sum of cars sold in each region
             - unit cost for each region
             - sales price of each car
         Analysis, not transactions
           - RDS is NOT suitable for analyzing large amount of data
           - use a data warehouse like Redshift, which is optimized for OLAP

  Multi-AZ RDS
    - with Multi-AZ, RDS creaates an exact copy of your production database in another AZ.
    - primary in one AZ and backup/standby in another AZ)
    - AWS handles the replication for you
    - when your write your production database, this write will automatically synchronize
      to the standby database

   Which RDS types can be configure as Multi-AZ:
      SQL Server, Oracle, MySQL, PostgreSQL, & MariaDB
      Note: Auroa is always Multi-AZ

  RDS Unplanned Failures and Maintenance
    - load balancer connects RDS database instance using a "connection string" (a web address for DB)
    - if RDS detects a failure, RDS will automatically point the web address to the standby DB (DNS failover)
    - RDS will automatically fail over to the standby during a failure so database operations
      can resume quickly without administrative intervention

  RDS Multi-AZ is for disaster recovery
     - it is not for improving performance
     - you cannot connect to the standby when the primary database is active
     - you can improve performance using read replicas (covered in another section)
     -
  RDS Multi-AZ Cluster
     - has two readable standby DB instances in different AZs (instead of one)

  IAM database authentication
    - works with MariaDB, MySQL, and PostgreSQL.
    - With this authentication method, you don't need to use a password when you connect to a DB instance.
      Instead, you use an authentication token.
    - Use authentication tokens which a unique string of characters that RDS generates on request. Authentication
      tokens are generated using AWS Signature Version 4. Each token has a lifetime of 15 minutes.
    - consider using IAM database authentication when your applications create fewer than 200 connections per second

  IAM database authentication provides the following benefits
    - Network traffic to and from the database is encrypted using SSL or TLS
    - You can use IAM to centrally manage access to your database resources, instead of managing access
      individually on each DB instance
    - For applications running on Amazon EC2, you can use profile credentials specific to your EC2 instance
      to access your database instead of a password, for greater security.

  Console Demo
    -> RDS -> create database -> select "MySQL", select MySQL version,
      template options
         - Production -> by default, multi-AZ DB cluster
         - Dev/Test
         - Free Tier (singel AZ)
       -> select EC2 instance type
       -> select EBS storage type and size
       -> include auto storage scaling
       -> enter password
       -> create

     After it creates, it provides
      endpoint and port 3306
       -> to connect, using endpoint:port along with username and password to log in and start create
          database

     After it is created, you can select DB -> Modify -> change property

  Exam Tips

    RDS Database Types:
       SQL Server, Oracle, MySQL, PostgreSQL, MariaDB, & Auroa

    RDS is for OLTP (OnLine Transaction Processing) workloads
      - great for processing lots of small transactions like customers orders, backing transactions,
        payments, and booking systems
        - not suitable for OLAP (OnLine Analytical Processing) workloads
           - use a data warehouse like Redshift, which is optimized for OLAP

---------------------------------------------------
7.2 Increasing Read Performance with Read Replicas

  Read Replicas:
  https://aws.amazon.com/rds/faqs/#Read_Replicas

  Read replicas, Multi-AZ, and multi-region deployments:
  https://aws.amazon.com/rds/features/read-replicas/#Read_replicas.2C_Multi-AZ_deployments.2C_and_multi-region_deployments

  What is a Read Replica
    - a read replica is a read-only copy of you primary database
    - great for read-heavy workloads and takes the load off you primary database
    - read-replica can be cross-AZ or cross-region
    - can create read-replicas with AWS console or CreateDBInstanceReadReplica API
    - each read replica has its own DNS (Domain Name System) endpoint
    - read-replicas can be promoted to be their own database
       - this breaks the replication

   Notes:
    - main purpose for multi-region is disaster recovery and local performance
    - main purpose for read replicas is scalability
    - main purpose for multi-AZ is deployments is availability
    - However, you may use a read replica for disaster recovery of the source DB
      instance either in the same AWS region or ain another region

  Read Replica Key Facts
    - about scaling read performance
      - primary used for scaling, not for disaster recovery
    - Requires automatic backup
      - automatic backup must be enabled in order to deploy a read replica
    - multiple read replicas are supported
      - MySQL, MariaDB, & PostgreSQL allow you to create up to 15 read replicas for a given source DB instance
      - Oracle and SQL allow you to create up to 5 read replicas for a given source DB instance

  Console Demo
    -> create RDS MySQL database
    -> create read replica of the MySQL database

  Exam Tips

       RDS Multi-AZ
         - an exact copy of your production database in another AZ
         - used for disaster recovery
         - in the event of a failure, RDS will automatically fail over to the standby instance

       RDS read-replicas
         - a read-only copy of your primary database in the same AZ, cross-AZ, or cross-region
         - used to increase or scale read performance
         - great for read-heavy workloads and takes the load off you primary database
           for read-only workloads (e.g. Business intelligence reporting jobs)

---------------------------------------------------
7.3 What is Amazon Aurora

  What is Aurora
    - a MySQL and PostgreSQL-compatible relational database engine that combines the speed
      and availability of high-end commercial databases with the simplicity and
      cost-effectiveness of an open-source databases

  Aurora performance
    - 5X better performance than MySQL and 3x better than PostgreSQL databases at a
      much lower price point, while delivering similar performance and availability

  Aurora Basics
    - starts with 10GB. Scales in 10GB increments to 128TB (storage auto scaling)
    - compute resources can scale up to 96 vCPUs and 768 GB of memory
    - 2 copies of you data are contained in each AZ, with a minimum of 3 AZs. 6 copies of your data

  Aurora Scaling
    - designed to transparently handle the loss of up to 2 copies of data without affecting
      database write availability and up to 3 copies without affecting read availability
    - Aurora storage is self-healing.
      - data blocks and disks are continuously scanned for errors and repaired automatically

  3 Types of Aurora Replicas
    - Aurora replicas
      - you can currently have 15 read replicas with Aurora
    - Aurora MySQL Replicas
      - you can currently have 5 read replicas with Aurora
    - Aurora PostgreSQL Replicas
      - you can currently have 5 read replicas with Aurora

  Aurora Backups
    - automated backups are always enabled on Aurora DB instances
    - Backups do not impact database performance
    - you can share aurora snapshots with other AWS accounts

  Aurora Serverless
    - an on-demand, auto-scaling configuration for the MySQL-compatible and PostgreSQL-compatible
      editions of Aurora
    - An aurora serverless DB cluster automatically starts up, shuts down, and scales capacity
      up or down based on your application's need
    - if exam question is what to use for spiky RDS workloads, use Aurora serverless

  Exam Tips

   Aurora Exam Tips
     - 2 copies of you data are contained in each AZ, with a minimum of 3 AZs. 6 copies of your data
     - you can share aurora snapshots with other AWS accounts
     - 3 types of Aurora replicas available: Aurora replicas, MySQL replicas, & Postgre SQL replicas
     - Automated failover is only available with Aurora replicas
     - Aurora has automated backups turned on by default
       - you can take snapshots with Aurora
       - you can share these snapshots with other AWS accounts

    Aurora Serverless
      - uses Aurora serverless if you want relatively simple, cost-effective option for infrequent,
        intermittent, or unpredicable workloads

---------------------------------------------------
7.4 DynamoDB Overview

  DynamoDB FAQ:
  https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/faqs/

  New DynamoDB Standard-Infrequent Access table class
  https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2021/12/amazon-dynamodb-standard-infrequent-access-table-class/

  What is DynamoDB
    - DynamoDB is a fast and flexible NoSQL database service for all applications that need
      consistent, single-digit millisec latency at any scale
    - a fully managed database and support both document and key-value data models
    - flexible data model and reliable performance make it a great fit for mobile, web,
      gaming, ad-tech, IoT, and many other applications

  High-Level DynamoDB
    - stored on SSD storage
    - spread across 3 geographically distinct data centers
    - eventually consistent reads (default)
    - optionally, strongly consistent reads

  Read Consistency
    Eventually consistent reads
      - consistency across all copies of the reached within a second
      - repeating a a read after a short time should return the updated data
      - Best read peformance
    Strongly consistent reads
      - returns a result that reflects all writes that receive a successful response
        prior to the read

  DynamoDB Accelerator (DAX)
    - fully managed, highly available, in-memory cache
    - 10x performance improvement
    - reduces request time from milliseconds to microsecond - even under load
    - no need for developers to manage caching logic
    - compatible with DynamoDB API calls

  On-Demand Capacity
    - pay-per-request pricing
    - balance cost and performance
    - no minimum capacity
    - pay more per request than with provisioned capacity
    - use for new product launches

  Security
    - Encryption at reset using KMS
    - site-to-site connections:
       - site-to-site VPN
       - Direct Connect (DX)
    - works with IAM policies and roles which allows to have Fine-grained access
    - integrates with CloudWatch and CloudTrail
    - integrates with VPC endpoints (allows you to directly communicate with DynamoDB without
      leaving the AWS backbone network)

  Exam Tips

    4 Facts about DynamoDB
      - stored on SSD storage
      - spread across 3 geographically distinct data centers
      - eventually consistent reads (default)
      - optionally, strongly consistent reads

    Read Consistency
      Eventually consistent reads
        - consistency across all copies of the reached within a second
        - repeating a a read after a short time should return the updated data
        - Best read peformance
      Strongly consistent reads
        - returns a result that reflects all writes that receive a successful response
          prior to the read

---------------------------------------------------
7.5 When Do We Use DynamoDB Transactions

  Amazon DynamoDB transactions
  https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/aws/new-amazon-dynamodb-transactions/

  ACID (Atomic Consistent Isolated Durable) Diagram
    Atomic
      - all changes to the data must be performed successfully or not at all
    Consistent
      - data must be in a consistent data before and after the transaction
    Isolated
      - no other process can change the data while the transaction is running
    Durable
      - the changes made by a transction must persist

  ACID with DynamoDB
     - DynamoDB transactions provide developers atomicity, consistency, isolation,
       and durability (ACID) across 1 or more tables within a single AWS account and region
     - you can use transactions when building applications that require coordinated inserts,
       deletes, or updates to multiple items as part of a single logical business operation

  All or Nothing
    ACID basically means all or nothing
    - All: transaction succeeds across 1 or more tables
    - Nothing: whole transaction fails

  Use Cases DynamoDB Transactions
     - processing financial transactions
     - fulfilling and managing orders
     - building multiplayer game engines
     - coordinating acrosss distributed components and services

  When do we use DynamoDB Transactions
    - Multiple "all-or-nothing" operations
    - Financial transactions
    - fulfilling orders
    - 3 options for reads: eventual consistency, strong consistency, and transactional
    - 2 options for writes: standard and transactional
    - up to 25 items or 4MB of data

  Exam Tips

    DynamoDB Transactions
      - if you see any scenario question that mentions ACID requirement, think
        DynamoDB transactions
      - DynamoDB transactions provide developers atomicity, consistency, isolation,
        and durability (ACID) across 1 or more tables within a single AWS account and region
      - all-or-nothing transactions

---------------------------------------------------
7.6 Saving Your Data with DynamoDB Backups

  On-demand backup and restore
    - full backups at any time
    - zero impact on table performance or availability
    - consistent within seconds and retained until deleted
    - operates within the same region as the source table

  Point-in-Time Recovery (PITR)
   - projects against accidental writes or deletes
   - restore to any point in the last 35 days
   - performed using incremental backups
   - not enabled by default
   - latest restoreable: 5 min in the past

  Exam Tips
    - can do (on-demand) manual backups of dynamoDB at any time
    - can turn on Point-in-Time Recovery (PITR)
       - PITR to any point in last 35 days
       - latest retorable: 5 min in the past

---------------------------------------------------
7.7 Taking Your Data Global with DynamoDB streams and Global Tables

  DynamoDB Streams
    - time-ordered sequence of item-level changes in a table
      - first-in, first-out streams of your data
    - DynamoDB streams stored for 24 hour
    - streams are broken up in to shards
      - shards are a bunch of data with sequential sequence numbers
    - everytime you make a change to dynamoDB database, these changes comes with a sequence number
      - changes are inserts, updates, & deletes
      - these sequences are stored in dynamoDB streams
    - can combine with Lambda functions for functionality like stored procedures

  DynamoDB Global Tables
     Managed multi-master, multi-region replication
       - great for globally distributed applications
       - based on DynamoDB streams
       - need dynamoDB streams to be turned on to enable Global Tables
       - multi-region redundancy for disaster recovery or high availability
       - built-in to DynamoDB, no application rewrite needed
       - replication latency under 1 sec in most cases

  Console Demo
    -> Databases -> DynamoDB -> Create Table -> Table Name xxx, partition key: user_id -> create table
    -> can enter data
       -> create item
          -> either Form or json
             -> create user_id value -> save item
             -> edit item -> add new attributes -> save change

   -> select xxx table,
      -> select "Gobal Table" tab -> Create Replica -> Choose region: London -> create

      -> new item to primary table
      -> verify new item is in Londom replica
      -> clean-up
         -> delete London Replica
         -> delete N Virginia xxx table (after replica delete has completed)


  Exam Tips
    DynamoDB Global Tables
      Managed Multi-Master, Multi-Region replication
       - great for globally distributed applications
       - based on DynamoDB streams
       - need dynamoDB streams to be turned on to enable Global Tables
       - multi-region redundancy for disaster recovery or high availability
       - built-in to DynamoDB, no application rewrite needed
       - no application re-rewrite because it is built-in to DynamoDB
       - replication latency under 1 sec in most cases

7.8 Operating MongoDB-Compatible Databases in AWS DocumentDB

  What is MongoDB
    - NoSQL database that stores using Documents model
    - a document is record in a document databaes
      - A document typically stores information about one object and any of its related metadata
      - documents store data in field-value pairs
    - a collection is a group of documents. Collections typically store documents that
      have similar content

    - documentDB that allows for scalability and flexibility with your data as well as
     robust querying and indexing features

  document Example

    {
       "_id": 1,
       "first_name": "Tom",
       "email": "tom@example.com",
       "cell": "765-555-5555",
       "likes": [
          "fashion",
          "spas",
          "shopping"
       ],
       "businesses": [
          {
             "name": "Entertainment 1080",
             "partner": "Jean",
             "status": "Bankrupt",
             "date_founded": {
                "$date": "2012-05-19T04:00:00Z"
             }
          },
          {
             "name": "Swag for Tweens",
             "date_founded": {
                "$date": "2012-11-01T04:00:00Z"
             }
          }
       ]
    }

  What is Amazon DocumentDB
    - allows you to run MongoDB on the AWS cloud
    - its a managed database service that scales with your workloads and safely and durably
      stores your database information

  Why use Amazon DocumentDB
    - no longer have to worry about all the manual tasks when running MongoDB workloads, such
      as cluster management software, configuring backups, or monitoring production workloads
    - get rid of you operational overheads

  Exam Tips
    - A typical question might be about moving your MongoDB database to AWS
       e.g. mongoDB on-premise -> use "AWS database Migration Services" -> use "Amazon DocumentDB" database
    - For scenario questions where they talk about migrating MongoDB from on-premise to AWS,
      think "Amazon DocumentDB"

7.9 Running Apache Cassandra workloads with Amazon Keyspaces

  What is Casandra
    - distributed database (e.g it runs on many machines) that uses open-source NoSQL data storage.
    - it is primarily used for big data solutions
    - Enterprises, such as Netflix, use Cassandra on their backend

  What is Keyspaces
    - AWS Apache Cassandra database services
    - It allows you to run Cassandra workloads on AWS and is a fully managed database service

  Why use Keyspaces
    - It is a fully managed database service - you don't need to worry about managing servers,
      software, patching, etc.
    - keyspaces is serverless
    - you pay only for the resources you use, and the service can automatically scale tables up
      and down in response to your applications

  Exam Tips
     - a scenario question about migrating a big data Cassandra cluster to AWS - think Amazon Keyspaces

7.10 Implementing Graph Databases Using Amazon Neptune

  What is a Graph Database
    - purpose-built to store and navigate relationships
      - essentially, stores relationship graphs
    - stores nodes and relationships instead of tables or documents

  Graph Database Use Cases
    - fraud detection.
    - recommendation Engines

  Neptune
    - Neptune is Amazon's graph database service
    - a fast, reliable, fully managed graph database service that makes it easy to build
      and run applications

  Neptune Use Cases
    build connections between identities
      - easily build identity graphs for identity resolution solutions such as social graphs, and
        accelerated updates for ad targeting, personalization, and analytics
    build Knowledge Graphs Applications between identities
      - add topical data to product catalog, mode general information, and help users quickly
        navigate highly connected datasets
    Detect Fraud Patterns
      - build graph queries for near real-time identity fraud pattern detections in financial and
        purchase transactions
    Security Graphs to Improve IT Security
      - proactively detect and investigate IT infrastructure using a layered security approach
      - Visualize all infrastructure to plan, predict, and mitigate risk

  Exam Tips
    - Neptune does not come up much, and when it comes up, it is often used as a distractor
    - Just know the that Neptune is a fully managed AWS graph database

7.11 Leveraging Amazon Quantum Ledger Database (QLDB) for Ledger Databases

  What is a ledger Database
    - a NoSQL database that is immutable, transparent, and has cryptographically verifiable
      transaction log that is by owned by one authority
    - You cannot update a record (e.g. replace old content) in a ledger database. Instead
      and update adds a new record to the database
  Ledger Database Use Cases
    - it is used for cryptocurrencies, such as Bitcoin, Ethereum, etc
    - it is used by shipping companies to track items, boxes, shipping containers, deliveries, etc.
    - pharmaceutical companies use it to track creation and distribution of drugs and ensure
      no counterfeits are produced

  Amazon Quantum Ledger Database (QLDB)
    - a fully managed ledger database that provides a transparent, immutable, and cryptographically
      verifiable transaction log

  QLDB Use Cases
    Store financial transactions
      - Create a complete and accurate record of all financial transactions, such as credit
        and debit transactions
    Reconcile Supply Chain Systems
      - record the history of each transaction, and provide details of every batch manufactured,
        shipped, stored, and sold fro facility to store
    Maintain Claims History
      - track a claim over its lifetime, and cryptographically verify data integrity to make
        the application resilient against data entry errors and manipulations
    Centralize Digital Records
      - Implement a system-of-record applications to create a complete, centralized record of
        employee details, such as payroll, bonus, and benefits

  Exam Tips

    QLDB (Quantum Ledger Database) is often used as a distractor
    - if the scenario is not talking about immutable databases, do not select Amazon QLDB
      as an answer.
      - you need to know what QLDB does only a very high level

    Amazon Quantum Ledger Database (QLDB)
      - a fully managed ledger database that provides a transparent, immutable, and cryptographically
        verifiable transaction log

7.12 Analyzing Time-Series Data with Amazon Timestream

  Time-Series Data
    - data points that logged over a series of time, allowing you to track your data
    - Examples could be temperature readings from weather stations around the world, on the
      hour, every hour for years

  Time-Series data Examples
    IoT
      - IoT sensors relay thousands, millions, and billions of points of information depending
        on the setup. One use case is agriculture
    Analytics
      - large websites such as Netflix server millions of users per second. Need to analyze
        incoming and outgoing web traffic
    DevOps Applications
      - Applications that change in response to users needs may need to be monitored continuously
        so they can scale correctly

  Amazon Timestream
    - A serverless, fully managed database service for time-series data
    - you can analyze trillions of events per day up to 1000 times faster and at as little as
      1/10th the cost of tranditional relational databases

  Exam Tips
    - a scenario question about where to store a large amount of time-series data for analysis,
      think of Timestream

7.13 Database Exam Tips

  RDS Exam Tips
    RDS Database Types:
       SQL Server, Oracle, MySQL, PostgreSQL, MariaDB, & Auroa

    RDS is for OLTP (OnLine Transaction Processing) workloads
      - great for processing lots of small transactions like customers orders, backing transactions,
        payments, and booking systems
        - not suitable for OLAP (OnLine Analytical Processing) workloads
           - use a data warehouse like Redshift, which is optimized for OLAP

   RDS Read Replicas - key Facts
    - about scaling read performance
      - primary used for scaling, not for disaster recovery
    - Requires automatic backup
      - automatic backup must be enabled in order to deploy a read replica
    - multiple read replicas are supported
      - MySQL, MariaDB, & PostgreSQL allow you to create up to 15 read replicas for a given source DB instance
      - Oracle and SQL allow you to create up to 5 read replicas for a given source DB instance

   RDS Multi-AZ and Read Replicas
       RDS Multi-AZ
         https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/aws/amazon-rds-multi-az-deployment/
         https://aws.amazon.com/rds/faqs/
         - used for disaster recovery
         - an exact copy of your production database in another AZ (hot standby)
         - Data written to the primary will be synchronously replicated to the standby.
         - If the primary fails, the standby becomes the primary and a new standby is created automatically.
         - in the event of a failure, RDS will automatically fail over to the standby instance
         - When failing over, Amazon RDS simply flips the canonical name record (CNAME) for your DB instance to
           point at the standby, which is in turn promoted to become the new primary
         - Typically, RDS Multi-AZ failovers complete within one to two minutes.
         -  Amazon RDS will emit a DB instance event to inform you that automatic failover occurred.

       RDS read-replicas
         - a read-only copy of your primary database in the same AZ, cross-AZ, or cross-region
         - used to increate or scale read performance
         - great for read-heavy workloads and takes the load off you primary database
           for read-only workloads (e.g. Business intelligence reporting jobs)

   Aurora Exam Tips

    - Aurora is AWS proprietary database compatible with MySQL and PostgreSQL

    Note: Aurora and Aurora Serverless are distinct products
      Aurora is "SQL database as a service" while Aurora Serverless is a "serverless SQL database service"

   Aurora Exam Tips - Key facts
     - 2 copies of you data are contained in each AZ, with a minimum of 3 AZs. 6 copies of your data
     - you can share aurora snapshots with other AWS accounts
     - 3 types of Aurora replicas available: Aurora replicas, MySQL replicas, & Postgre SQL replicas
     - Automated failover is only available with Aurora replicas
     - Aurora has automated backups turned on by default
       - you can take snapshots with Aurora
       - you can share these snapshots with other AWS accounts

     Aurora Serverless Use Case
     - uses Aurora serverless if you want relatively simple, cost-effective option for infrequent,
       intermittent, or unpredicable workloads

  DynamoDB Exam Tips
    What is DynamoDB
      - DynamoDB is a fast and flexible NoSQL database service for all applications that need
        consistent, single-digit millisec latency at any scale
      - a fully managed database and support both document and key-value data models
      - flexible data model and reliable performance make it a great fit for mobile, web,
        gaming, ad-tech, IoT, and many other applications

    4 Facts about DynamoDB
      - stored on SSD storage
      - spread across 3 geographically distinct data centers
      - eventually consistent reads (default)
      - optionally, strongly consistent reads

    Read Consistency
      Eventually consistent reads
        - consistency across all copies of the reached within a second
        - repeating a a read after a short time should return the updated data
        - Best read peformance
      Strongly consistent reads
        - returns a result that reflects all writes that receive a successful response
          prior to the read

    DynamoDB Transactions
      - multiple "all-or-nothing" transactions/operations
         - either need to update all the tables (write completes) or none (write fails)
      - financial transactions
      - fulfilling orders
      - 3 options for reads: eventual consistency, strong consistency, and transactional
      - 2 options for writes: standard and transactional
      - Up to 25 items for 4 MB of data
      - if you see any scenario question that mentions ACID requirement, think
        DynamoDB transactions
      - DynamoDB transactions provide developers atomicity, consistency, isolation,
        and durability (ACID) across 1 or more tables within a single AWS account and region

     DynamoDB on-demand Backup and Restore
       - can do (on-demand) manual backups of dynamoDB at any time
       - zero impact on table performance or availability
       - consistent within seconds and retained until deleted
       - operates in the same region as the source table

     DynamoDB Point-in-Time Recovery (PITR)
       - protects against accidental writes or deletes
       - PITR is not enabled by default
       - Restore to any point in last 35 days
       - latest retorable: 5 min in the past
       - Incremental backups

     DynamoDB Streams
       - time-ordered sequence of item-level changes in a table
         - first-in, first-out streams of your data
       - DynamoDB streams stored for 24 hour
       - streams are broken up in to shards
         - shards are a bunch of data with sequential sequence numbers
       - everytime you make a change to dynamoDB database, these changes comes with a sequence number
         - changes are inserts, updates, & deletes
         - these sequences are stored in dynamoDB streams
       - can combine with Lambda functions for functionality like stored procedures

     DynamoDB Global Tables
        Managed multi-master, multi-region replication
          - great for globally distributed applications
          - based on DynamoDB streams
          - need dynamoDB streams to be turned on to enable Global Tables
          - multi-region redundancy for disaster recovery or high availability
          - built-in to DynamoDB, no application rewrite needed
          - replication latency under 1 sec in most cases

  Operating MongoDB-Compatible Database in Amazon DocumentDB
    What is Amazon DocumentDB
      - allows you to run MongoDB on the AWS cloud
      - its a managed database service that scales with your workloads and safely and durably
        stores your database information
     Amazon DocumentDB Exam questions
       - A typical question might be about moving your MongoDB database to AWS
          e.g. mongoDB on-premise -> use "AWS database Migration Services" -> use "Amazon DocumentDB" database
       - For scenario questions where they talk about migrating MongoDB from on-premise to AWS,
         think "Amazon DocumentDB"

  AWS Keyspaces
    What is Keyspaces
      - AWS Apache Cassandra database services
      - It allows you to run Cassandra workloads on AWS and is a fully managed database service
     Keyspaces Exam questions:
       - a scenario question about migrating a big data Cassandra cluster to AWS - think Amazon Keyspaces

  AWS Neptune
    Neptune
      - Neptune is Amazon's graph database service
      - a fast, reliable, fully managed graph database service that makes it easy to build
        and run applications
    Neptune Exam questions:
      - Neptune does not come up much, and when it comes up, it is often used as a distractor
      - Just know the that Neptune is a fully managed AWS graph database

  Amazon Quantum Ledger Database (QLDB)
    What is Amazon Quantum Ledger Database (QLDB)
      - a fully managed ledger database that provides a transparent, immutable, and cryptographically
        verifiable transaction log

    QLDB Exam questions:
       - QLDB is often used as a distractor
         - if the scenario is not talking about immutable databases, do not select Amazon GLDB
           as an answer.
         - you need to know what QLDB does only a very high level

  Amazon Timestream
    What is Amazon Timestream
      - A serverless, fully managed database service for time-series data
      - you can analyze trillions of events per day up to 1000 times faster and at as little as
        1/10th the cost of tranditional relational databases

    Timestream Exam Tips
        - a scenario question about where to store a large amount of time-series data for analysis,
          think of Timestream

7.15 AWS Certified Solutinon Architect - Associate (SAA-C03) Databases Quiz

Q: Which of the following are benefits of using RDS Multi-AZ? (Choose 3).
Choose 3
  a. If your primary database fails, you'll automatically fail over to the secondary copy.
  b Backups are taken from a secondary copy of your data.
  c. All updates or maintenance changes are applied to a secondary database. Multi-AZ rollovers are typically automatic but can be manually initiated.
  d. For an RDS Multi-AZ deployment with two readable standbys, in the event of a failure, both remaining standbys can take over and serve the workload (writes) from the primary.

  Note: for d: In the event of a failure, only one of the two remaining standbys takes over the write operations.
 Correct Answer: a, b, c

Chapter 8 Virtual Private Cloud
---------------------------------------------------
8.1 VPC Overview

  VPC Introduction
    - VP is a virtual data center in the cloud
    - logically isolated part of AWS cloud where you can define your own network
    - Complete control of virtual network, including your IP address range, subnets,
      route tables, and network gateways
  Networking
    Fully customizable network
      - you can leverage multipe layers of security, including security groups and
        network access control lists, to help control access to EC2 instances in each subnet

    typical 3 tier setup
       Web tier
         - public facing subnet
       Application tier
         - private subnet. Can only speak to web tier and database tier
       Database tier
         - private subnet. Can only speak to application tier

  VPNs
    - Additionally, you create hardware Virtual Private Networks (VPN) connection between
      your corporate data center and your VPC and leverage the AWS Cloud as an extension of
      your corporate data center

     - largest range is: 10.0.0.0/16 (and network range for this course) (65k addreses
         10.0.0.0 to 10.0.255.255)
     - smallest AWS support range is 10.0.0.0/28  (16 address - 10.0.0.0 to 10.0.0.15)

  Networking Diagrams
     valid IP address ranges:
        10.0.0.0    - 10.255.255.255  (10/8 prefix) - most companies use
        172.16.0.0  - 172.31.255.255  (172.16/12 prefix)
        192.168.0.0 - 192.168.255.255 (192.168/16 prefix)

     VPC (acloudguruVPC) - 10.0.0.0/16
        [public subnet  (10.0.1.0/24) ec2 security group]  <- Network ACL <- Route tables <- router <- internet gateway
        [private subnet (10.0.2.0/24) ec2 security group]  <- Network ACL <- Route tables <-|
                                                                                            |--------- Virtual Private

  VPC Features
    What can we do with a VPC
      launch instances
        - launch instances into a subnet of your choosing
      Custom IP Addresses
        - assign custom IP address range in each subnet
      Route Tables
        - configure route tables between subnets
      Internet Gateway
        - Create internet gateway an attach it to our VPC
      More Control
        - much better security control over your AWS resources
      Access Control Lists
        - subnet network access control lists
     Network Access Contrl lists (NACLs)
        - can use to block specific IP addresses

Note: VPC DHCP (Dynamic Host Configuration Protocol) Option: DHCP option sets give you control over the
      following aspects of routing in your virtual network:
       - You can control the DNS servers, domain names, or Network Time Protocol (NTP) servers used by the
         devices in your VPC.
       - You can disable DNS resolution completely in your VPC

  Comparing VPCs
     Default VPC vs Custom VPC
       Default VPC:
         - Default VPC is user friendly
         - all subnets in default VPC have a route out to the internet
         - Each EC2 instance has both a public and private IP address
       Custom VPC:
         - fully customizable
         - takes time to set up

  VPC Exam Tips

    - VPC is a virtual data center in the cloud
    - Consists of internet gateways (or virtual private gateways), route tables,
      network access control lists, subnets, and security groups
    - 1 subnet is always in 1 AZ


---------------------------------------------------
8.2 Demo: Provisioning a VPC - Part 1
   -> can use VPC wizard to create a VPC, but you don't learn how
   -> default VPC
      - has 1 subnet per AZ
      - has it own route table

   -> Create VPC -> Name: acloudguruVPC IPv4 CIDR: 10.0.0.0/15 -> Create
     Note: This also created a route table, Network ACL, and a Security Group
       -> route table has route for VPC CIDR range, but no subnet associatations

    # Note:  public subnet
    -> Create subnets -> Name: 10.0.1.0/24 - us-east-1a, AZ: us-east-1a, VPC: acloudgurVPC
       CIDR Block: 10.0.1.0/24 -> create

       Note: Subnet shows 251 available IPv4 addresses instead of 256
          For example, in a subnet with CIDR block 10.0.1.0/24, the following five IP addresses
           are reserved:
              10.0.1.0: Network address.
              10.0.1.1: Reserved by AWS for the VPC router.
              10.0.1.2: Reserved by AWS. The IP address of the DNS server
              10.0.1.3: Reserved by AWS for future use.
              10.0.1.255: Network broadcast address. We do not support broadcast in a VPC,
                          therefore we reserve this address.


    # Note:  private subnet
    -> Create subnets -> Name: 10.0.2.0/24 - us-east-1b, AZ: us-east-1b, VPC: acloudgurVPC
       CIDR Block: 10.0.2.0/24 -> create

  Exam Tips
    VPC Demo
    - think of a VPC as a logical data center in AWS
    - Consists of internet gateways (or virtual private gateways), route tables,
      network access control lists, subnets, and security groups
    - 1 subnet is always in 1 AZ

  -> internet gateway -> create internet gateway -> Name: MyIGWACG -> create
