  Chapter 10: Elastic Load Balancing (ELB)

---------------------------------------------------
10.1 ELB Overview

  What is Elastic Load Balancing
    - automatically distributes incoming application traffic across multipe targets, such as
      EC2 instances.
    - this can be done across multiple AZs

  3 Types of Load Balancers
     Application Load Balancer
       - bested suited for load balancing of HTTP and HTTPS traffic
       - operates at Layer 7 and are application aware (e.g. routing HTTP and HTTPS based routings, content routing)
       - intelligent load balancing
     Network Load Balancer
       - operates at the connection level (layer 4)
       - are capable of handling millions of requests per sec, while maintaining ultra-low latenceis
       - performance load balancer
       - The static IP address that's assigned to a Network Load Balancer doesn't change and provides
         a fixed entry point for your Application Load Balancer.
     Classic Load Balancer
       - legacy load balancers
       - can load HTTP/HTTPS applications and use layer-7 specific features, such a X-Forwarded and
         stickly seessions
       - Classic/Test/Dev Load Balancer

  Health Checks
    - all AWS load balancers can be configured with Health checks
    - health checks periodically send requests to LB's registered instances to test their status
    - the status of instances that are healthy at the time of the health check are "InService"
    - the status of instances that are unhealthy at the time of the health check are "OutOfService"
    - LBs perform health checks on all registered instances, whether the instance is in a
      healthy state or an unhealthy state
    - LB routes requests only to the healthy instances
    - LB resumes routing requests to the instance when it has be restored to a healthy state

  ELB Exam Tips
  3 Types of Load Balancers
     Application Load Balancer
       - best suited for load balancing of HTTP and HTTPS traffic
       - operates at Layer 7 and are application aware
       - intelligent load balancing
     Network Load Balancer
       - operates at the transport layer (layer 4)
       - are capable of handling millions of requests per sec, while maintaining ultra-low latenceis
       - performance load balancer
       - can be assigned a static IP address (note: ALB cannot be assigned a static IP address)
     Classic Load Balancer
       - legacy load balancers
       - can load HTTP/HTTPS applications and use layer-7 specific features, such a X-Forwarded and
         stickly seessions
       - Classic/Test/Dev Load Balancer
    Gateway Load Balance (not use much - should not be on Exam)
      - layer 3 LB
      - used when you need to deploy and a fleet of 3rd party virtual appliances that support GENEVE

   Health Check
     - you can use health checks to route your traffic to instances or targets that are healthy

---------------------------------------------------
  OSI (Open Systems Interconnection) Model
    https://www.imperva.com/learn/application-security/osi-model/

    - describes the 7 layers that computer systems use to communicate over a network
    - the internet is not based on OSI, but on the simplier TCP/IP model, but OSI is widely used

  7 layers:
    7 Application Layer
      - Human-computer interaction layer where applications can access the network services
      - used by web browsers and email clients
      - application protocols include HTTP, FTP, Simple Mail Transfer Protocol (SMTP), DNS
    6 Presentation Layer
      - Ensures that data is in usable format and is where data encryption occurs
      - defines how 2 devices should encode, encrypt, and compress data so it is received correctly
        at the other end
    5 Session Layer
      - maintains connections and is responsible for controlling ports and sessions
      - creates communication channels, called sessions, between devices
    4 Transport Layer
      - transmits data using transmission protocols including TCP and UDP
      - takes data transferred in the session layer and breaks it into "segments" on transmitting end
      - it resposible for reassemblying the segments on the receiving end
    3 Network Layer
      - decides which physical path the data will take
      - 2 main functions:
         1: breaking up segments into network packets, and reasembling the backs on the receiving end;
         2: routing packets by discovering the best path across a physical network
      - uses network addresses (typically IP addresses) to route packets
    2 Data Link Layer
      - defines the format of the data on the network
    1 Physical Layer
      - transmit raw bit stream over the physical medium
      - responsible for the physical cable or wireless connections between network nodes

---------------------------------------------------
10.2 Using Application Load Balancers

   Layer 7 Load Balancing
     - functions at the Application layer - the 7 layer of the Open Systems Interconnection (OSI) model
     - after the LB receives a request, it evaluates the listener rules in priority order to determine
       which rule to apply, and selects a target from target group for the rule action
   Listeners, Rules, and Target Groups
   Listeners
     - a listener checks for connection requests from clients, using the protocol and port you configure
     - you define rules that determine hwo the load balancer routes requests to its registered targets
     - each rule consists of a priority, one or more actions, and one or more conditions
     - listener support HTTP & HTTPS protocols with ports 1 - 65535
   Rules
     - when the conditions for a rule are met, then its actions are performed
     - you must define a default rule for each listener, and you can optionally define additional rules
     - supported rule conditions: host-header, http-request-method, path-pattern, source-ip, http-header, query-string

   Target Groups
     - each target group routes requests to one or more registered targets, such as EC2s, Lambda, etc, using
       protocol and port number you specify

   Application Load Balancer Diagram
   Path-based Routing
     Common Exam Scenerio
       - Enable Path Pattern example

     user -> Route 53 URL -> Application LB us-east1a -> if path "myurl.com"        ->  us-east-1a EC2
                                                      -> if path "myurl.com/images" ->  us-east1b EC2s

   Limitations of Application Load Balancers
     - only support HTTP and HTTPs

   HTTPS Load balancing
     - to use HTTPS listener, you must deploy at least one SSL/TLS server certificate on you LB
     - LB uses server certificate to terminate the frontend connection and then decrypt requests
       from clients before sending them to the targets
     - if you use Route 53 to register your own domain name,  AWS will generate the SSL certificate for you

   Console Demo
      Listeners and routing
         -> Listener HTTP and/or HTTPS
         -> Routing is to a Target Group
         -> need to create target group
             - has choose target: Instances, IP Addresses, Lambda Function, Appliciation LB

   Exam Tips

     Application Load Balancers
       Listeners:
         - a listener checks for connection requests from clients, using the protocol and port your configure
      Rules
        - determine how the load balancer routes requests to its registered targets
        - each rule consists of a priority, one or actions, and one or more conditions

     Target Groups
       - each target group routes requests to one or more registered targets, such as EC2s, using
         protocol and port number you specify

     Limitations of Application Load Balancers
       - only support HTTP and HTTPs

   HTTPS Load balancing
     - to use HTTPS listener, you must deploy at least one SSL/TLS server certificate on you LB
     - LB uses server certificate to terminate the frontend connection and then decrypt requests

---------------------------------------------------
10.3 Extreme Peformance with Network Load Balancers

  Layer 4 Load Balancing
    - functions at the 4 [transport] layer of the Open Systems Interconnection (OSI) model
    - it can handle millions of requests per second

  Request Received, Listeners, and Target Groups
  Request Recived:
    - after it receives a connection request, it selects a target from the target group
      for the default rule
    - attempts to open a TCP connection to the select target on the port specified in the
      listener configuration

   Listeners
     - a listener checks for connection requests from clients, using the protocol and port you configure
     - the listener on a Network LB then forwards the request to the target group
     - there are no rules, unlike with Application LB

   Target Groups
     - each target group routes requests to one or more registered targets, such as EC2s, using
       protocol and port number you specify

  Ports and Protocols
     Protocols: TCP, TLS (Transport Security Layer), UDP, TCP_UDP
     ports: 1 - 65535
  Encryption
    - you can use TLS listener to offload the work of encyrption and decryption to your
      LB so your application can focus on their business logic
    - if the listener protocol is TLS, you must deploy exactly one SSL server certificate
      on the listener
  Exam times
    Use Cases
      - are best suited for load balancing of TCP traffic where extreme peformance is required
      - it can handle millions of requests per second, while maintaining ultra-low latencies
    4 Tips for Network Load Balancers
      - operate at Layer 4 [transport]
      - use for extreme performance
      - Other use cases are where you need protocols not supported by the Application Load Balancer
      - can decrypt traffic, but you will need to install the certificate on the network LB

---------------------------------------------------
10.4 Using the Classic Load Balancer

  Classic Load Balancers
    - legacy load balancer
    - can load balance HTTP/HTTPS applications using Layer 7 [Application] and use layer-7
      specific features, such as X-Forwarded and sticky sessions
    - You can also use strick Layer 4 [transport] balancing for applications that reply
      purely on the TCP protocol

  X-Forward-For Header
     - when traffic is sent from a LB, the server access logs contain the IP address of the
       proxy or LB only
     - to see the original IP address of the client, the X-Forwarded-For requset header is used
     - The X-Forwarded-For request header may contain multiple IP addresses that are comma separated.
       The left-most address is the client IP where the request was first made. This is followed by any
       subsequent proxy identifiers, in a chain.

  X-Forward-For header
     user         -> load balancer    -> EC2
     124.12.3.231    10.0.0.23        see the request from 10.0.0.23
                                      X-Forward-For header has: 124.12.3.231

  Gateway Timeouts
    - if your applications stops responding, the Classic LB responds with 504 error
    - this means the application is having issues
    - this could be either at the web server layer or database layer

  Console Demo
    -> EC2 -> Load Balancers -> Create Load Balancer -> Classic Load Balancer
       LB Name: MyCLB, VPC: acloudguruVPC, Load Balancer Protocols: HTTP (can add TCP ports, SSL, etc)
       next -> add security group: WebDMZ -> next Health Checks -> add EC2 instance

  Exam Tips

    Classic Load Balancer
       A 504 error means the gateway has timed out
         - this means the application is not responding with the idle timeout period
         - troubleshoot the application - is it the web servier or data server?
         - not a Classic LB issue
      Need the IPv4 address of your end user
        - look for the X-Forward-For header

---------------------------------------------------
10.5 Getting "Stuck" with Sticky Sessions

  What are Sticky Sessions
    - Classic LBs route each request independently to the registered EC2 instance with
      the smallest load
    - stickly sessions allow you to bind a user's session to a specific EC2 instance
      (e.g. if data is being saved on the EC2 for the specific session)

  Network Diagram
     user         -> load balancer    -> EC2
                                      -> EC2 (has sticky session, but EC2 is now diabled)

     Solution: Need to disable sticky session

  Exam Tips
    Sticky Sessions
       - enable your users to stick to the same EC2 instance [when using Classic LBs]
       - can be useful if you are storing information locally to that instance

    Sticky Sessions with Application Load Balancers
      - you can enable stickly sessions for Application LBs as well, but the traffic
        will be sent to the target group level. If multiple EC2 in target group,
        traffic could go to other EC2 instances

    Scenario based question:
      - if you remove an EC2 instance from a pool, but the load balancer continues to
        direct traffic to the EC2 instance
      - solution: disable sticky sessions

---------------------------------------------------
10.6 Leaving the Load Balancer with Deregistration Delay
  Note: with classic LB, it is called connection draining

  What is Deregistration Delay (or Connection Draining)
    - allows LB to keep existing connections open if the EC2 instances are de-registered
      or become unhealthy
    - enables the LB to complete in-flight requests made to the instances that are
      de-registering or unhealthy
    - you can disable deregristraion delay if you want the LB to immediately close connections
      to instances that are deregistering or have become unhealty

  Console Demo

    For Classic Load Balancer:
    Select Classic LB -> instances tab -> Connection Draining -> Edit
      unselct "Enable Connection Draining" -> Save

    For Application Load Balancer:
       -> Select Target Groups (useby the Application LB) -> Attributes -> Edit
         "Deregistration Delay" set to "0"

  Exam Tips

     Deregistration Delay / Connection Draining
       enable deregistration delay:
        - allows LB to keep existing connections open if the EC2 instances are de-registered
          or become unhealthy
       disable deregistration delay:
         - do this if you want your LB to immediately close connections to instances that are
           de-registering or have become unhealty

---------------------------------------------------

What kind of ELB load balancer would you select if you need to route traffic based on the contents of the request?

Chapter 11 Monitoring
---------------------------------------------------
11.1 CloudWatch Overview

  CloudWatch FAQ
    https://aws.amazon.com/cloudwatch/faqs/

  CloudWatch Concepts
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudWatch/latest/monitoring/cloudwatch_concepts.html

    Namespace
      - a container for CloudWatch Metrics
    Metric
      - time-ordered set of data points that are published to CloudWatch
    Metric Retentions
      - time to retain data points
        - longer interval data points are retains for longer retention periods
    dimension
      - a name/value pair that is part of the identity of a metric. You can assign up to 30 dimensions to a metric.
    dimension combinations
      - CloudWatch treats each unique combination of dimensions as a separate metric, even if the metrics
        have the same metric name
    Resolution
      - Each metric is one of the following:
          Standard resolution, with data having a one-minute granularity
          High resolution, with data at a granularity of one second
      - Metrics produced by AWS services are standard resolution by default. When you publish a custom metric,
        you can define it as either standard resolution or high resolution. When you publish a high-resolution metric,
        CloudWatch stores it with a resolution of 1 second, and you can read and retrieve it with a period of
        1 second, 5 seconds, 10 seconds, 30 seconds, or any multiple of 60 seconds.
        ...


  Why do we Monitor

  What is CloudWatch
    - a monitoring and observability platform that was designed to give us insight into our AWS
      architecture
    - It allows us to monitor multiple levels of our applications and identify pontential issues

  CloudWatch Features
    System Metrics
      - these are metrics that you get out of the box
      - the more managed the service is, the more you get
      - e.g. CPU usage is to high, memory utilization is to high
    Application Metriics
      - By installing the CloudWatch agent, you can get information from inside your EC2 instances
      - e.g. is my apache process running on the EC2 instance
    Alarms
      Alarms can alert you when something goes wrong

  Types of Metrics
    Default:
      - are provided out of the box and do not require any additional work on your part to configure
      - e.g. CPU Utilization (AWS cannot see what the CPU is being used for, but can see how much bandwith is used),
             Network Throughput (AWS cannot see the actual traffic, but can see the utilized bandwith)
    Custom:
      - will need to be provided by using the CloudWatch agent installed on the host
      - e.g. EC2 Memory Utilization, EBS Storage capacity

  Console Demo
    -> Prep: Launch EC2 instance
    -> goto CloudWatch -> Alarms -> Create Alarm -> Metric -> Select Metric -> EC2 -> Per-instance metrics ->
      -> StatusCheck -> select Metric -> threshold type: "static", Whenever StatusCheck Failed is: "Greater' than "0"
         period: 1 min, Name: CloudWatch_webserver
      -> create Alarm ->
          - initially in "Insufficient data" state because it need to wait 1 min

    Notes: - 50+ EC2 default metric on EBS (*Ops, IObalance, ByteBalance) Network throughput stuff, CPU stuff,
              statusCheck stuff
           - Metric choices include EBS, EC2, NATGateway, Logs, S3, Usage
           - Period (how often) to check - it can be from 10 sec to 1 day including custom
           - When alarm occurs, you have options to "stop this instance", "Terminate this instance", & "Reboot this instance"
           - have notications options for SNS to send an email, etc.

  Exam Tips
     - What tool do we use to Monitor: CloudWatch
         - you need to know the basic checks that included out of the box as well as what you are responsible
           for bringing to the table
     CloudWatch
       go to tool:
         - for monitoring
       Alarms:
         - no default alarms, anyting you want to hear about must be created
       Default vs Custom
         - AWS can't see path the hypervisor level for EC2 instances
         - get standard checks out of the box suchs as CPU Utilization, but you do not get Memory Utilization
           and how full is the disk without adding a cloudWatch agent
       Managed Services
         - The more managed a service is, the more checks you get out of the box
       Standard vs Detailed
         - standard reporting period is 5 min intervals, whereas detailed is 1-minute (small additional cost)

---------------------------------------------------
11.2 Application Monitoring with CloudWatch Logs

   Scaling based on Amazon SQS:
   https://docs.aws.amazon.com/autoscaling/ec2/userguide/as-using-sqs-queue.html

   What is CloudWatch Logs
     - a tool that allows you to monitor, store, and access log files from a variety of different sources
     - gives you the ability to query you logs to look for potential issues or data that is
       relevent to you

      What do we do with all our logs?
        - what about on-premise logs, cloudtrail logs, Lamdba logs, RDS logs, EC2 logs?

   3 CloudWatch Logs Terms
     log Event
       - the record of what happened - just a data point
       - it contains a timestamp and the data
       - e.g. you are tracking your apache website error log - everytime an error is reported in
         the error log is Log Event
     Log Stream
       - a collection of log events from the same source create a log stream
       - think of one continuous set of logs from a single source
     Log Group
       - a collection of log streams
       - for example, you have grouped all you Apache web server logs across host together

   CloudWatch Logs Features
     Filter Patterns
       - look for specific terms in you logs
       - Think 400 errors (bad request response) in web server logs
     CloudWatch Logs Insights
       - allows you to query all your logs using a SQL-like interactive solution
     Alarms
       - once you have identified your trends or patterns, it's time to alert on them

   Console Demo
      on your EC2 install the cloudWatch agent
         # centos 7
         sudo you install amazon-cloudwatch agent
         # ubuntu
         wget https://s3.amazonaws.com/amazoncloudwatch-agent/ubuntu/amd64/latest/amazon-cloudwatch-agent.deb
         sudo dpkg -i -E ./amazon-cloudwatch-agent.deb

          # config cloudWatch agent:
          sudo /opt/aws/amazon-cloudwatch-agent/bin/amazon-cloudwatch-agent-config-wizard
           -> answer the questions on what to be monitored & logs - this generates a JSON output and
                /opt/aws/amazon-cloudwatch-agent/bin/config.json file
          # load config to cloudWatch agent:
          sudo /opt/aws/amazon-cloudwatch-agent/bin/amazon-cloudwatch-agent-ctl -a fetch-config -m ec -s \
                -c file:/opt/aws/amazon-cloudwatch-agent/bin/config.json


          # Note: EC2 needs Role attached with CloudWatchAgentServerPolicy
            -> create role cloudWatchAgentServerRole -> AWS Service, Common: EC2, Policy: CloudWatchAgentServerPolicy
            -> attach Role to EC2

          -> CloudWatch -> logs -> Log Groups -> 1 log group called "access.log"

   Exam Tips

     Logs should go to CloudWatch Logs
       - if you need to view them or process them, then they should go to CloudWatch Logs
       - except for situations where you don't need to process them, then they should go straight to S3
       - you need a high-level understanding of what services act as a source for CloudWatch Logs
          - common log creating services that can write to CloudWatch Logs: EC2, Lambda, RDS, cloudTrail, on-premise

     CloudWatch Logs
       Go-to tools
         - generally, favor CloudWatch logs, unless the exam asks for a real-time solution (then Kinesis
           whiich is used to collect and process large streams of data records in real time.)
       Alarms
         - cloudWatch alarms can be used to alert if the filter pattern are found
       Agent Based
         - cloudWatch agent must be installed and configured plus EC2 needs IAM Role containin CloudWatchAgentServerPolicy
       SQL
         - if the examine mentions SQL, think CloudWatch Logs Insights

11.3 Monitoring with Amazon Manged Service for Prometheus and Amazon Managed Grafana


   What is Amazon Managed Grafana
     - fully managed AWS service allowing secure data visualization for instantly querying, correlating,
       and visualizing your operation metrics, logs, and traces from different sources

   Amazon Managed Grafana Oveview
     Grafana made easy
       - easily deploy, operate, and scale Grafana within your AWS account
     Logical Separation
       workspaces: (logical Grafana server) allow for separation of data visualization and querying
    AWS Managed
      - AWS handles scaling, setup, and maintenance of all workspaces
    Secure
      - Built-in security features help you meet corporate goverance and compliance requirements
        - includes tools like single sign on, data control, and reporting for audit purposes
    Pricing
      - based per active user in a workspace
    Data Sources
       - integrate it with several sources including CloudWatch, Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus,
         OpenSearch Service, TimeStream, etc

   Use Cases
     Container Metric Visualization
       - Connect to data sources like Prometheus for visualization EKS (Elastic Kubernetes Service),
         ECS (Elastic Container Service), or your own Kubernetes cluster metrics
     Internet of Things (IoT)
       - Vast data plugins make the service a perfect fit for monitoring IoT and edge device data
     Troubleshooting
       - Centralized dashboards allow for more efficient operational issue troubleshooting

   What is Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus
     - serverless, Prometheus-compatible service used for securely monitoring container metrics at scale

   Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus Oveview
     1. Open-source Prometheus
       - leverage the open-source Prometheus data model with AWS-managed scaling and availability
     2. Automatic Scalling
       - lets AWS manage automatically scaling based on ingestion, storage, and querying of metrics
     3. Designed For High Availability
       - AWS replicates data across 3 AZs in the same Region. Designed for Availability
     4. Choose your Kubernetes
       - works with clusters running on EKS (Elastic Kubernetes Service) or self-managed Kubernetes clusters
     5. PromQL
       - leverage the open-source PromQL query language for exploring and extracting data
     6. Data is stored in workspaces for 150 days (automatically deleted afterwards)

   Exam Tips

     What is Amazon Managed Grafana
       - fully managed AWS service allowing secure data visualization for instantly querying, correlating,
         and visualizing your operation metrics, logs, and traces from different sources

     What is Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus
       - serverless, Prometheus-compatible service used for securely monitoring container metrics at scale

     Security and scaling
       - Both services let AWS handle the high availability and automatic scaling of infrastructure
       - Leverage VPC endpoints for secure VPC access

    Grafana Data Sources
       - integrate it with several sources including CloudWatch, Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus,
         OpenSearch Service, TimeStream, AWS X-Ray, etc

    Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus Uses
       - leverage the service for (Elastic Kubernetes Service) or self-managed Kubernetes clusters

   Amazon Managed Grafana Use Cases
     Container Metric Visualization
       - Connect to data sources like Prometheus for visualization EKS (Elastic Kubernetes Service),
         ECS (Elastic Container Service), or your own Kubernetes cluster metrics

11.5 Implementing Advanced CloudWatch Monitoring for a Web Server

Learning Objectives
  - Download and Run the CloudWatch Logs Installer

  -> Connect to the webserver-01 instance, and install the CloudWatch Logs agent:
    $ wget -O awslogs-agent-setup.py https://s3.amazonaws.com/aws-cloudwatch/downloads/latest/awslogs-agent-setup.py

    $ sudo python ./awslogs-agent-setup.py --region us-east-1

- Configure CloudWatch Logs

  Follow the prompts to configure CloudWatch Logs, including opening the IAM console to generate an access key and secret key.
  Copy and paste the below path for the log file to upload: /var/log/apache2/error.log

- Log in to the CloudWatch Logs Website
  Open the CloudWatch Logs website to observe if the log has streamed correctly.
   Also view the contents of the error log in the CLI via: sudo cat /var/log/apache2/error.log

11.6 Work AWS VPC Flow Logs for Network Monitoring

  Learning Objectives:
   - Create a CloudWatch Log Group and a VPC Flow Log to CloudWatch
   - Create CloudWatch Filters and Alerts
   - Use CloudWatch Logs Insights
   - Analyze VPC Flow Logs Data in Athena

  ------------------------------|
  | VPC                         |
  |                             |
  ||----------------|           |
  || EC2 instance   |           | /--> S3 ---> Athena
  ||           ENI----> VPC Flow /    bucket
  ||----------------|    Logs    \
  | security Group              | \--> CloudWatch --> CloudWatch --> SNS --> Email --> user
  --------------------------------                       Alarm

  Note: VPC flow logs can be configured at the VPC, network interface (ENI), or subnet level.
        For lab, VPC Flow will be at the VPC level.

Learning Objectives

1. Create a CloudWatch Log Group and a VPC Flow Log to CloudWatch

    Create a VPC flow log to S3, setting the Maximum aggregation interval to 1 minute and the S3 bucket ARN to the VPC Flow Logs S3 Bucket ARN found in the lab credentials or obtained via S3.
    Create a CloudWatch log group. Name it VPCFlowLogs.
    Create a VPC flow log to CloudWatch using the IAM role provided for the lab (the one with DeliverVPCFlowLogsRole in the name) and the CloudWatch log group you created.
    Generate some traffic for the flow logs.

2. Create CloudWatch Filters and Alerts

    Create a CloudWatch log metric filter named dest-port-22-rejects. For the Metric namespace, use VPC Flow logs; for Metric name, use SSH Rejects; and for Metric value, enter 1.

        Use the below filter pattern:
        [version, account, eni, source, destination, srcport, destport="22", protocol="6", packets, bytes, windowstart, windowend, action="REJECT", flowlogstatus]

        Use the following for the log data:
        2 086112738802 eni-0d5d75b41f9befe9e 61.177.172.128 172.31.83.158 39611 22 6 1 40 1563108188 1563108227 REJECT OK
        2 086112738802 eni-0d5d75b41f9befe9e 182.68.238.8 172.31.83.158 42227 22 6 1 44 1563109030 1563109067 REJECT OK
        2 086112738802 eni-0d5d75b41f9befe9e 42.171.23.181 172.31.83.158 52417 22 6 24 4065 1563191069 1563191121 ACCEPT OK
        2 086112738802 eni-0d5d75b41f9befe9e 61.177.172.128 172.31.83.158 39611 80 6 1 40 1563108188 1563108227 REJECT OK

    Create an alarm called SSH-rejects based on the metric filter.

    Note: There may be a New Console Experience button in the top left of the CloudWatch console. These instructions are designed for this new layout, so ensure this is toggled on if it is present.

3. Use CloudWatch Logs Insights
  Apply the Top 20 source IP addresses with highest number of rejected requests sample query.

4. Analyze VPC Flow Logs Data in Athena

    Create the Athena table:
    CREATE EXTERNAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS default.vpc_flow_logs (
      version int,
      account string,
      interfaceid string,
      sourceaddress string,
      destinationaddress string,
      sourceport int,
      destinationport int,
      protocol int,
      numpackets int,
      numbytes bigint,
      starttime int,
      endtime int,
      action string,
      logstatus string
    )
    PARTITIONED BY (dt string)
    ROW FORMAT DELIMITED
    FIELDS TERMINATED BY ' '
    LOCATION 's3://{your_log_bucket}/AWSLogs/{account_id}/vpcflowlogs/us-east-1/'
    TBLPROPERTIES ("skip.header.line.count"="1");

    Create partitions to be able to read the data:
    ALTER TABLE default.vpc_flow_logs
    ADD PARTITION (dt='{Year}-{Month}-{Day}')
    location 's3://{your_log_bucket}/AWSLogs/{account_id}/vpcflowlogs/us-east-1/{Year}/{Month}/{Day}';

    Run the following query in a new query window:
    SELECT day_of_week(from_iso8601_timestamp(dt)) AS
      day,
      dt,
      interfaceid,
      sourceaddress,
      destinationport,
      action,
      protocol
    FROM vpc_flow_logs
    WHERE action = 'REJECT' AND protocol = 6
    order by sourceaddress
    LIMIT 100;

Exam Tips:

    VPC Flow Logs:
     - a feature that enables you to capture information about the IP traffic going to and from network interfaces in your VPC.
     - Flow log data can be published to the following locations: Amazon CloudWatch Logs, Amazon S3, or Amazon Kinesis Data
       Firehose. After you create a flow log, you can retrieve and view the flow log records in the log group, bucket, or
       delivery stream that you configured.

    VPC Flow logs can help you with a number of tasks, such as:
     - Diagnosing overly restrictive security group rules
     - Monitoring the traffic that is reaching your instance
     - Determining the direction of the traffic to and from the network interfaces

11.7 AWS Certified Solution Architect - Associate (SAA-C03) Monitoring
Question 2
A period is the length of time associated with a specific Amazon CloudWatch statistic. What is the default period value?
choices:
 - 10 seconds
 - 300 seconds
 - 600 seconds
 - 60 seconds

anwer
The default Amazon CloudWatch period value is 60 seconds.

A period is the length of time associated with a specific Amazon CloudWatch statistic. Each statistic represents an aggregation of the
metrics data collected for a specified period of time. Periods are defined in numbers of seconds, and valid values for period are
1, 5, 10, 30, or any multiple of 60. For example, to specify a period of six minutes, use 360 as the period value. You can adjust
how the data is aggregated by varying the length of the period. A period can be as short as one second or as long as one day
(86,400 seconds).  The default value is 60 seconds. Periods.

Chapter 12 High Availability and Scaling
---------------------------------------------------
12.1 Horizontal vs Vertical Scaling Overview

  Vertical Scaling
    - increasing instance size
  Horizontal Scaling
    - increasing the number of EC2 instances
    - increases availability
  3 W's of Scaling
    What do we scale?
      - need to decide what sort of resource we are going to scale
      - how do we define the template
    Where do we scale?
      - should we scale out database? Or Webserver?
    When do we scale?
      - how do we know when we need more?
      - CloudWatch alarm can tell use when it's time to add more resources

  Exam Tips
    Anytime you see scaling question, keep the 3 @'s in mind
      - What do we scale?
      - Where do we scale?
      - When do we scale?

---------------------------------------------------
12.2 What are Launch Templates and Launch Configurations

  What are we scaling?
     [EC2] Launch Template
        - specifies all of the needed settings that go into building out an EC2 instance
        - it is a collection of settings that you can configure so you don't have to walk
          through the EC2 wizard over and over

  Launch Templates vs Launch Configurations
    Launch Templates
      - more than just autoscaling
        - can also spin up non-autoscaling EC2 instance(s)
      - support versioning
      - more granularity
      - AWS recommended
    Launch Configurations
      - only for autoscaling
      - immutable
      - Limited Configuration options
      - Don't use them

  Let's go hands on
    -> EC2 -> Launch Templates -> Create Lanuch Template -> template name: aws-train-template, select "Auto Scaling Guidance",
       OS: AL2, instance type: t2.micro, key pair: pat_patfree2key  -> Create launch template

       -> Select "aws-train-template" template -> Action -> Launch Instance from Template
          subnet:  10.0.1.0/24 - us-east-1a, Security Group:  WebDMZ... -> Launch Instance


  Notes: - Many settings can be overridden in autoscaling (e.g. instance type), but key pair cannot (not true now)
         - if subnet is specified (previously VPC), then it cannot be specified in the auto scaling group


  Exam Tips

      What goes into a Launch Template
        - includues all the basics: AMI, EC2 instance type/size, security groups, potentially networking info
        - if networking info is included, it cannot be used in auto scaling group

      Launch Templates / Launch Configurations
        Launch templates
          - most up to date and flexible way to create a template - AWS best practices
          - can be versioned - can make different versions of the same template
        Launch Configurations
          - the older version. It is not "wrong" to use temp, but if possible use templates
          - configurations are immutable
        User Data
          - is included in the template or configuration
        Networking
          - configurations don't include networking information
          - template can included networking information

---------------------------------------------------
12.3 Scaling EC2 instances with Auto Scaling


   Amazon EC2 Auto Scaling FAQs
     https://aws.amazon.com/ec2/autoscaling/faqs/

   On-Demand Instance Limits
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/ec2-on-demand-instances.html#ec2-on-demand-instances-limits

  Auto Scaling Groups
    - Launch templates are the "what"
    - Auto Scaling Groups are the "where" (where the instances come on line, where are they created, where are they terminated)
    - an auto scaling group contains the collection of EC2 instances that are treated as a collective group
      for the purposes of scaling and management

  Auto Scaling Steps
    Define you Auto Scaling Template
     - you have to pick from your available launch templates or launch configurations
    Networking and Purchasing
      - pick your networking space and purchasing options
      - using multiple AZs allows for high availability (use at least 2 AZs for high availability)
      - for cost control, you can choose on-demand, spot, and reserved instances
    ELB Configuration
      - EC2 instances can be registered behind a load balancer as they come on line,
        and deregistered when they go away
      - the auto Scaling Group can be set to respect the load balancer health checks
        - if enabled, for instances failing health checks, the auto scaling group can deregister the
          failing ec2, terminate it, and start a new instance
    Set Scaling Policies
      - minimum, maximum, and desired capacity needs to be set to encure you don't have too many
        or too few resources
    Notifications
      - SNS can act as a notification tool, allowing you to let someone know when a scaling event occurs

  Setting Capacity Limits
    Auto Scaling Restrictions
      minimum
        - the lowest number of EC2 instance you will ever have online
        - if you set min to "0", and you have 1 EC2 which is failing health checks, it will be
          terminated since the min is "0"
        - for high availability, the min should be set to at least 2
      maximum
        - the highest number of EC2 instance you will ever have provision
      desired capacity
        - How many EC2 instaneces do you want right now - constantly changing
        - can never be lower than the "minimum and never by higher the maximum

  Console Demo
     -> EC2 -> Auto Scaling Group -> Create Auto Scaling Group -> Auto Scaling Group Name: aws-train-scaling,
       Launch Template: aws-train-template, VPC: default VPC, pick 2 subnets  -> Next
        select "No load balancer", Health check type "EC2"
        Group Size: Desired capacity: 2, Minimum Capacity: 2, Max Capacity: 5, scaling policy: None  -> Next
        Add notifications  -> Next

     # change desired capacity to 4
     -> select "aws-train-scaling" -> edit -> Desired Capacity: 4 -> update
     # instance increased for 2 to 4

     # change desired capacity to 3
     -> select "aws-train-scaling" -> edit -> Desired Capacity: 3 -> update
     # instances decreased for 4 to 3


   Note: - you can specify percentage of on-demand and spot instances
         - if using ELB, can use ELB health checks

  Exam Tips

     Auto Scaling and High Availability
       - Auto Scaling is vital to creating high availability application
       - for high availability, you want 2+ AZs plus load balancer

    Auto Scaling Group
      Networking
        - auto scaling group will contain the location of where your instance will live
        - Note: Can be set in launch template, but you want to define in auto scaling group
          so you can specify multiple subnets
      ELB
        - it's vital to select a load balancer for the instance to live behind
      Limits
        - min, max, and desired capacity are the 3 most important settings
      Notifications
        - SNS can act as a notification tool
        - You can be notified when EC2 Auto Scaling is launching or terminating the EC2 instances in
          your Auto Scaling group. You manage notifications using SNS.
      Balancing
        - Auto Scaling will balance your EC2 instances across the AZs

   EC2 Auto Scaling Dynamic Scaling Policies:
    Target tracking scaling
      - Increase and decrease the current capacity of the group based on CloudWatch metric and a target value.
      - It works similar to the way that your thermostat maintains the temperature of your home - you select
        a temperature and the thermostat does the rest.
      - an Auto Scaling group scales in direct proportion to the actual load on your application
    Step scaling
      - Increase and decrease the current capacity of the group based on a set of scaling adjustments, known
        as step adjustments, that vary based on the size of the alarm breach.
    Simple scaling
      - Increase and decrease the current capacity of the group based on a single scaling adjustment,
        with a cooldown period between each scaling activity.


---------------------------------------------------
12.4 Diving Deeper into Auto Scaling Policies

  Step Scaling
    Scaling out policy, e.g.:
      - add 10 instances when memory is between 60 - 80%
      - add 15 instances when memory is between 80 - 100%
    Scaling in policy, e.g.:
      - terminate 10 instances when memory is between 40 - 20%
      - terminate 15 instances when memory is between 20 - 0%

  Instance Warm-up and Cooldown
    Warm-up
      - period to wait for instance to start up before placing them behind the load balancer, and
        counting them towards auto scaling current instance count
      - stops instances from being placed behind the load balancer, falling health checks, and being terminated
    Cooldown
      - pauses Auto Scaling for set amount of time
      - helps to avoid runaway scaling events
      - default is 5 min
    Avoid Thrashing
      - you want to create instances quickly and spin them down slowly

  Scaling Types
    Reactive Scaling
      - you are playing catchup
      - once the load is there, you measure it and then determine if you need to create more resources
    Scheduled Scaling
      - if you have a predictable workload, create a scaling event to get your resources ready to go
        before they are actually needed
    Predictive Scaling
      - AWS uses machine learning algorrithms to determine when you will need to scale
      - they are reevaluated every 24 hours to create a forecast for the next 48 hours
    Demand (dynamic) Scaling
      - A more advanced way to scale your resources, using dynamic scaling, lets you define a scaling policy that
        dynamically resizes your Auto Scaling group to meet changes in demand.

  Console Demo
     demos a "Steady State auto scaling group"
        -> each time he terminates his ec2 instance, it automatically starts a new ec2 instance in a different AZ

     -> To create "Steady State auto scaling group" set Desired=Min=Max Capacity=1, and set multiple subnets each in different AZ
        so it will likely switch AZs each time is terminated
       -> create auto scaling group
         Launch Template: aws-train-template, VPC: default VPC, pick 3 subnets  -> Next
        Group Size: Desired capacity: 1, Minimum Capacity: 1, Max Capacity: 1, scaling policy: None  -> Next

  Exam tips

    Auto Scaling Policies
      - need a good understanding how auto scaling handles min, max, & desired capacity
      Scale out aggressively
        - get ahead of the workload
      Scale in conservively
        - once the instances are up, slowly roll them back when not needed
     Provisioning
       - keep an eye on provisioning times
       - bake those AMIs to minimize provisioning time
     Costs
       - Use EC2 Rls (Reserved Instances) for minimum count of the EC2 instances
       - if possible, scale with spot instances with a fallback to on-demand instances
    CloudWatch
      - your #1 tool for alerting Auto Scaling that you need more or less of something

---------------------------------------------------
12.5 Scaling Relational Databases (Scaling RDS)

   4 Ways to scale relational databases (scaling RDS)
     Types of Scaling
       - there are 4 types of scaling we can use to adjust our relational database performance
     Vertical Scaling
       - resizing the database from one size to another size can create greater performance
     Scaling Storage
       - storage can resized, but it's only able to go up, not down
       Note: Aurora automatically auto scales storage in 10GB increments, thus no need to pre-size Aurora storage
     Read Replicas Scaling
       - create read-only copies of our data can help spread out the workload
       - read replicas can be across AZs and regions
       - horizontal scaling for your read only workload
     Auora Serverless (note: different than Aurora)
       - offload scaling to AWS
       - excels with unpredictable workloads

   console demo
     -> RDS -> started with Aurorai(MySQL compatible) with default dev/test template settings & burst instances -> Create

     # scale veritically
     -> select  "database-1-instance-1" -> Modify ->
        -> can change instance type -> select large instance: db.t4g.large
        -> can schedule immediately are "apply during the next schedule maintenance window

        Note: Since Aurora database, storage does not need to be scaled since it automatically scaled
              in 10GB increments

     # read replica scaling
     -> select  "database-1" -> Action -> add reader -> DB Instance Identifier: db-reader-1, DB Instance class: can change size


     Notes:
        - Can also create a cross-region read replica (Action -> Create cross-region read replica)
        - read replica has a separate endpoint, thus, your code needs to be update to point write to primary DB
          and reads to replica database

   Exam Tips

     Scaling vs Refactoring
       - if given scenarios, and unless other specified, refactoring and changing to DynamoDB is viable scaling choice
         - it won't work that easily in the real world, but in the exam, switching database types can solve the problem

     Relational Database (RDS) scaling
       Read Replicas
         - read-heavy workload = read replica scaling
         - your code would need to be updated to point reads to the read replica endpoint
       Storage Scaling
         - RDS storage only scales up, it won't scale back down
         - Aurora scales storage automatically in 10GB increments
       Vertical Scaling
         - don't shy aws from this scaling
       Multi-AZ
         - unless in dev/test env, turn on Multi-AZ
         - with Multi-AZ, automatically fails over if the primary dataset fails
       Aurora Everything
         - Whenever possible, use Aurora if the situation calls for a relational database

---------------------------------------------------
12.6 Scaling Non-Relational Databasesa (DynamoDB)

  Scaling (Capacity) Options
    DynamoDB
      - a fully managed NoSQL database and it supports both document and key-value data models
      - Scaling is simplified when using DynamoDB, as AWS does all the heavy lifting for you
    DynamoDB Provisioned Model
      Use Case: generally predicatable workloads
      Effort to Use:
        - need to review past usage to set upper and lower scaling bounds
        - can setup autoscaling - set up a minimum, maximum, and target utilization
      Cost: more cost-effective scaling model
    DynamoDB On-Demand Model
      Use Case: sporadic workloads
      Effort to Use:
        - simply select on-demand
        - can setup autoscaling - set up a minimum, maximum, and target utilization
      Cost: pay small amount of money per read and write. Less cost effective

  Note:
    NoSQL Document Database Data Storage:
     - stores data in the form of documents, which can include nested data structures. Each document can have a
        unique structure and can contain different fields.
    NoSQL Key-Value Database Data Storage:
      - stores data as a collection of key-value pairs, where each key is a unique identifier and the value can
        be any type of data.

  Note:
    DynamoDb quotas (formerly limits)
      https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/ServiceQuotas.html
      - some quotas include:
        - Read/write capacity mode and throughput
          - can change twice in a 24-hour period

  Note:
     DynamoDB auto scaling:
      - If you use the AWS Management Console to create a table or a global secondary index, DynamoDB
        auto scaling is enabled by default
     - DynamoDB auto scaling uses the AWS Application Auto Scaling service to dynamically adjust provisioned
       throughput capacity on your behalf, in response to actual traffic patterns. This enables a table or a
       global secondary index to increase its provisioned read and write capacity to handle sudden increases in
       traffic, without throttling. When the workload decreases, Application Auto Scaling decreases the throughput
       so that you don't pay for unused provisioned capacity`

  Console Demo
    -> DynamoDB -> Create Table -> Table Name: my-db-table1, Partition Key: user-id,
      table settings: select "Custom Settings", Capicity Mode: "On-Demand" -> Create Table


    # after DynamoDB table is created, you can change Capacity Model back to Provisioned:
     -> click on "my-db-table1" -> Action -> Edit Capacity -> Capacity Mode: Provisioned -> save changes
         Auto Scaling: On, Min Capacity: 1  , Max Capicity: 10  , Target Untilization %: 75,
         Initial provisioned Units: 5
         option to "Go to CloudWatch"

    Notes:
         - DynamoDB "Default Settings" use "Provisioned" Scaling Model. You need to select
           "custom settings" to select "On-Demand" scaling model.
         - after you change from on-demand to provisioned, you cannot change back for 24 hours.


  Exam Tips

      Cost
        - keep cost in mind rather than performance when scaling DynamoDB
        - exam will generally approach from a cost perspective
        Predicatable workload: pick provisioned capacity
        sporadic workload:     pick on-demand capacity

      Non-Relational (DynamoDB) Database Scaling
         Access Patterns
           - know if it's predicatable or unpredicatable
         Design Matters
           - avoiding hot partitions will also nead to better performance
              - hot partition occur when the "partition key" values are not very distinct
                resulting heavy access for some partition key values
                https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/bp-partition-key-uniform-load.html
         Switch Capacity Modes
            - you can switch between on-demand and provisioned capacity modes, but only
              once every 24 hours per table

12.7 High Availability and Scaling Exam Tips

  Disaster recovery options in the Cloud
  https://docs.aws.amazon.com/whitepapers/latest/disaster-recovery-workloads-on-aws/disaster-recovery-options-in-the-cloud.html

  4 Questions to ask in the exam
    - is it highly available
    - which is appropriate for the situation: horizonital or vertical scaling? (generally horizontal, but not always)
    - is the scaling solution cost effective? (always keep cost in mind)
    - would switching databases fix the problem?
       - it won't work that easily in the real world, but in the exam, switching databas types can solve the problem


  What to Know for the Exam:?

   Auto Scaling is only for EC2:
        - no other service can be scaled using Auto Scaling
        - no longer true, now there is "AWS Auto Scaling" and "EC2 Auto Scaling:
        https://aws.amazon.com/autoscaling/faqs/
     Correction:
     EC2 Auto Scaling is only for EC2:
        - no other service can be scaled using EC2 Auto Scaling
        - other services might have built-in options, but they aren't included in the
          EC2 Auto Scaling Groups

       Get ahead of the workload
          - whenever possible, favor solutions that are predictable rather than reactive

       Provisioning
         - keep an eye on provisioning times
         - bake everything needed in those AMIs to minimize provisioning time (using user data will take longer)

       Spread out across Multiple AZs:
          - make sure your are spreading your Auto Scaling groups over multiple AZs

       Steady state groups:
          - allows us to create a situation where the failure of a legacy codebase or resource that can't
            be scaled can automatically recover for failure
          - To create "Steady State EC2 auto scaling group" set Desired=Min=Max Capacity=1, and set
            multiple subnets each in different AZ

       ELB are essential
         - make sure you enable health checks from load balancers - otherwise, instances won't be
           terminated and replaced when the fail health checks
           - health checks are NOT enabled by default
         - distribute traffic to your EC2 instances

   Scaling Databases
      RDS
        - has the most database scaling options
        - vertical scaling by changing instance type/size
        - storage scaling out option (but not storage scaling in)
        - read horizontal scaling with read replicas
      Horizontal scaling is usually preferred over vertical
      Read replicas are for read performance horizontal scaling

      DynamoDB
        - scaling comes down to access patterns
        - scaling options
          Predicatable workload: pick provisioned capacity
          sporadic workload:     pick on-demand capacity

   Disaster Recovery strategties
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/whitepapers/latest/disaster-recovery-workloads-on-aws/disaster-recovery-options-in-the-cloud.html
     Disaster recovery strategies available to you within AWS can be broadly categorized into four approaches:
     1. Backup & Restore
       - RPO/RTO: Hours
       - lower priority use cases
       - provision all AWS resources after event
       - restore backups after event
     2. Pilot Light
       - RPO/RTO: 10s of minutes
       - services idle
       - provision some AWS resources and scale after event
     3. Warm Standby
       - RPO/RTO: minutes
       - always running but smaller
       - business critical
       - scale AWS resources after event
     4. Multi-site
       - RPO/RTO: real-time
       - zero downtime
       - mission critical Services

12.8 AWS Certified Solutinon Architect - Associate (SAA-C03) High Availability and Scaling

Chapter 13 Decoupling Workflows

---------------------------------------------------
13.1 Decoupling Workflows Overview

  Tight Coupling
     e.g.: user -> EC2 Frontend -> EC2 backend
       - if either EC2 Frontend or EC2 backend go offline, user is down
       - one instance talking directly to another instance

  Loose Coupling
                 ->  EC2 Frontend ->      -> EC2 Backend
     User -> ELB ->  EC2 Frontend ->  ELB -> EC2 Backend
                 ->  EC2 Frontend ->      -> EC2 Backend

       - with loose coupling, one or more instances can go offline, but service remains up
       - never want 1 EC2 talking directly to another EC2
       - there are other non-ELB options for loose Coupling (using SQS)

  3 UpComing up services
     Simple Queue Service (SQS)
       - a fully managed message queuing service that enables you to decouple and scal microservices,
         distributed systems, and serverless applications
     Simple Notification Service (SNS)
       - a fully managed messaging service for both application-to-application (A2A) and
         application-to-person (A2P) communication
     API Gateway
       - a fully managed service that makes it easy for developers to create, publish, maintain, monitor,
         and secure APIs at any scale

  Exam Tips

     Never Tightly Couple
       - avoid answers that include tight coupling - such as an EC2 instance that is directly communicating
         to another EC2 instance

      Decoupling Workflows
        - always loosely couple
        - never tightly couple
        - internal and external
          - every level of the application should be loosely coupled
          - no direct EC2 to EC2 communication - always buffered by ELB, SQS, etc.
        - 0ne size doesn't fit all
          - There is not single way to decouple

---------------------------------------------------
13.2 Messaging with SQS (Simple Queue Service)

  Poll-Based Messaging
    - one resource sends messages to SQS queue, another resource retrieve messages when it is ready (polls queue)

  What is SQS
    - a messaging queue that allows asynchronous processing of work
    - frontend resource will write a message to an SQS queue, and then backend resource will retreive that
      message from SQS

  SQS Important Settings
    Delivery Delay (e.g. The ReceiveMessage call sets WaitTimeSeconds to 0)
      Default is 0; can be set up to 15 minute
      - time before the message is available for the backend resource
      - e.g. maybe need a delay to verify credit card info
    Message Size
      Messages can be up to 256KB of text in any format
      - message size can be set lower, but it cannot go higher
    Encryption
      - messages are encrypted in transit by default
      - you can add at-rest encryption, but not be default
      - provides SSE (Server Side Encryption) using SQS owned encryption (SSE-SQS) by default
      - at rest encryption using SSE-SQS is provided at no additional charge and is supported
        for both Standard and FIFO SQS queues using HTTPS Endpoints (just need to enable it)
    Message Retention
      - default is 4 days
      - can be set between 1 minutes and 14 days
      - after message retention period, message exceeding this period are purged
    Long Polling and Short Polling
      - short polling is the default
      - in most cases, you should use long polling
      - short polling: check for message, if no message, then disconnect
      - long polling:  check for message, if no message, Wait for a period of time before disconnecting
    Queue Depth
      This can be a trigger for autoscaling
       - auto scale up as queue depth increases, auto scale down as depth decreases


  SQS Visibility Timeout
    - default Visibility timeout is 30 sec, min is 0 sec, max is 12 hours
    - after backend resource receives the message, the SQS queue locks out the message for the
      Visibility Timeout period (no other resource has access to the message during the lock out)
    - if backend does NOT respond in the Visibility Timeout period to purge the message, the message
      reappears in the SQS queue and it is available for other backend resources

  Exam Tips


    Know SQS Important settings
      Delivery Delay, Message Size, in-transit Encryption, at-rest encryption, Message retention,
      Polling (long vs short), Queue Depth, Visibility Timeout

    Delivery Delay
      Default is 0; can be set up to 15 minute
      - time before the message is available for the backend resource
      - e.g. maybe need a delay to verify credit card info

    Encryption
      - messages are encrypted in transit by default
      - you can add at-rest encryption, but not be default
      - provides SSE (Server Side Encryption) using SQS owned encryption (SSE-SQS) by default
      - at rest encryption using SSE-SQS is provided at no additional charge and is supported
        for both Standard and FIFO SQS queues using HTTPS Endpoints (just need to enable it)
      - SSE protects the contents of messages in queues using SQS-managed encryption keys (SSE-SQS)
        or keys managed in the AWS Key Management Service (SSE-KMS).

    Nothing Last forever
      Message Retention
        - default is 4 days
        - can be set between 1 minutes and 14 days
        - after message retention period, message exceeding this period are purged

    Long vs Short Polling
      - short polling is the default
      - long polling is more efficient with your cost and CPU cycles
      - in most cases, you should use long polling
      - short polling: check for message, if no message, then disconnect
      - long polling:  check for message, if no message, Wait for a wait time period (max 20 sec)
        of time before disconnecting

    Message Size
      Messages can be up to 256KB of text in any format
      - message size can be set lower, but it cannot go higher

    Queue Depth: From SQS FAQs: How large can Amazon SQS message queues be?
    - A single Amazon SQS message queue can contain an unlimited number of messages. However, there is a
      quota of 120,000 for the number of inflight messages for a standard queue and 20,000 for a FIFO
      queue. Messages are inflight after they have been received from the queue by a consuming component,
      but have not yet been deleted from the queue.

---------------------------------------------------
13.3 Sidelining Messages with Dead-letter Queuues

  Dead-Letter Queues (DLQ)
   - e.g. SQS message has bad data (phone number instead of address) so it cannot be processed. In this
          scenario, the backend resource fails to processes, it so it place back on the SQS after the
          Retention Period to be continuing sent to another backend resource and failing again and again
          until it hits the Message Retention period and get purged
    - Sideline Queue to store the problemmatic messages after the number retries has been exceed

  Console Demo
     # create DLQ queue
     -> SQS -> Create Queue -> Name: 2nd-chance-queue, Message Retention: 14 days -> Create
     # create primary SQS queue
     -> SQS -> Create Queue -> Name: primary-sqs-queue, Dead-Letter Queue: Enable, Choose queue: arn.*2nd-chance-queue,
       Maximum Receives: 2 -> Create Queue

     # send a message
        -> primary-sqs-queue -> send and receive message -> send message -> Message Body: Hello Gurus ->

     # poll for message 3 times
       -> during 3 poll for message, the message is move from the "primary-sqs-queue

     # poll in 2nd-chance-queue
        -> 2nd-chance--queue -> send and receive message -> receivied message -> poll for message -> stop polling
          -> "hello Gurus" message is now in the 2nd-chance-queue
     Notes:
         - You need to create the Dead-Letter Queue (DLQ) 1st before creating primary SQS queue
           because you need to give DLQ ARN to the primary Queue when creating
         - DLQ is not a special type of queue, just another SQS queue setup to act as a DLQ
         - DLQ Maximum receives is the number of times a message should be process attempts before
           for moving it to the DLQ queue
         - Message Received count in DLQ carries over (sum of message receive count from primary queue and DLQ queue)

  Exam Tips

     Dead-Letter Queue are the best sideline
       - problems with a message in SQS , think about DLQs and visibility timeout
       - DLQ are just regular SQS queues being used as DLQ

      Queue Depth
        - use CloudWatch alarm to monitor queue depth on primary queue AND DLQ


      Dead-Letter Queues
        - monitor with CloudWatch Alarms
        - DLQ are just SQS queues that are set to receive the reject messages from the primary queues
        - same Retention Window
             - message can be held up to 14 days (default: 4 days)
        - Usable with SNS
           - you can create SQS DLQ for SNS topics
        - The redrive policy specifies the source queue, the dead-letter queue, and the conditions under
          which SQS moves messages from the former to the latter if the consumer of the source queue fails to
          process a message a specified number of times. The maxReceiveCount is the number of times a consumer
          tries receiving a message from a queue without deleting it before being moved to the dead-letter queue


---------------------------------------------------
13.4 Ordered Messages with SQS FIFO

  Standard SQS Message Ordering
    - best effort ordering
    - occassionally, may have duplicate message out
    - nearly unlimited transactions per second
  FIFO SQS Message Ordering
    - if ordering matters and non-duplication matters use FIFO SQS
    - FIFO SQS guarentees ordering and no duplication
    - 300 messages per second limit
    - more CPU processing on AWS side, as a result slightly higher cost
  Console Demo
    # need for message to stay in primary SQS que for this demo
    -> for "primary-sqs-queue" -> Edit -> DLQ Maximum receives: 1000 -> save
    # send 10 messages with numbers 1 to 17 to 'primary-sqs-queue"
    -> for "primary-sqs-queue" -> Send and receive messages -> Message Body 1 - 17 -> send message
    # message not in order
    -> poll more message with Max Message Count: 10 -> examine order

    # create a FIFO SQS queue
     # create primary SQS queue
     -> SQS -> Create Queue -> Name: primary-sqs.fifo, Type: FIFO -> create queue

    # send 5 messages with numbers 1 to 'primary-sqs.fifo" all with Message deduplication ID: 1
    -> for "primary-sqs-queue" -> Send and receive messages -> Message Body: 1,
        Mesage group ID: 1, Message deduplication ID: 1 -> send message 5 times
        -> Poll messages: only 1 received

    # send 5 messages with numbers 1 to 5 to 'primary-sqs.fifo" all with Message deduplication ID: 1 - 5
    -> for "primary-sqs-queue" -> Send and receive messages -> Message Body: 1 to 5,
        Mesage group ID: 1, Message deduplication ID: 1 to 5 -> send messages
        -> Poll messages: 5 received, and messages are in order 5 to 1

    Notes:
        - FIFO queue names must end with ".fifo"
        - FIFO Queue additional message fields:
          Message group ID: tag that specifies that a message belongs to a specific message group
          Message deduplication ID
            - token used for deduplication of messages with a the deduplication interval (5 min)
            - messages with the same "deduplication value within the deduplication interval are
              ignore (not put in the queue)
  Exam Tips

     SQS FIFO
       - if message ordering is required, SQS FIFO type is required
       Note: There are other ways to enforce message ordering, FIFO is not only way for the exam
            e.g. add message id  to message, etc

      SQS FIFO Queues:
         performance:
           - have a lower level of performance compared to standard SQS queues
           - 300 transactions per sec max
         ordering
           - you can order messages with standard SQS queue, but it on the user to do it
         message group ID
           - ensures messages are processed one by one
           - messages the belong to the same message group are always processed one by one,
             in a strict order relative to the message group (however, messages that below to
             a different message group might be processed out of order)
         cost
           - SQS FIFO queue cost a little more since AWS must spend more compute resources
             to deduplicate the message and reorder the messages.
             messages

13.5 Delivering Messages with SNS (Simple Notification Service)

  Amazon SNS message delivery retries
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/sns/latest/dg/sns-message-delivery-retries.html

  Amazon SNS FAQs
     https://aws.amazon.com/sns/faqs/

  Push-Based Messaging (vs Poll based)
    poll based messaging:
      - a request/response model
      - client makes a request to the server and gets a response from it immediately with
        the new messages or without any
    push based messaging:
      - a client opens a connection to the server and waits for messages
      - a client opens a connection to the server and waits for messages
      - the connection is kept open and server pushes messages synchronously to the client
        when it receives them from the sender

  What is SNS
    - is a push-based messaging service
    - it will proactively deliver messages to the endpoints subscripted to it
    - this can be used to alert a system or a person
    - e.g send a email or text message when a cloudwatch alarm goes off

  SNS Important Settings
    Subscribers:
     - where the messages goes to
     - e.g. Kinesis Data, Firehose, SQS, Lambda, email, HTTP(S), SMS, Platform application endpoint
    Message size:
      - up to 256KB of text in any format
    DLQ:
      - messages that fail to be delivered can be stored in an SQS Dead Letter Queue (DLQ)
      - SNS will not retry delivery messages that fail to be delivered except for HTTP(S)
    SNS FIFO or Standard topic:
      - SNS FIFO topic only supports SQS as a subscriber
      - for most cases, use a standard SNS topic type
    Encryption:
      - messages are encrypted in-transit by default
      - you can add at-rest encryption, but you need to enable it
    Access Policy:
      - a resource policy can be added to a SNS topic, similar to S3
      - publisher: who can publish messages to the topic
      - subscribers: who can subscribe to the topic (receive topic notifications)

   to setup:
      - create topic
      - create subscribers to the topic
      - publish messages to topic

     Notes:
         Kinesis Data: service to collect, process, and analyyze real-time video and data streams
         Kinesis Data Firehose: an extract, transform, and load (ETL) service that reliably
            captures, transforms, and delivers streamin data to data lakes, data stores, and
            analytics service

  Console Demo
    -> pre-created a few SQS queues

    # create SNS topic
    -> SNS -> Topics -> Create Topic -> Type: Standard, Name: sns-std-demo, Display Name: sns std demo
       -> create topic
    # add subscription to SNS topic to SQS queues
      -> Create subscription -> Protocol: SQS, Endpoint: primary-sqs-queue -> Create Subscription
      -> Create subscription -> Protocol: SQS, Endpoint: 2md-chance-queue -> Create Subscription
      -> Create subscription -> Protocol: email, Endpoint: phreg20@gmail.com -> Create Subscription
        -> need to confirm from email account
      # MUST create acces policy on SQS queue for SNS message to be received!
      e.g access policy for 2nd-chance-queue:
         {
           "Version": "2012-10-17",
           "Statement": [
             {
               "Sid": "MySQSPolicy001",
               "Effect": "Allow",
               "Principal": "*",
               "Action": "sqs:*",
               "Resource": "arn:aws:sqs:us-east-1:012345678901:2nd-chance-queue",
               "Condition": {
                 "ArnEquals": {
                   "aws:SourceArn": "arn:aws:sns:us-east-1:012345678901:sns-std-demo"
                 }
               }
             }
           ]
         }
      # create a message
      -> SNS -> Topic -> sns-std-demo -> Publish message -> Subject/Body: my demo 1st msg -> send

      # verify message was received
      -> SQS -> Q


  Exam Tips

     SNS Messages
       Push based notification: SNS
     CloudWatch and SNS
       - are the easiet way to alert you that something happened
     SNS Subscribers:
       - Kinesis Data, Firehose, SQS, Lambda, email, HTTP(S), SMS, Platform application endpoint
       Note: SES (Simple Email Service) is used for bulk email not notifications
     SNS Retries:
      - SNS will not retry delivery messages that fail to be delivered except for HTTP(S)
      - need to setup SQS DLQ so you do not lose failed message deliveries

13.6 Fronting Applications with API Gateway

   What is API Gateway
     - a fully managed service that allows you to easily publish, create, maintain, monitor,
       and secure your API
     - it allows you to put a safe "front door" on your application
     - allows outside world to talk to my AWS resources in a safe, secure, and monitor manner
     - allows you to be who, what, when restrictions around access to your resources
   API Gateway Notable features
     Security
       - allows you to easily protect your endpoints by attaching a web application firewall (WAF)
       - WAF allows you to: rate limiting, block specific countries, looking for SQL injection attacks,
           filtering out malicious behavior, etc
     Stop Abuse
       - implement DDoS (Distributed Denial of Service) protection and rate limiting to curb abuse
         of their endpoints
     Ease of Use
       - simple to get started with
       - easily build out the calls that will kick off other AWS services in your account

   API Gateway in Practice
                                        |--> API Gateway  --> Lambda --> Aurora (e.g. user account info)
     a cloud guru user ->  CloudFront --|
                                        |--> (videos) -> S3
   Console Demo
     # already created API
     # for exam, just need to know use cases

      - demo set to accept any request (delete, get, head, options, patch, post, put)
      - authorization option
      - native integration with Lambda
       flow:
                 |-> Method Request (Authorization option) -> Integration Request (e.g Lambda) ->|
          client |                                                                               | Lambda
                 |<- Method Response                        <- Integration Response            <-|

      Notes:
          - use API Gateway so you do not have to set keys/roles, etc., to access lambda
          - can create different stages (versions, e.g. test, prod)

   Exam Tips

      API Gateway:
        - secure and safe front door for your applications
        API
          - creating or managing an API, think API Gateway
        Web Application Firewall (WAF)
          - can front API gateway with a WAF to prevent maliciuous behavior like DDoS
        Stages (Versions)
          - supports versioning of your API
          - set different credential for different versions/stages
         Credentials
           - API gateway stops you from baking credential into your code
           - how to get public env safe/secure access to my resources
