16.21 Security Exam Tips

  DDoS (Distributed Denial of Service) Attack
    - DDoS attack is an attack that attempts to make website or application unavailable to
      you end users
    - Common DDoS attacks include Layer 4 attacks such as SYN Floods, NTP application attacks
    - Common Layer 7 attacks includes floods of GET/POST requests

  CloudTrail
     - logs all API calls made to your AWS account and stores these logs in S3

  What CloudTrail Allows?
     - after-the-fact incident investigations
     - near real-time intrustion detection
        - can integrate cloudTrail with Lambda functions to implement intrusion detection
     - Industry and regulatory compliance

  AWS Shield
     - questions talking about DDoS mitigation or protection against Layer 3 and Layer 4 attacks, think Shield
     - Shield (standard) protects only against Layer 3 and Layer 4 DDoS attacks on your ELB, CloudFront,
       and Route 53 (according to Shield FAQs, Shield protects applications running on AWS, not just these services)
  AWS Shield Advanced:
    - $3K/month, but will give a dedicated 24/7 DDoS Response Team
    - provides enhanced protections for your applications running on project EC2, ELB, CloudFront, and Route 53
      against larger and more sophisticated attacks

  AWS WAF (Web Application Firewall
     - WAF operates at Laye r 7
     - WAF can block Layer 7 DDoS attacks as well as things like SQL injections and cros-site scripting
     - WAF can block access from specific countries or specific IP Addresses
     AWS WAF Allows 3 different behaviors;
       - allow all requests except the ones you specify
       - block all requests except the ones you specify
       - counts the requests that match the properties you specify
     Create a rate-based rule on your AWS WAF and associate the web access control list (ACL) to
     the Application Load Balancer

  AWS Firewall Manager
    - a security management service in a single pane of glass
    - it allows you to centrally set up and manage firewall rules across multiple AWS accounts and
      applications in AWS organizations

  GuardDuty
    - Uses AI to learn what is normal behavior looks like in your account and to alert you any abnormal
      malicious behavior
    - updates a database of known malicious domains using external feeds from 3rd parties
    - monitors your CloudTrail logs, VPC Flow logs, and DNS logs
    - Findings appear in the GuardDuty dashboard. CloudWatch events can be used trigger a
      lambda function to address a threat

  Macie
    - uses AI to analyze data in S3 and help identify PII, PHI, and financial data
    - great for HIPAA and GDPR compliance as well as preventing identify theft
    - Macie alerts can be sent to EventBridge and integrated with your event management systems
    - automate remediation actions using other AWS services, such as Step Functions
    Notes:
      PII: Personal Identifiable informatino
      PHI: Personal Health Information

  AWS Inspector
    - used to perform vulnerability scans on EC2 instances and VPCs
    network Assessments
      - Network configuration analysis to check for ports reachable from outside the VPC
      - inspector agent is not required
    Host Assessments
      - Vulnerable software (CVE), host hardening (CIS Benchmarks), and security best practices
      - inspector agent is required
    Assessments
      - can run assessments once or, alternatively, run them weekly


   AWS KMS (Key Management Service)
     - KMS is managed service that makes it easy for you to create and control encryption keys
       used to encrypt your data
   Getting Started with KMS
    - you start using the service by requesting the creation of a CMK (Customer Master Key)
      Note: CMK now change to KMS Key or AWS KMS Key
    - you control the lifecycle of the CMK as well as who can use or manage it

   3 ways to Generate a CMK (Customer Master Key)
     1. AWS creates the CMK for you
       - the key material for a CMK is generated within HSMs (Hardware Security Module) managed by AWS KMS
     2. Import Key material from you key management infrastructure and associate it with a CMK
     3. Have the key material generated and used in AWS CloudHSM cluster as part of the
        custom key store feature in AWS KMS

   3 ways to control permissions
     Use a Key Policy
       - controlling access this way means the full scope of access to the CMK is defined
         in a single document (the key policy)
     Use IAM Policies in combination with Key Policy
       - Controlling access this way enables you to manage all the permissions for you IAM
         identity in IAM
     Use grants in combination with Key Policy
      - Controlling access this way enable you to allow access to the CMK in the key policy,
        as well as allow users to delegate their access to others


 Note: AWS KMS supports two kinds of keys - master keys and data keys.
       - Master keys can be used to directly encrypt and decrypt up to 4 kilobytes of data and can
         also be used to protect data keys.
       - The data keys are then used to encrypt the customer data and the master keys are used to
         decrypt the customer data.

   KMS vs CloudHSM
     KMS
       - shared tenancy of underlying hardware
       - automatic key rotation
       - automatic key generation
     CloudHSM
       - dedicated HSM to you
       - full control of underlying hardware
       - full control of users, groups, keys, etc.
       - no automatic key rotation

  Secrets Manager
    - used to securely store your application secrets:
      database credentials, API Keys, SSH keys, passwords, etc
    - Applications use the Secrets Manager API to interact with the Secrets Manager
    Credential Rotation
    - rotating credentials is super easy - but be carefull that your applications
      are not using embedded credentials
    - when enabled, Secrets Manager will rotate credentials immediately
    - make sure all your application instances are configured to use Secrets Manager
      before enabling credential rotation

  Parameter Store or Secrets Manager
     For exam, when should use Parameter Store vs Secrets Mnagers?
       - if you are trying to minimize cost, choose Parameter Store
       - if you need more than 10K parameters, key rotation, or ability to generate
         passwords using CloudFormation, use Secrets Manager

  Presigned URLs
    -  if you need to share a private files in your S3 buckets, think presigned URLs
    - presigned URL can be generated via command line (AWS CLI) or programmatically
    - presigned URL expiration time (expires-in) can be set in seconds (default: 3600 sec)

  IAM Policy Documents
    - not explicitly allowed == implicitly denied
    - explicit deny > everything else
       e.g. explicit S3 deny access wins over also having an explicit allow S3 access
    - only attached policies have effect
    - AWS joins all applicable policies to a resource
      e.g. have ec2 admin access attached to your developers and S3 access attached to our developer group
    - AWS managed policies vs customer managed policies

  AWS Certificate Manager
    Know what it is:
     - In the exam, you will get scenario-based questions around SSL certificates
       and what services you should use to integrate SSL certificates
    Supported Services
      - AWS Certificate Manager integrates with Elastic Load Balancer, CloudFront, and API Gateway
    Benefits
      - free servcie that saves time and money
      - automatically renew your SSL certificates and rotate the old certificates with new
        certificates with supported AWS services

   Continuous Auditing with AWS Audit Manager
     - if you have a scenario question about HIPAA or GDPR compliance that asks about continuous
       auditing or automating auditing reports, think AWS Audit Manager

   AWS Artifact
     - if you see a scenario about audits and the need to download compliance reports,
       think AWS Artifact

   Cognito Exam Tips
     user pools
       - directories of users that provide sign-up and sign-in options for users of your application
     identity pools
       - allow your users to access to other AWS services
     user pools + identity pools
     - you can use identity pools and user pools either separately or together


    Cognito Sequence
                       |-------------------|
             |<----------> user pool       |     1. Authentication and get token
      device |         |                   |
             |<----------> identity pool   |     2. Exchange token and get AWS Credentials
             |         |                   |
             |<----------> AWS Services    |     3. Access AWS services using credentials
                       |    s3   dynamoDB  |
                       |                   |
                       |-------------------|


   Amazon Detective
     - operates across multiple AWS services and analyzes the "root cause" of an event
     - using detective, you can analyze, investigate, and quickly identify the root
       causes of potential security issues or suspicious activties
     - Detective pulls data in your from your AWS resources and uses machine learning,
       statistical analysis, and graph theory to build a linked set of data that enables
       you to quickly figure out the root cause of your security issues
     vs Inspectory
     - do not confuse with Inspector, which is automated vulnerability management
       service that continually scans EC2 and container workloads for software
       vulnerabilities and unintended network exposure

  AWS Network Firewall
    - physical firewall to your VPC
    - a managed service that makes it easy to deploy physical firewall protection across your VPCs
    - it has managed infrastructure (i.e., a physical firewall managed by AWS)
    - questions about filtering your network traffic before it reaches your internet gateway,
      or if you require intrusion prevention systems or any hardwary firewall requirements,
      think Network Firewall

  Security Hub
    - question about a single place to view all your security alerts across multiple AWS
      security services and accounts, think Security Hub

17.1 Why do we automate?

    Why use Automation
    Benefits of Automation
      time
      Security
      Consistency
    3 Upcoming Services
      CloudFormation
        - infrastructure as code
        - allows you to provision resources quickly and consistently, and manage them
          throughout their lifecycles, but treating infrastructure as code
      Elastic Beanstalk
        - easy-to-use all in one service for deploying and scaling web applications and
          services developed with a variety of supported languages
      Systems Manager
        - gives you the ability to easily patch, update, manage, and configure your EC2
          instances along with on-premise architecture

    Exam Tips

      Automation
        - whenever possible, select an answer that doesn't include manual steps
        - replace a manual step with automated tools, so make sure what you're selecting
          automates the entire process and just a portion of it
       Automate Everything
       Tools - you need to know the right tool for the job
       Benefits: it's more reliable, more secure, and faster
       Immutable: allows you to easily create and destroy your architecture as needed

17.2 CloudFormation


  CloudFormation Overview
    - is a declarative programming language
    - it supports either JSON or YAML formatting
       - YAML supports comments, but JSON does not comments in the clouldFormation templates

  example code:

	AWSTemplateFormatVersion: 2010-09-09
	Resources
	   InternetGateway:
	    Type: 'AWS::EC2::InternetGateway'
	    Properties:
	      Tags:
	        - Key: Application
	          Value: !Ref 'AWS::StackName'
	        - Key: Network
	          Value: Public
	  GatewayToInternet:
	    Type: 'AWS::EC2::VPCGatewayAttachment'
	    Properties:
	      VpcId: !Ref VPC
	      InternetGatewayId: !Ref InternetGateway

  Console Demo

    -> AWS Console -> CloudFormation -> set region to Oregon -> Create Stack ->
       upload a template file: cloudformation_17_2.yaml
       Stack Name: cloudformationDemo
       parameter: InstanceTypeParameter: t2.micro
       -> Next -> Next -> Review
       Note: Template is upload to an S3 bucket
       check "I acknowledge that AWS CloudFormation might create IAM resources" (because it includes IAM role)

     -> select stack event tab
       -> shows status of resources being created
     -> select stack resourc tab
       -> shows created resources status
     -> go to EC2 -> see "CLI Host" running

 Note: Parameter Section of the template:
      - ec2 InstanceTypeParameter,  can be set to only 3 specified values:

	AWSTemplateFormatVersion: 2010-09-09
	Description: CloudFormation Demo
	Parameters:
	  InstanceTypeParameter:
	    Type: String
	    Default: t2.micro
	    AllowedValues:
	      - t2.micro
	      - m1.small
	      - m1.large
	    Description: Enter t2.micro, m1.small, or m1.large. Default is t2.micro.

 Note: Mapping Section of the template defines values to be used in template:
	 Mappings:
	  SubnetConfig:
	    VPC:
	      CIDR: 10.0.0.0/16
	    Public1:
	      CIDR: 10.0.0.0/24
	    Public2:
	      CIDR: 10.0.1.0/24
	    Public3:
	      CIDR: 10.0.2.0/24

   Mapping section could be used to define AMI values (different for each region),
     resource values, etc.


  Exam Tips:

    CloudFormation
       Basics
          - need to know the layout of a cloudFormation template and what the section do
          - know the "parameter" section (options for users), mappings (values for use in template),
            and resource section
       Cross-Region
          - hard-code values in the resource IDs can be the reason template fail to create in
            another region (e.g. hardcoded AMI values)
          - solution: include regional resource IDs in mapping section
       Troubleshooting
         - if it finds an error, cloudFormation rolls back to the last known good state
         - the last known good state for a brand new template means deleting the entire stack
       It's just and API call
         - CloudFormation makes the same API calls you make manually
       Immutable
         - cloudFormation can easily create and destroy your entire architecture as needed

17.3 Elastic Beanstalk

  What is PaaS (Platform As A Service)?
    - a single-stop application deployment model
    - you bring your code, and provider builds everything for your, deploys your application,
      and manages it going forward
  Elastic Beanstalk
    - the AWS PaaS tool for deploying and scaling web application and services
    Automation
      - automates your full deployment.
      - You can templatize what you'd want like your environment to look like
    Deployment
      - upload your code and it automatically handles the deployment from capacity provisioning,
        load balancing, and auto scaling to application health monitoring
      - you can upload your code, test your code in a staging environment, and then deploy to
        production
    Management
      - it will handling building out the insides of your EC2 instances for you
      - you don't have to get on the hosts yourself
  Elastic Beanstalk use cases:
    Quickly Launch Web Applications
    Create mobile API Backends for your applications
    Replat Critical business applications
  Console Demo

    - AWS console -> Elastic Beanstalk -> Create Application -> Name: bsDemo
      Platform: python , Application Code: "Sample application"  (could upload your own code)
      -> next, next, next -> create

      -> click on URL -> takes you uncustomized website


  Note:
      - Platform has many language choices (e.g. .NET, Go, Java, Node.js, PHP, python, ..., plus docker [container])
      - options to pick security group, AMI, EC2 instances types, create auto scaling  group, etc

  Exam Tips

    Elastic Beanstalk
      - if exam scenarios looking for simple one-stop solutions,
      - approach of "bring your code and that's it"
      PaaS (Platform As A Service)
        - it is a PaaS tool that can handle it all
      Platform Types
        - supports containers, Windows and Linux Applications
      Hand Holding
        - it's great solution to start with, but in general it's only for simplier web applications
      Traditional
        - it's not serverless
        - Beanstalk is creating and managing standard EC2 architecture

17.4 Systems Manager

  AWS System Manager Documentation
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/systems-manager/index.html

  What is Systems Manager?
    - a suite of tools designed to let you view, control, and automate both your AWS architecture and
      on-premise resources
    - a free set of tools

  Systems Manager Feature Overview
    - requires an agent to be installed to your ec2 instance and/or your on-premise instances
    Automation Documents (renamed Run Books)
      - can be used to control your ec2 instances or AWS resources (e.g. S3 bucket policy)
    Run Command
      - uses the systems manager run agent installed on your ec2 instances
      - executes commands on your hosts (using the run agent) across your entire fleet
    Patch Manager
      - manages your application versions
      - can schedule a time and patches to apply, and systems manager will handle it for you
    Parameter Store
      - securely store your secret values
    Hybrid Activations
      - can control your ec2 and on-premise architecuture using Systems Manager
    Session Manager
      - Remotely connect and interact with your architecture (do not have manage all the SSH keys)

  Console Demo
    -> Prep: must have EC2 running with system manager agent installed

    -> AWS System Manager -> select instance
       -> File System # can browse file system
       -> Actions -> start session (logs on to instance with sudo privileges - did not need to handle ssh keys )
       -> Parameter Store # store secret values
           -> Create parameter -> name: secret-password, type: secureString (encrypted) -> create
              -> need to compile parameter store with your resources
      -> Systems Manager -> Documents (left side) -> check "automation documents"

  Exam Tips
     Systems Manager
       - systems manager will rarely be called out by name. Instead, the names of the features
         (Parameter Store, Automation Documents) will be used
           example: using Automation documents to fix S3 Bucket permissions or using System Manager
              to connect to an instance
       On-site Support
         - System manager can manage both on-premise an cloud architectures
         - system manager agent must be installed on instances
       Overview Required
         - can be used to patch, update, and configure instances
       Unpaid SysAdmin
         - if an admin can do it, Systems Manager can as well
         - not cost for using in AWS, but a small fee for using on-premise
       Automation Documents
         - these are unable by AWS config to enforce architecture state

---------------------------------------------------
17.5 Automation Exam Tips

  4 Questions to Ask in the Exam
    - can you automate? Which automation tool can avoid the manual procedure
    - what kind of automation works in this scenario?
    - is the automation repeatable?
    - how would this work cross-region or cross account?

  CloudFormation
     Understand the CloudFormation template sections
        - need to know the layout of a cloudFormation template and what the section do
        - know the "parameter" section (options for users), mappings (values for use in template),
          and resource section
     Immutable architecture is preferabled
       - cloudFormation can easily create and destroy your entire architecture as needed
     Mappings, Parameters, and Parameter Store
        - hard-code values in the resource IDs can be the reason template fail to create in
          another region (e.g. hardcoded AMI values)
        - solution: include regional resource IDs in mapping section
        - cloudFormation can look up values store in parameter store
     Troubleshooting
       - if it finds an error, cloudFormation rolls back to the last known good state
       - the last known good state for a brand new template means deleting the entire stack
     It's just and API call
       - CloudFormation makes the same API calls you make manually
     Immutable
       - cloudFormation can easily create and destroy your entire architecture as needed

  Elastic Beanstalk
    - if exam scenarios looking for simple one-stop solutions,
    - approach of "bring your code and that's it"
    - If scenario is more complex and requires lambda, SQS queues, API gateway, think cloudFormation
    PaaS (Platform As A Service)
      - it is a PaaS tool that can handle it all
    Platform Types
      - supports containers, Windows and Linux Applications
    Hand Holding
      - it's great solution to start with, but in general it's only for simplier web applications
    Traditional
      - it's not serverless
      - Beanstalk is creating and managing standard EC2 architecture


   Systems Manager
     - systems manager will rarely be called out by name. Instead, the names of the features
       (Parameter Store, Automation Documents) will be used
         example: using Automation documents to fix S3 Bucket permissions or using System Manager
            to connect to an instance
     On-site Support
       - System manager can manage both on-premise an cloud architectures
       - system manager agent must be installed on instances
     Overview Required
       - can be used to patch, update, and configure instances
     Unpaid SysAdmin
       - if an admin can do it, Systems Manager can as well
       - not cost for using in AWS, but a small fee for using on-premise
     Automation Documents
       - these are unable by AWS config to enforce architecture state

---------------------------------------------------
17.6 Getting Started with CloudFormation

Question 2
What best describes "immutable pattern" for application code deployments?

Choices:
 - Deployed application code can't destroy cloud resources.
 - Deployed application code can't modify existing cloud resources, which, as a result, requires the creation of new resources.
 - Deployed application code can't proceed because AWS has locked your account.
 - Deployed application code requires your cloud architecture to be in read-only mode.
Sorry!

Correct Answer
 - Deployed application code can't modify existing cloud resources, which, as a result, requires the creation of new resources.

The immutable pattern specifies a deployment of application code by starting an entirely new set of servers
with a new configuration or version of application code. When we go immutable, we don't want to ever upgrade
in place. Once the cloud resource exists, it can't be modified.
Reference Documentation: Immutable and Blue/Green Deployment.

18.1 Caching Overview

  What is caching
  Where to Cache
    External
      - cache data thats's going to be returned to our users (e.g. images, videos,
        static contents)
    Internal
      - to speed up our databases by caching common/recent queries
    External
  AWS Caching Solutions
    CloudFront
      - external caching solution
    ElasticCache
      - internal caching solution to in front of databases
    DAX
      - DynamoDB accelerator caching solution
    Global Accelerator
      - external caching solution

  Exam Tips

    Caching
      - whenever possible, pick a solution that includes a caching solution
      Caches Everywhere
        - caches go in from of everything
      Speed
        - caches can help with speed and latency
      Internal caching vs External Cache
        - know which type of cache solution should be used

---------------------------------------------------
18.2 Global Caching with CloudFront

   CloudFront
     - a content delivery network (CDN) is a system of distributed servers (networks) that
       securely delivers data, video, applications, and APIs to customer globally
     - It helps reduce latency and provide higher transfer speeds using AWS edge locations

   CloudFront Important Settings
     Security
       - Defaults to HTTP connections
       - the option to add custom SSL certificates if you do not what to use the
         cloudFront endpoint - that is, if you want to route your entire domain
         through cloudFront
       - allows you to put a secure connection on your static S3 website
     Global Distribution
       https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/georestrictions.html
       - can use CloudFront geographic restrictions to specify list of allowed countries or
         a list of banned countries
       - Note: to block specific countries, you should use Web Application Firewall
         instead of cloudFront
     Endpoint Support
       - Can be used to front AWS endpoints along with non-AWS applications (e.g.
         your data center or another cloud provider)
     Expiring Content
       - you can force an expiration of content for the cache if you can't wait for the TTL

   Console Demo
     -> Started with Static S3 Website
     -> AWS Console -> CloudFront -> Create a CloudFront Distribution ->
        Origin: S3 bucket ->
        use defaults -> Create

        copy domain name for create distrubtion and paste in web browser

       Notes:
          Origin can be S3 Bucket, ELB, API Gateway, Media Container, ...
          Viewer protocol policy: HTTP and HTTPs, Redirect HTTP to HTTPS, & HTTPS only:
          Retrict viewer access: if yes, viewers must use CloudFront signed URLs or
            signed cookies to access your content
          Edge location selection: NO options to pick specific edge locations
              only can select all edge locations, only N. American & Europe locations, or
              use N. America, Europe, Asia, Middle East, and Africa
          WAF: you can attach a WAF
          SSL Certificate: can use the default SSL certificate or upload a custom SSL
             certificate
   Exam Tips

     CloudFront
       - for exam, every sort of external customer performance can be solved by
         putting cloudFront in fron of your application
       Speedy
         - cloudfront main purpose is to cache content at the edge locations to speed up
           delivery of data
       on-Site support
         - The CloudFront CDN works for AWS and on-site architecture
       Blocking Connections
         - can be used to block individual countries, but the WAF is a better tool for
           blocking countries plus rating limiting, SQL inject attacks, had DDoS
           layer 7 protection
       All the locations
         - you cannot pick specific edge locations only all or certain geographic areas

---------------------------------------------------
18.3 Caching your Data with ElastiCache and DAX

  What is ElastiCache
    - a manged version of 2 open-source technologies: Memcached and Redis
    - Neither of these tools are specific to AWS, but by using ElastiCache you avoid a lot
      of the common issues you might encounter
  ElastiCache Memcached vs Redis
    Memcached
      - simple database caching solution
      - not a database by itself
      - no failover of Multi-AZ support
      - no backups
    Redis
      - supported as a caching solution
      - functions as a standalone non-relational/no-sql database
        - next best no-sql database after DynamoDB is Redis
      - failover and Multi-AZ support
      - supports backups
  DynamoDB Accelerator (DAX)
    in-memory cache
      - DAX can reduce DynamoDB response times from milliseconds to microseconds
    location
      - this cache is highly available and lives inside the VPC you specify
    You're in Control
      - you determine the node size and count for the cluster. TTL for the data,
        and maintenance windows for the changes and updates

  Picking a Cache - ElastiCache vs DAX
    DAX
      - specific to DynamoDB and can only be used with storing data in this service
    ElasticCache
      - it can front just about any database, but really excels being placed in
        front of RDS

  Exam Tips

    ElastiCache and DAX
      Always Cache
        - on exam, always favor answers that include a database caching solution
      ElastiCache
        - it can front just about any database, but really excels being placed in
          front of RDS
        ElastiCache Redis
          - Redis can be a cache or a standalone non-relational/no-sql database
        ElastiCache Memcached
          - simple database caching solution
      DAX
        - specific to DynamoDB and can only be used with storing data in this service

---------------------------------------------------
18.4 Fixing IP Caching with Global Accelerator

  What is Global Accelerator
    - is a networking service that sends your users' traffic through AWS's global
      network infrastructure.
    - it can increase performance and help deal with IP caching
  Global Acclerator diagram

     User --> Global      ---> ELB -|-------> EC2
              Accelerator           |-------> EC2
              [2 static IP          |-------> EC2
                Addreses  ]         |-------> EC2


      Note: If ELB goes offline and replace with new ELB, its IP Address changes.
            However, "user" see Global Accelerator address and Global Accelerator
            updates with new ELB IP address

  Global Accelerator 3 top Features
    Masks Complex Architecture
      - as your applications grow and change and deployment happen, your users
        won't notice, because they will use the same Global Accelerator IP addresses
        no matter what
    Speeds things up
      - traffic is routed through AWS's global network infrastructure
      - select edge locations around the world are used
    Weighted Pools
      - you can create weighted pools/groups behind the IPs to test out new features
        or hand failure in your environment

  Console Demo
    -> setup EC2 static website instances in Oregon and London and place a ELB infront of them

    -> AWS console -> Networking ... -> AWS global accelerator -> Create accelerator
      Name: MyGlobAcc  -> Next -> Listener Port: 80, Protocol: TCP -> Next
      Add endpoint groups: Listener: 80 TCP, Region info: us-west-2 -> Next -> Create

      # add Oregon load balancer endpoint
      select "MyGlobAcc"  -> select Listener ID -> endpoint group -> Endpoints: select
        Oregon ELB ->  create

      # add London load balancer endpoint
      select "MyGlobAcc"  -> select Listener ID -> add endpoint ->  Endpoints: select
        London ELB ->  create

    select DNS Name -> paste in web browser -> send traffic to ELB endpoint closes
     to your location

    Notes:
       - IP address Pool Selection: can select from you pool of IP Addresses or
       - Each Listener can have multiple endpoints each with a weighting to
         determine how often selected
         select from Amazon's pool (default)

  Exam Tips

    Global Accelerator
      - solve IP Caching
        - the global accelerator 2 static IP addresses you're assigned with solve
          the problem of users not being able to access proper endpoints because of
          your architecture failed and the IP Changed behind the scene
       Speed
         - it uses edge locations to help speed up everything
       Weighting
        - you can create different weighted groups in each region your architecture
          lives in
       2 Static IP addresses
        - you are provided 2 IPs that don't change. You can also bring your own 2 IP addresses

18.5 Caching Exam Tips
   DynamoDB Accelerator (DAX)
     https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/dax/

   Comparing Redis and Memcached
     https://aws.amazon.com/elasticache/redis-vs-memcached/


  CloudFront and Global Accelerator
     - only option to add HTTPS to a static website being hosted in an S3 bucket
     - always favor answers that include caching
     - Global Accelerator when scenerio talks about IP caching

    Database Caches
      - if you see "in-memory database", think of 2 soluctions: Redis and DynamoDB. If
        scenario offers compares the 2, pick DynamoDB
      - Redis has more features that Memcached. Redis can be used as a cache or as a
        persistent data store, whereas Memcached is just a cache
      - Backup are supported by Redis, but not Memcache nor DAX

19.1 Managing Accounts with Organizations

  Consolidated Biling with Organizations
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/awsaccountbilling/latest/aboutv2/consolidated-billing.html

   Organizations
     - is a free goverance tool that allows you to create and manage multiple
       accounts.
     - With Organizations, you can control your accounts from a single location
       rather tha jumping from account to account

   Organizations Key Features
     Logging Accounts
       - it is best practice to create a specific account dedicated to logging
         [store logs from all accounts in logging account's S3 buckets]
       - CloudTrail supports logs aggregation
     Programmatic Account Creation
       - easily create and destroy AWS accounts using Organization's API
     Reserved Instances
       - RIs can be shared across all accounts
     Consolidated Billing
       - the primary account pays the bills
     Service Control Policies (SCPs)
       - SCPs can limit users' permissions [in each account]

   SCP Example:

   {
    "Version": "2012-10-17",
    "Statement": [
        {
            "Sid": "DenyStopAndTerminate",
            "Effect": "Deny",
            "Action": [
                "ec2:StopInstances",
                "ec2:TerminateInstances"
            ],
            "Resource": "*"
        }
    ]
   }

   Organizations Service Control Policies (SCPs)
     SCP
       - once implemented, SCPs will be applied to every single resource inside an account
       - they are the ultimate way to restrict permissions, and even apply to the root
         account

       - uses the same JSON structure as an IAM policy but applied to an account
       - SCPs never give your permissions, they only take permissions away
       - allows and denies
          allows: if using allow, only specified services/action/resources are allowed
                  resulting in implicitly denying for all other services/actions/resources
                  (e.g. if SCP allows ec2 and S3, all other AWS services are
                  implicitly denied)
          denies: denies the explicitly specified action/resource/service

   Console Demo
     -> Pre-demo: setup another account and setup 2 service control policies

     -> AWS Console -> Management & Goverance -> Orgaizations -> Polices
       -> Service Contol Policies -> [Enable Service Control Policies]

          -> demo created SCPs 1: NoEC2StopTerminate:

	   {
	    "Version": "2012-10-17",
	    "Statement": [
	        {
	            "Sid": "DenyStopAndTerminate",
	            "Effect": "Deny",
	            "Action": [
	                "ec2:StopInstances",
	                "ec2:TerminateInstances"
	            ],
	            "Resource": "*"
	        }
	    ]
	   }

          -> demo created SCPs 2: OnlyS3AndEC2:

	   {
	    "Version": "2012-10-17",
	    "Statement": [
	        {
	            "Sid": "Statement1",
	            "Effect": "Allow",
	            "Action": [
	                "s3:*",
	                "ec2:*"
	            ],
	            "Resource": "*"
	        }
	    ]
	   }

     -> root logged into the 2nd account with the above SCPs attached via organizations
        -> has 1 ec2 running
           -> tries to terminate -> terminate fails
           -> successfully creates a S3 Bucket (because SCP allows S3 and logged in as root)
           -> tries to create dynamoDB table -> fail due to allow SCP

  Exam Tips

    Logs and SCP (Service Control Policies)
      - for scenarios about wanting to ensure logs are centralized and no one can
        edit or delete them, pick answer that uses Organizations to centralize
        your logs and SCPs to restrict anyone from making changes to them
    Organizations
      Control
        - SCPs are the best way to have the final say in what you can do in your AWS account
      Cost
        - Billings easily roll up to a single account for payment
      CloudTrail & Logs
        - a lot AWS services (e.g. CloudTrail) have integration with Organizations
        - you can designate a single AWS account to hold your logs
      Sharing Reserved Instances
        - RIs can be shared across accounts
        - this allows you to have one "billing" account to hold the RIs
        - you can turn off the RI sharing

19.2 Sharing resources with AWS RAM (Resource Access Manager)

  Resource Access Manager (RAM)
   - a free service that allows you to share AWS resources with other accounts and within
     your organization (e.g. sharing networking space with other accounts)
   - RAM allows you to easily share resources rather than having to create duplicate
     copies in your different accounts
  What can be shared using AWS RAM?
    - transit gateways
    - VPC subnets
    - license manager
    - Route 53 Resolver
    - Dedicated Hosts
    - and many more
  Console Demo
    -> Prep: Used Organizations to create 2nd AWS account

    -> AWS Console -> Resource Access Manager -> Create Resource Share ->
        Resource: Subnets, Select Subnet -> Next -> Managed permissions for ec2:Subnet:
          AWSRAMDefaultPermissionSubnet (default)  -> Next ->
          Grant access to Principals AWS Account: <accountID> -> Next -> Create

     -> Login to 2nd AWS account:
        -> examine VPCs and see shared VPC (owner ID shows: shared)
        -> spin up EC2 in new VPC/subnet

     -> Login to 1st AWS account:
        -> does not show ec2 started in 2nd account
        -> can ping ec2 instance in 2nd account with local IP for ec2 in 1st account

  Notes:
     - Can optionally choose AWS resources to share (long list)
     - cannot share default subnets
     - can set a large range policy templates used to set permissions on the shared subnet
     - can share with any AWS account or only Accounts in your Organization

  Exam Tip

    RAM vs VPC Peering
      When to use VPC Peering or RAM?
         - if sharing resources in the same region -- RAM due to easy of use
         - if sharing resources across regions -- Use VPC Peering
    AWS RAM (Resource Access Manager)
      Sharing:
        - sharing resources means you don't have to duplicate them
      VPC Peering
        VPC Peering excels when you are connectiong to separate networks
      Paying:
        RAM is free, but the user creating the architecture pays
      Organizations:
        RaM easily allows organizations to share architecture

19.3 Setting up Cross-Account Role Access

  Cross-Account Role Access
    - As the number of AWS accounts you manage increases, you'll need to setup
      cross-account access
    - Duplicating IAM accounts creates a security vulnerability
    - Cross-accounts role access gives you the ability to set up temporary
      access you can easily control

  Cross-Account Role Access Steps to set it up

     Prod Account                      Prod Acccout
     Acc ID: 99 9999 999 999           Acc ID: 11 1111 111 111

    >--- Step 1 ------->       ------ Step 2 ------>    ---- Step 3 --->
                               |--------|----------|
    |------------------|       | policy | Develops |
    | Update App       |       |   yes  | Group    |--|  |-------------|
    | IAM Role         |       |        |----------|  |->|     S3      |
    |------------------|       |        |----------|     |   Bucket    |
    | Dev ID           |       |   No   |  Testers |  |-X|-------------|
    | 11 11111 111 111 |       |        |  Group   |--|
    |------------------|       |-------------------|
        Create Role               Grant Access              Test Access

  Console Demo

    -> Prep -> 2 AWS Accounts

     -> AWS IAM accounts -> IAM -> Roles -> Create New Role ->
         Trusted Entity Type: AWS account, AWS Account ID: <accID>
         -> Next -> permissions: Policy: S3FullAccess -> Next ->
            Role Name: s3-role -> Create


     -> 2nd AWS account
       -> Account -> switch Role -> account: <accId> Role: <s3-role>
          -> S3 -> can see buckets
          -> EC2 -> errors - no permissions
          -> IAM -> errors - no permissions

    Note:
       - Can add restrictions such MFA, Require external ID

  Exam Tips

    Cross-Account Roles
      Everywhere
        - Use Roles everywhere
      Auditing
        - any temporary employees get role access
        - no permanent credentials
      Temporary
        - Roles assumptions is temporary
        - you cannot permanently assume a role

19.4 Inventory Management with AWS Config

  AWS Config
    - an inventory management and control tool
      - can tell you what you have inside your AWS account include state of the resources
    - it allows you to show the history of your infrastructure along with creating
      rules to make sure it conforms to the best practices you've laid out
    - not a free service

  State of Your Architecture
    Query
      - you can easily discover what architecture you have in your account
      - can query by resource type, tag, and even see deleted infrastructure
    Enforce
      - rules can be created to flag something is going wrong
      - whenever a rule is violated, you can be alerted or even have it automatically fixed
      - example: create a rule when an S3 bucket goes out of compliance such as becoming
        public
    Learn / History
      - Can tell the history of your environment
      - when did something change? Who made that call?

  Console Demo
      -> AWS Console -> Management & Compliance -> Config
        -> Resources -> lists your resources
           -> select ec2 instance ->
                               Manage Resource <- allows your change its
                               Resource Timeline <- gives history of the resource
           -> select S3 Bucket -> Resource Timeline
                              -> shows bucket is not compliant with 7 config rules
                                 previously created for s3 buckets
                                 e.g. s3-bucker-public-read-prohibited
                                 -> Remediation action: AWS-DisableS3PublicReadWrite
                                    # Remediation is using an Automation Document
                                    # remediation can be set to automatically run
                                    # for demo, ran remediation manually
                                    -> select bucket -> Remediate
  Exam Tips

    AWS Config
      - exam questions about enforcing standards, think Config
         - examples: ensure your S3 buckets are not public, ec2 instances are using
           approved AMIs, EBS volumes are encrypted, etc
      Standards
        - Config is the best way to check what standards are applied to your architecture
      Deleted Resources
        - you can track previously deleted resources using Config
      Enforcements
        - you can use Automation documents or Lambda to enforce your standards
      Consolidation
        - you can roll up all your results to a single region

19.5 Offloading Active Directory to Directory Service

 AWS Directory Service [also known as "AWS Managed Microsoft AD]
    - a fully managed version of Active Directory
    - it allows you to offload the painful parts of keeping AD online to AWS
      while still giving you the full control and flexibility AD provides

  AWS Directory Service Types
    Managed Microsoft AD
      - this the entiry AD suite
      - when you want a complete AD suite in AWS and not anywhere else (e.g not onpremise)
    AD Connector
      - Creates a tunnel between AWS and your on-premise AD
      - get an endpoint in AWS for verification while leaving AD on premise
      - Provide federated sign-in to the AWS Management Console by mapping Active Directory identities
        to AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) roles.
    Simple AD
      - Standalone directory powered by Linux Samba Active Directory-compatible server
      - just as the AD basic authentification service

  Note: LDAP (Lightweight Directory Access Protocol):  a standard communications protocol used to read
             and write data to and from Active Directory. Some applications use LDAP to add, remove, or
             search users and groups in Active Directory or to transport credentials for authenticating
             users in Active Directory.

  Exam Tips

    AWS Directory Service [also known as "AWS Managed Microsoft AD]
      AWS Directory Service Types
        - fully managed AD service
        Managed Microsoft AD
          - this the entiry AD suite
          - when you want to migrate AD completely to AWS
        AD Conector
          - when you want to leave AD on premise (or somewhere else)
          - Creates a tunnel between AWS and your on-premise AD
          - get an endpoint in AWS for verification while leaving AD on premise
        Simple AD
          - Standalone directory powered by Linux Samba Active Directory-compatible server
          - just as the AD basic authentification service

19.6 Exploring with Cost Explorer

  Why do we Budget?
    - money

  Cost Explorer
    easy-to-use tool that allows you to visualize your cloud costs
    - you can generate reports based on a variety of factors include resource tags

  Cost Explorer Features
    - lets you build  reports
    Service
      - easily break down costs on a service-by-service basis
    Time
      - reports expense by monthly time frames (last month, last 3 months, last year)
      - estimates expenses going forward based on previous month, utilizations, etc.
    Filter
      - filter by tags, by categories, by region, by instance type, by service, etc.

  Console Demo
     -> AWS Cost Explorer ->
        - provides graphical view of costs over specified months per of time
        - can filter on: Linked Accounts, Regions, Instances, Usage Type, Resources,
          Cost Category, Tags, etc
         - need to enable tags as cost allocation tags before you can use them
           (e.g. createdby, named, department)
        - provide cost estimates for the future based on current resources and past history

  Exam Tips

    Cost Explorer
     Tags
       - tags are one of the most important ways to track your spend
       - need to enable tracking on specific tags going forward
     Budgets
       - Cost Explorer and Budgets go hand in hand
     Predictive
       - can estimate your spend for the upcoming months

19.7 Using AWS Budgets

  AWS Budgets
    - allows organizations to easily plan and set expectations around cloud costs
    - you can easily track your ongoing spend and create alarms to let users know when
      they are close to exceeding their alloted spend

  Types of Budgets
    - 4 types of budgets you can create
    - you get 2 free every month
    Cost Budget
      - how much are we spending
    Usage Budget
      - how much are we using
      - for your set architecture amounts, how much are you using?
    Reservation Budget
      - are we being efficient with our RIs?
    Savings Plans Budget
      - is what were doing covered by our savings plan?

  Console Demo
    -> AWS console -> AWS cost management -> AWS Budget -> Create a budget ->
       -> Customized (New) -> Cost Budget ->
           Set budget amount: period: monthly, fixed amount: 25
           filter: e.g tags, name: monthlyBudget,
           Alert #1: Threshold: 75% of budget amount
           email recipients: <emailAddress>
           SMS Alert <need to create SNS topic> or provide SNS ARN
           Action #1: can specify action such as stop ec2 or rds instances
           -> Create Budget

    Note: AWS Budget now has
     -  Budget Setup:
         - use a template
           - NEW: gives you for 4 budget templates (zero spend, Monthly cost, Daily
             Saving Plans Coverage, & Daily reservation utililzation)
         - Customized
            - must enable cost explorer before using
            - this gives you the for types of budgets (Cost, Usage, Reservation, & Savings pLan)

  Exam Tips

    AWS Budgets
       - can create budgets, alert on current spend or project spend
       - can budgets with tags as filters
       - can use cost explorer to create budgets
       Alerts
         Budgets are the best wasy to let users know they're getting close to overspending
       Cost Explorer
        - you can use Cost Explorer to create fine-grain budgets
       Proactive
         - it best to be proactive
       Tags
         - tag can be used to create way specific budgets

19.8 Optimining Costs With AWS Costs and Usage Reports

  Managing access to the AWS Billing Console
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/awsaccountbilling/latest/aboutv2/control-access-billing.html

  Creating Cost and Usage Reports
    https://learn.acloud.guru/course/certified-solutions-architect-associate/learn/6960ca74-1766-4151-a4dc-d36cec5cd68e/25c7ad6c-4e58-4750-ad4a-b3b4ff706871/watch

  AWS Cost and Usage Reports (CUR) Overview
     Comprehensive
       - the most comprehesive set of cost and usage data available for AWS spending
     Publish
       - publish billing reports to S3 in centralized collection
     Breakdown
       - Break costs down by the time span (hour, day, and month), service and resource,
         or by tags
     Daily CSV
       - AWS CUR updates reports in S3 budgets once a day using CSV format
     Integrations
       - Easily integrated with Athena, Redhsift, or Quicksight

  AWS Cost and Usage Reports (CUR) Use Cases
    AWS Organizations
      - use within AWS Organizations for entire OU groups or individual member accounts
    Tracking Saving Plans
      - track saving plans utilizations, charges, and current allocations
    Monitor
      - monitor on-demand capacity reservations
    AWS data transfer charges
      - break down your AWS data transfer charges (external and inter-region)
    Cost Tags
      - dive deeper into cost allocation tag resource spending

  Exam Tips

    AWS Cost and Usage Reports (CUR) Overview
       Comprehensive
         - the most comprehesive set of cost and usage data available for AWS spending
         - e.g. more comprehsive than cost explorer
       Centralized Storage
         - publish billing reports to S3 in centralized collection
       AWS Organizations
         - use within AWS Organizations at organization level, OU level, or
           member account level
       AWs Integrations
         - Query with Athena, ingest to Redhsift, or visualize with Quicksight
       Keywords Scenarios
         - mention of detailed cost breakdowns, delivery or daily usage reports,
           or tracking saving plans utilizations

19.9 Reducing Compute Spending Using Savings Plans and AWS compute Optimizer

  AWS Compute Optimizer
    Optimizes
      - Analyzes configuration and utilization metrics of your AWS resources
    Reporting
      - reports current usage optimizations and potential recommendations
    Graphs
      - provides graphical history data and project utilization metrics
    Informed Decisions
      - use graphs, metric data, and recommendations for moving or resizing resources

  AWS Compute Optimizer Resources
    EC2
    EC2 Auto Scaling Groups
    EBS
    AWS Lambda

  AWS Compute Optimizer Supported Accounts
    Standalone
      - any standalone AWS accounts without AWS organizations enabled
    Member Account
      - Single member accounts within an AWS organizations
    Management Account
      - AWS Organization management account with recommendations based the entire
        organization

  AWS Compute Optimizer Things to Know
    - Disabled by default
    - must opt in to leverage AWS compute optimizer
    - after opting in, enhance recommendations via activation of recommendations
      preference (e.g. enhanced infrastructure paid feature)

  Saving Plans
    Flexible Pricing
      - offering of flexible pricing models for up to 72% savings on compute
    Lower Prices
      - lower prices on EC2 instances regardless of instance family, size, OS,
        tenancy, or regions
    Variety
      - Savings can also apply to AWS Lambda and AWS Fargate usage
    SageMaker
      - SageMaker plans for lowering SageMaker instance pricing
      Note: Amazon SageMaker is a managed service in the AWS public cloud. It provides
            the tools to build, train and deploy machine learning (ML) models for predictive
            analytics applications.
    Commitments
      - Savings are provided as a trade for long-term commitments
      - there are one-year or 3-year pricing options
    Pricing Plan Options
      - Choose from All Upfront, Partial upfront, or no upfront

  Saving Plans Types
    Compute Savings
      - most flexible
      - applies to any EC2 compute, Lambda, or fargate usage
      - up to 66% savings on compute on-demand pricing
    EC2 instance Savings
      - Applies only to EC2 instances of a specific instance family in a specific Region
      - up to 72% savings
    SageMaker Savings
      - Applies to SageMaker instances regardless of instance family or sizing
      - any region and any component
      - up to 64% savings

  Using and Applying Saving Plans
    1. View recommendations within your AWS billing console
    2. Recommendations are automatically calculated to make purchasing easier
    3. Add to cart and purchase directly within your AWS account
    4. Apply to usage rates after Reserved Instances are applied and exhausted
    5. Consolidated Billing Family: Applied to the account owner first, then can be
       spread to others if you enable sharing

  Exam Tips

    AWS compute Optimizer
      Overview
        - provides recommendations based on collected utilization and configuration metrics
      Resources
        - works with EC2, EC2 Auto Scaling groups, AWS Lambda, and EBS
      supported Account types
        - Use for organization in management account, at member account level, or a standalone
        account

    Saving Plans
      - flexible offerings for pricing on AWS compute usage;
      - Similar to Reserver Instances
      Saving Plans Types
        - Compute Saving Plans
          - apply to EC2, Lambda, and Fargate
        - EC2 Saving Plans
        - SageMaker Saving Plans
      Saving Plans Pricing Terms
        - one or 3-year agreements
        - all upfront, partial upfront, or no upfront

19.10 Auditing With Trusted Advisor

  Trusted Advisor
    - a fully managed best-practice auditing tool
    - it will scan 5 different parts of your account and look for places where you could
      improve your adoption of the recommended best practices provided by AWS
  Trusted Advisor Supported Checks
    Cost Optimization
      - are you spending money on resources that are not needed?
    Performance
      - Are your services configured properly?
    Security
      - is your AWS architecture full of vulnerability
    Fault Tolerance
      - Are you protected when something fails?
    Service Limits
      - Do you have room to scale?
      - e.g. are you near or at your account limit for Elastic IP addresses (EIPs) so you
            should request more?

  Console Demo
    -> AWS Console -> Management & Goverance -> Trusted Advisor

    Note: Need an AWS support plan to get all Trusted Advisor checks
        e.g. Cost Optimization, Performance, & Fault Tolerance require support plan

  Exam Tips
    Trusted Advisor
      Alerts
        - Use SNS to let users know
      Costs
        - to get the most useful checks, you need a business or Enterprise support plan
      Limits
        - Trusted Advisor will not fix the problems for you, just alert you to issues
      Automate
        - Use EventBridge (CloudWatch Events) to kick off Lambda to solve the problem for you

19.11 Enforcing Account Goverance via AWS Control Tower

  AWS Control Tower
    Goverance
      - Easy way to setup and govern an AWS multi-account environment
    Orchestration
      - Automate account creation and security credentials vai other AWS services
        (e.g. Organizations, IAM, Identity Center, & AWS Config)
    Extensions
      - Extends AWS Organizations to prevent goverance drift and leverage different
        guardrails
    New AWS Accounts
      - User can provision new AWS accounts quickly, using central admin established
        compliance policies
      - using Account Factory (template for new accounts), cloud admins can monitor
        usage and make sure they are aligned with established guidelines
    Simple Terms
      - Quickest way to create and manage a secure, compliant, multi-account environment
        best on best practices

  AWS Control Tower Features and Terms to Know
    Landing Zone:
      - Well-architected, multi-account environment based on compliance and securty best practices
      - a container that holds all your organizational units, accounts in those OUs, and
        users and other resources that you want to enforce compliance on
    Guardrails:
      - high-level rules providing continuous goverance for the AWS enviroment
      - 2 types: preventive and detective
    Account Factory:
      - configurable account templates for standardizing pre-approved configs of new accounts
    CloudFornation StackSet:
      - automated deployment of templates deploying repeated rsources for goverance
    Shared Accounts:
      - Three accounts used by Control Tower, 2 of which are created during landing
        Zone creation

  AWS Control Tower Guardrails
    - high-level rules in plain language providing ongoing goverance
    - 2 types: preventive and detective
    Preventive
      - Ensures accounts maintain goverance by disallowing violating actions
      - leverages service control policies (SCPs) in AWS organizations
      - Statuses of Enforced or not enabled
      - supported in all regions
    Detective
      - detects and alerts on non-compliant resources within all accounts
      - leverages AWS Config Rules
      - Statuses of clear, in violation, or not enabled
      - only apply in certain Regions (regions supported by Control Tower)

  AWS Control Tower Example Diagram

     |---------------------|       |------------------|       |--------------|
     |  Management Account |       |   Log Archive    |       |    Audit     |
     |                     |       |   Account        |       |   Account    |
     |   Organizations     |       |                  |       |              |
     |                     |       |      S3          |       |              |
     |    Control Tower    |       |                  |       |              |
     |---------------------|       |------------------|       |--------------|
             |           |
            Preventive   Detective
            GuardRails   Guardrails
             |           |
             v           v
        |-----------------------------------------------------------------|
        ||-----------------|     |-----------------|     |--------------| |
   Team ||  Org Account 1  |     | Org Account 2   |     |Org Account 3 | |
   OU   ||                 |     |                 |     |              | |
        ||                 |     |                 |     |              | |
        ||-----------------|     |-----------------|     |--------------| |
        |-----------------------------------------------------------------|

      Control Tower
         - creates AWS Log Archive account and Audit Accounts
         - Management, Log Archive, & Audit are the 3 Shared accounts
         - places preventive Guardrail rules in each Team OU account using SCPs
         - places dectective Guardrail rules in each Team OU account using AWS Config
         - set up AWS CloudTrail logging in all Team OU accounts
         - set up Goverance Notifications in all Team OU accounts
         - any goverance notifications that occur will be sent to the audit account
            - all configuration events, aggregate security notifications, and drift
              notifications are sent to audit account
              - they go to an SNS topic in the audit account
         - AWS config and AWS Cloudtrail send logs to the Log Archive S3 bucket


  Exam Tips
     AWS Control Tower
       - orchestrates multiple AWS services on your behalf while maintaining the security and
         compliance needs of your organization.
       Goverance
         - easy way to set up and maintain goverance in multi-AWS account environments
       Accounts
         - Automated account deployment with preconfigured compliance rules
       3 Shared Accounts
         - Management account, log archive account, and audit account
       Preventive Guardrails
         - leverages service control policies (SCPs) in AWS organizations to prevent
           non-compliant actions
       Detective Guardrails
         - leverages AWS Config Rules to detect and alert on violating actions or changes
       Keywords and Scenarios
         - automated multi-account goverance, guardrails, account orchestraction, and
           governed user account provisioning

19.12 Managing Software Licenses in AWS with AWS License Manager

  AWS License Manager
    Licenses Made Easy
    - Simplifies managing software licenses with different vendors (e.g.: Microsoft,
      SAP, and Oracle)
    Centralized
      - Helps centrally manage licenses across AWS accounts and on-premise environments
    Set Usage Limits
      - Control and visibility into usage of licenses and enable license usage limits
    Reduce Overages
      - reduces overages and penalities via inventory tracking and rule-based
        controls for consumption
    Versatile
      - supports any software based on vCPU, physical cores, sockets, and number of
        machines

  Exam Tips
    License Manager
      - AWS service making license management simplier and more efficient
      Keywords an Scenarios
        - AWS-Hosted License management
        - hybrid environment license management
        - preventing license abuse

19.13 Monitoring Health Events in the AWS Personal Health Dashboard

  AWS Pesonal Health Dashboard (now: AWS Health) Overview
    AWS Health
      - shifting from AWS Personal Health Dashboard to AWS Health in documenation
    Visibility
      - gain visibility of resource performance and availability of AWS services or accounts
    How it affects you
      - view how the health events affect you and your services, resources, and accounts
    Timely
      - AWS attempts to maintain timeliness and relevant information with the events
    Be Prepared and Automate
      - view upcoming maintenance tasks that may affect your account and resources
        and remediate before they cause any issues
    Alerts
      - it has near-instant delivery of notifications and alerts to speed up troubleshooting
        or prevention
    Pro tip: Automate actions based on incoming events using EventBridge to kick off the
             automation (e.g. connected to SNS topic, Lambda function, ...)

  AWS Health Concepts
    AWS Health Events
      - Notifications sent on behalf of AWS services or AWS
    Account-specific Events
      - Events specific to your AWS account or AWS organizations
    Public Events
      - Events reported on services that are public, not specific to accounts
    AWS Health Dashboard
      - Dashboard showing account and public events, shows service health as well
    Event Type Code
      - include the affected service and the specific type of event
    Event Type Category
      - Associated category - will be attached to every event
    Event Status
      - reports if event is open, closed, or upcoming [maintenance task]
    Affected Entities
      - Which AWS resources are or may be affected by the event

  AWS Health Event Examples
    1. AWS_ECS_SYSTEM_REBOOT_MAINENANCE
      - hardware running on EC2 instance in your account needs to be rebooted for
        system maintenance
      - to remediate, you could stop and restart the instance
    2. AWS_ECS_OPERATIONAL_ISSUE
      - indicates some type of AWS service operation issue
      - it could be delays in service response times or downtimes
    3. AWS_ECS_SYSTEM_REBOOT_MAINENANCE
      - an AWS account has outstanding charges
      - it's at risk of suspenion or closure

  Exam Tips

    AWS Pesonal Health Dashboard (now: AWS Health)
      Health Visibility
        - gain visibility into service and resource health of your AWS resources
          and accounts
      Alerts
        - receive notifications and alerts for affected resources and upcoming events
      Automate
        - Automate actions based on events using Eventbridge
      Account-Specific and Public Events
        - you receive public and account-specific events
      Keywords and Scenarios
        - look out for questions about checking alerts for service health and automating
          the reboot of EC2 instances for AWS maintenance

19.14 Standardizing Deployments Using Service Catalog and AWS Proton

  AWS Service Catalog Overview
    Catalogs
      - Allows organizations to create and manage catalogs of approved IT services
    Multipurpose Catalogs
      - List of things like AMIs, servers, software, databases, and other preconfigured
        components
    Centralized
      - AWS Organizations can centralize manage IT services and maintain compliance
    End-User-Friendly
      - End users can be allowed to easily deploy preapproved catalog items within an
        organization
    CloudFormation
      - Catalog Templates are written and listed using CloudFormation templates
      - Catalog templates are just preapproved CloudFormation templates uploaded as
        catalog items for easy deployment

  AWS Service Catalog Benefits
    Standardized
      - restrict launching products to a specific list of preapproved solutions
    Self-Service
      - end users can browse products and deploy approved services on their own
    Access Control
      - Add contraints and grant access to products using AWS IAM
    Versioning
      - update products to newer versions and propagate changes automatically

  AWS Proton
    - a service that creates and manages infrastructure and deployment tooling for users
      as well as serverless and container-based applications
    1. Automate Infrastructure as Code (IaC) provinging and deployment
    2. Define standardized infrastructure for your serverless and container-based apps
    3. Use templates to define manage app stacks that contain all components
    4. AWS Proton automatically provisions resources, configures CI/CD pipelins,
       and deploys the code for you
    5. Supports AWS CloudFormation and Terraform IaC providers

  Exam Tips

    AWS Service Catalogs
      - AWS Service Catalogs provides catalogs of preapproved services as CloudFormation
        templates
      - AWS Service can be used to create CloudFormation templates for various types of uses
        cases /workloads. Ex: Database Server Product , WebServer Product , JEE App Server
        Product , Proxy Server Product etc..
      End Users
        - allows end users to deploy the approved services into their own accounts

    AWS Proton
      - a fully managed application deployment service for containers and serverless applications.
      Standardized Infrastructure
        - AWS Proton offers Infrastruture as Code (IaC) provisioning and deployment of
          serverless/container architectures
      Empowers Developers
        - Enables developers to move faster with self-service tool to provision infrastructure
          and manage code deployment

  AWS Well-Architected Framework Review
    The 6 Well-Architected Pillars
      1. Operational Excellence
      2. Reliability
      3. Security
      4. Performance Efficiency
      5. Cost Optimization
      6. Sustainability

  AWS Well-Architected Tool
    1. Provides a consistent process for measuring cloud architectures
      - measuring against AWS Best practices
    2. Enables assistance and documentiont workloads and architectures
    3. Guides for making workloads reliable, secure, efficient, and cost effective
    4. Measurce workloads against years of AWS best practices
    5. Intended for specific audiences such as technical teams, CTO, architecture,
       and operation teams

  Exam Tips

        - tool used for measuring current workload against established AWS best practices
