19.16 Goverance Exam Tips

  Identity and access management for IAM Identity Center (formerly SSO [Single Sign-On])
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/singlesignon/latest/userguide/iam-auth-access.html

  Getting Started with Cognito
    https://aws.amazon.com/cognito/getting-started/

  AWS Managed Microsoft AD
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/directoryservice/latest/admin-guide/directory_microsoft_ad.html

  Active Directory Connector
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/directoryservice/latest/admin-guide/directory_microsoft_ad.html

  IAM Tutorial: Delegate access across AWS accounts using IAM roles
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/tutorial_cross-account-with-roles.html

  What are AWS Cost and Usage Reports
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cur/latest/userguide/what-is-cur.html

  SNS FAQs
    https://aws.amazon.com/sns/faqs/

  Trusted Advisor and AWS Personal Health Dashboards FAQs
    https://aws.amazon.com/premiumsupport/faqs/?nc=sn&loc=6

  Tutorial: Schedule AWS Lambda functions using EventBridge
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/eventbridge/latest/userguide/eb-run-lambda-schedule.html


    4 Questions to Ask:

      1. can it be centralized?
         - Can AWS RAM be used to share resources through Organizations?
           Resource Access Manager (RAM)
            - a free service that allows you to share AWS resources with other accounts and within
              your organization (e.g. sharing networking space with other accounts)
         - should you use Control Tower to centralize multi-account goverance and deployment?
           AWS Control Tower
             - orchestrates multiple AWS services on your behalf while maintaining the security and
               compliance needs of your organization.
      2. How do we standardize?
        - can we leverage Control Tower guardrails for standardizing our infrastructure deployments and
          account deployment?
          AWS Control Tower Guardrails:
            Preventive Guardrails
              - leverages service control policies (SCPs) in AWS organizations to prevent
                non-compliant actions
            Detective Guardrails
              - leverages AWS Config Rules to detect and alert on violating actions or changes
      3. How do we enforce the standards?
         - We can use Service Control Polices (SCPs), AWS Config rules, AWS Control Tower Guardrails
           to enforce standards and AWS CloudTrail for auditing our history of API Actions
            Organizations Service Control Policies (SCPs)
              SCP
                - once implemented, SCPs will be applied to every single resource inside an account
                - they are the ultimate way to restrict permissions, and even apply to the root
                  account
                - uses the same JSON structure as an IAM policy but applied to an account
                - SCPs never give your permissions, they only take permissions away
                - allows and denies
                   allows: if using allow, only specified services/action/resources are allowed
                           resulting in implicitly denying for all other services/actions/resources
                           (e.g. if SCP allows ec2 and S3, all other AWS services are
                           implicitly denied)
                   denies: denies the explicitly specified action/resource/service
            AWS Config
              - an inventory management and control tool
                - can tell you what you have inside your AWS account include state of the resources
              - it allows you to show the history of your infrastructure along with creating
                rules to make sure it conforms to the best practices you've laid out
              - not a free service
      4. Are the users internal or external? Either way, how do we want to grant them access
         to AWS resources and accounts?

    SCP (Service Control Policies)
      - have the ultimate say as to whether an API call goes through
      - they are the only way to restrict what the root account can do
      - once implemented, SCPs will be applied to every single resource inside an account
      - they are the ultimate way to restrict permissions, and even apply to the root
         account
      - uses the same JSON structure as an IAM policy but applied to an account

    Organizations
      Control
        - SCPs are the best way to have the final say in what you can do in your AWS account
      Cost
        - Billings easily roll up to a single account for payment
      CloudTrail & Centralized Logs
        - a lot AWS services (e.g. CloudTrail) have integration with Organizations
        - you can designate a single AWS account to hold your logs
      Sharing Reserved Instances
        - RIs can be shared across accounts
        - this allows you to have one "billing" account to hold the RIs
        - you can turn off the RI sharing
      Isolating Workloads
        - isolating workloads into separate accounts is a great way to add more layers
          of security and controls. Avoid answers that lump everything together
        - examples: have a dev account, test account, product account for the same
          application

    AWS Config
      - exam questions about enforcing standards, think Config
         - examples: ensure your S3 buckets are not public, ec2 instances are using
           approved AMIs, EBS volumes are encrypted, etc
      Standards
        - Config is the best way to check what standards are applied to your architecture
        - anytime a "rule" needs to be set up for an account, think about AWS Config to
          check for compliance via AWS Config predefined customizable Rules which can evaluate
          whether your AWS resources comply with common best practices
      Automate the Response
        - Config offers the ability to automatically remediate problems using Automation
          documents or Lambda functions
        - Config just does the detection, but it can trigger workloads to do the remediation
      Know what Changed
        - AWS Config is the one-stop shop to see what changed. It provides you with a history
          of all your architecture
        - you can track previously deleted resources using Config
      Enforcements
        - you can use Automation documents or Lambda to enforce your standards
      Consolidation
        - you can roll up all your results to a single region

    Authentication
      User Management requires the right tool
        - use AWS SSO (Single Sign-ON) for internal user management
        - use Cognito for external user management
      Active Directory
        - should make you think AWS Directory Service
        - If it's a lift and shift, pick Managed Microsoft AD
        - if AD is staying on-premise, select AD Connector
          AWS Directory Service [also known as "AWS Managed Microsoft AD]
             - a fully managed version of Active Directory
      Cross-Account Roles access
        - using cross-account roles is always better solution than creating unnecessary IAM credentials

    AWS IAM Identity Center (formerly  AWS Single Sign-On [SSO])
      https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2022/07/aws-single-sign-on-aws-sso-now-aws-iam-identity-center/
       - create, or connect, your workforce users once and centrally manage their access to multiple
         AWS accounts and applications.
       - You can create user identities directly in IAM Identity Center, or you can connect your
         existing identity source, including Microsoft Active Directory and standards-based identity
         providers, such as Okta Universal Directory or Azure AD.
       - You can choose to manage access just to AWS accounts, just to cloud applications, or to both.
       - Your users can utilize their existing credentials for one-click access to their assigned AWS
         accounts, AWS applications, like Amazon SageMaker Studio, and other standards-based cloud
         applications, like Salesforce, Box, and Microsoft 365.

    Cognito Exam Tips
      user pools
        - directories of users that provide sign-up and sign-in options for users of your application
      identity pools
        - allow your users to access to other AWS services
      user pools + identity pools
        - you can use identity pools and user pools either separately or together
      Post Authentication Lambda trigger
        - Immediately after Amazon Cognito signs in a user, it optionally activates the post authentication Lambda function.
        - You can also create an AWS Lambda function to make an API call to a custom analytics solution (e.g. to log user
          sign-in events) and then invoke that function by using an Amazon Cognito post authentication trigger.


     Cognito Sequence

                        |----------------------|
              |<----------> user pool          |     1. Authentication and get token
              |         |                      |
              |<----------> Post Authentication|     1a. (Optionally) Post Authentication Lambda trigger
       device |         |                      |
              |<----------> identity pool      |     2. Exchange token and get AWS Credentials
              |         |                      |
              |<----------> AWS Services       |     3. Access AWS services using credentials
                        |    s3   dynamoDB     |
                        |                      |
                        |----------------------|


   Cost Management
      Tracking Cost
        - use a combination of tags, Cost Explorer and AWS Budgets
      Proactive Alerts
        - get ahead of problem by creating proactive alerts
        - When users get to 80% threshold, tell someone via SNS
      Automating Reponses
        - spending too much money? Shut something down in automated manner.
        - think about you can remove the human interaction
      Detailed Reports and exploring costs
        - these questions usually involve AWS Cost and Usage Reports or Cost Explorer for
          in-depth reporting purposes
          - AWS cost and Usage Reports are delivered daily
          - AWS Cost Explorer gives you an in-depth process to explore those costs
      AWS Compute Optimizer
         - use to generate recommendations on implementing more accurately sized compute based on
           your actual needs

    Cost Explorer
      easy-to-use tool that allows you to visualize your cloud costs
      - you can generate reports based on a variety of factors include resource tags
      Cost Explorer Features
        - lets you build  reports
        Service
          - easily break down costs on a service-by-service basis
        Time
          - reports expense by monthly time frames (last month, last 3 months, last year)
          - estimates expenses going forward based on previous month, utilizations, etc.
        Filter
          - filter by tags, by categories, by region, by instance type, by service, etc.

    AWS Budgets
      - allows organizations to easily plan and set expectations around cloud costs
      - you can easily track your ongoing spend and create alarms to let users know when
        they are close to exceeding their alloted spend

      Types of AWS Budgets
        - 4 types of budgets you can create
        - you get 2 free every month
        Cost Budget
          - how much are we spending
        Usage Budget
          - how much are we using
          - for your set architecture amounts, how much are you using?
        Reservation Budget
          - are we being efficient with our RIs?
        Savings Plans Budget
          - is what were doing covered by our savings plan?


    AWS Cost and Usage Reports (CUR) Overview
       Comprehensive
         - the most comprehesive set of cost and usage data available for AWS spending
         - e.g. more comprehsive than cost explorer
       Centralized Storage
         - publish billing reports to S3 in centralized collection
       AWS Organizations
         - use within AWS Organizations at organization level, OU level, or
           member account level
       AWs Integrations
         - Query with Athena, ingest to Redhsift, or visualize with Quicksight
       Keywords Scenarios
         - mention of detailed cost breakdowns, delivery or daily usage reports,
           or tracking saving plans utilizations

    AWS compute Optimizer
      Overview
        - provides recommendations based on collected utilization and configuration metrics
      Resources
        - works with EC2, EC2 Auto Scaling groups, AWS Lambda, and EBS
      supported Account types
        - Use for organization in management account, at member account level, or a standalone
        account


    Trusted Advisor
      - it free to use, but you'll need a Business or Enterprize Support plan to get the most
        useful checks
      Alerts
        - Use SNS to let users know
      Costs
        - to get the most useful checks, you need a business or Enterprise support plan
      Limits
        - it strickly an auditing and reporting tool, and it won't solve the problem for you
        - Trusted Advisor will not fix the problems for you, just alert you to issues
      Automate
        - Use EventBridge (CloudWatch Events) to kick off Lambda to solve the problem for you

    Accounts and Licenses
      AWS Control Tower
        - can be used to implement compliance and account goverance withing multi-account
          environments using automated accounts setups
      AWS Control Tower leverage preventive and detective guardrails
        - preventive guardrails are implemented via SCPs (Service Control Policies)
        - detective guardrails are implemented via AWS Config [rules]
      AWS License Manager
        - provides a simplified way to manage licenses from supported vendors to prevent license
          abuse and overcharges
        - it works in AWS and on-premise
        - Simplifies managing software licenses with different vendors (e.g.: Microsoft,
          SAP, and Oracle)

    Infrastructure and Deployments
      AWS Service Catalog
        - allows end users to provision pre-approved products and services via shared catalog
          portfolios.
        - Catalogs are written in CloudFormation and deployed in their own accounts
      AWS Proton
        - can empower developers to automate the provisioning of their entiry application stack
          for container-based or serverless architectures
        - it can do the code itself, infrastructure, CI/CD pipeline, and deployment


    AWS Service Catalogs
      - AWS Service Catalogs provides catalogs of preapproved services as CloudFormation
        templates
      - AWS Service can be used to create CloudFormation templates for various types of uses
        cases /workloads. Ex: Database Server Product , WebServer Product , JEE App Server
        Product , Proxy Server Product etc..
      End Users
        - allows end users to deploy the approved services into their own accounts

    AWS Proton
      - a fully managed application deployment service for containers and serverless applications.
      Standardized Infrastructure
        - AWS Proton offers Infrastruture as Code (IaC) provisioning and deployment of
          serverless/container architectures
      Empowers Developers
        - Enables developers to move faster with self-service tool to provision infrastructure
          and manage code deployment

      What kind of architectural elements can AWS Proton manage?
        - You can use AWS Proton to manage and coordinate any element that can be provisioned and described
          with existing infrastructure-as-code tools, including AWS CloudFormation, Terraform, and
          AWS Cloud Development Kit.
      How is AWS Proton different from other tools like CloudFormation or Service Catalog?
       - AWS Proton is a deployment workflow tool for modern applications. It can be used to manage Infrastructure
         as Code (IaC) templates build using tools like CloudFormation or Terraform. By comparison, Service Catalog
         is a catalog of AWS resources that enables customers to store, share, and govern Infrastructure-as-code
         templates and create individual stacks, for example for specific building blocks like an S3 bucket product.
         Some AWS customers have used AWS Proton to manage architectures that include Service Catalog product components

    AWS Well-Architected Tool (AWS WA Tool)
      - tool used for measuring current workload against established AWS best practices
      Documentation
        - aids in documentation of workload and architecture decisions


    AWS Health (formerly: AWS Pesonal Health Dashboard)
      - a dashboard and service meant to provide notifications of both public and account-specific
        events within AWS
      Health Visibility
        - gain visibility into service and resource health of your AWS resources
          and accounts
      Alerts
        - receive notifications and alerts for affected resources and upcoming events
      Automate
        - Automate actions based on events using Eventbridge and AWS Lambda
      Account-Specific and Public Events
        - you receive public and account-specific events
      Keywords and Scenarios
        - look out for questions about checking alerts for service health and automating
          the reboot of EC2 instances for AWS maintenance

19.17 Using Tags and Resource Groups

About this lab

To simplify the management of AWS resources such as EC2 instances, you can assign metadata using tags.
Resource groups can then use these tags to automate tasks on large numbers of resources at one time.
They serve as a unique identifier for custom automation, to break out cost reporting by department,
and much more. In this hands-on lab, you will explore tag restrictions and best practices for tagging
strategies. You will also get experience with the Tag Editor, AWS resource group basics, and leveraging
automation through the use of tags.

Learning objectives
  - Set Up AWS Config
  - Tag an AMI and EC2 Instance
  - Tag Applications with the Tag Editor
  - Create Resource Groups and Use AWS Config Rules for Compliance

20.1 Migrating Data with AWS Snow Family

  How Do we Migrate Data
     Internet
       - using your existing connection is convenient, but potentially slow and could be
         a security risk
     Direct Connect
       - can be faster and more secure, but it's not always practical if it's not needed
         after the migration
     Physical
       - bundle your data and physically deliver it to AWS using an enclosure provided by AWS

  Snow Family
    - a set of secure appliances that provide petabyte-scan data collection and processing
      solutions at teh edge and migrate large-scale data into and out of AWS
    - Some offer built-in computing capabilities, enable customers to run their operations in
      remote locations that do not have data center access or reliable network connectivity

  Snowcone
    - 8TB of storage 4 GB of memory, and 2 vCPUs
    - easily migrate data to AWS after you've processed it
    - use case: remote location [non-data center] data transfer
    - IoT sensor integration
    - perfect for edge computing when space and power are contrained

  Snowball Edge
    - 50 TB (42 TB usable) US regions only and 80 (72 TB usable) in storage
    - storage, compute, and GPU flavors
    - varying amount of CPU and RAM
    - perfect for off-the-grid computing or a migration to AWS

  Snowmobile
    - semi-truck that drives up to your data center
    - 100 PB of storage
    - Designed for exabyte-scale data center migration

  Exam Tips

    Migrating Data to AWS
      Exam Scenario:
         - pay attention to restriction such as not being transfer data over the internet,
           large amount of data, slow internet connection, or data needs to be encrypted
           at reset and in transit could be reasons to use Snowball

    Snow Family
      - use cases
        - know the general use cases for each snow family member
      Snowcone
        - small, robust edge computing
        - 8 TB of storage, 4 GB of memory, and 2 vCPUs
      Snowball
        - Storage
        - 50 TB (42 TB usable) US regions only and 80 (72 TB usable) in storage
      Snowball Edge
        - Storage, Compute, and GPU
        - 100 TB (80 TB usable) in storage
      Snowmobile
        - up to 100 PB in a very large truck
      Data transfer to and from AWS
        - The Snow Family works to get data into AWS, as well as shipped back to you
      Timing
        - generally, turnaround is a week, but that mostly depends on the customer

20.2 Storage Gateway

   Storage Gateway FAQs
     https://aws.amazon.com/storagegateway/faqs/
   Storage Gateway Use Cases
     https://aws.amazon.com/storagegateway/#Use_cases
   What is Storage Gateway
     https://aws.amazon.com/storagegateway/#Use_cases
   What is Amazon S3 File Gateway
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/filegateway/latest/files3/what-is-file-s3.html
   Creating a Volume Gateway
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/storagegateway/index.html
   Creating a Tape Gateway
     https://docs.aws.amazon.com/storagegateway/index.html

   Storage Gateway
     - a hybrid cloud storage service that helps you merge on-premisie resources with the cloud
     - It can help with one-time migration or a long-term pairing of your architecture with AWS
     Types: File Gateway, Volume Gateway, & Tape Gateway

   File Gateway
     - caching local files
     - NFS or SMB mount
     - helps with migrations to AWS
     - VMs that you run on-premise
     Different Versions:
       1. back all your data to S3 and it justs act as this backup data gateway
       2. keep a local cached copy of recently used files on-premise and everything else on S3
         - extend on-premise storage

        On-Premises                    AWS Cloud
     |------------------|         |---------------------|
     | File Gateways --------------------> AWS Storage  |
     |                  | HTTPS   |  |     Gateway      |
     |    NFS/          |         |  |                  |
     |    SMB           |         |  |---> S3           |
     |                  |         |         |           |
     |------------------|         |         |           |
                                  |      lifecycle      |
                                  |         |           |
                                  | Any S3 -|           |
                                  | Storage Class       |
                                  |---------------------|


   Volume Gateway
     - backup drives
     - iSCSI Mount (backing up disks that the VMs are currently write to / reading from)
     - cached or stored mode
     - Can Create EBS snapshots and restore volumes
     - perfect for backup or migration

        On-Premises                    AWS Cloud
     |------------------|         |----------------------|
     |                  |         |  |------------------||
     | Volume Gateway -------------->|AWS Storage -- S3 ||
     |    |             | HTTPS   |  |Gateway        |  ||
     |    |iSCSI        |         |  |---------------|--||
     |    |             |         |                  |   |
     | Application      |         |                  |   |
     | Server           |         |  AWS  --------- EBS  |
     |------------------|         |  Backup              |
                                  |                      |
                                  |----------------------|
   Tape Gateway
     - ditch the tapes
     - replace physical tapes
     - doesn't change current workflow
     - encrypted communication


        On-Premises                    AWS Cloud
     |------------------|         |--------------------------|
     |                  |         | |-----------------------||
     | Tape Gatewayu  ------------->| AWS Storage -- Tape   ||
     |    |             | HTTPS   | | Gateway        library||
     |    |iSCSI        |         | |                       ||
     |    | VTL         |         | |                S3     ||
     | Backup           |         | |                |      ||
     | Server           |         | |Tape Archive ---|      ||
     |------------------|         | |S3 Glacier /           ||
                                  | |S3 Glacier Deep Archive||
                                  | |-----------------------||
                                  |--------------------------|


   Exam Tips
      Storage Gateway
        - Storage Gateway excels at being a Hybrid Storage solution for data migration
        Use cases for File Gateway, Volume Gateway, and Tape Gateway
          - know the general use cases for each storage gateway type
          Storage Gateway
          - e.g. NFS service is out of on-premises storage, you can use storage gateway
          Volume Gateway
            - back up drives
          Tape Gateway
            - replace tape backups

20.3 AWS DataSync

  How AWS Datasync Works
    https://docs.aws.amazon.com/datasync/latest/userguide/how-datasync-works.html#terminology
  AWS Datasync FAQs
    https://aws.amazon.com/datasync/faqs/

  DataSync
    - an agent-based solution for migrating on-premises storage to AWS
    - it allows you to easily move data between NFS and SMB shares to AWS storage solutions
    - data migration tool for 1 time migration
    - all encrypted
    - can be stored in S3 buckets, EFS File System, or FSx for Windows File Server

  Using DataSync

         On-Premises                     AWS Region
     |---------------------------|     |-------------------------|
     |                           |     |         |--------------||
     | NFS, SMB, S3 API          |     |         | AWS Storage  ||
     |                           |     |         | Resources    ||
     | On-premises <--- DataSync |<--- | Data -->|              ||
     |  Server     ----> Agent   |---->| Sync <--|  S3 Bucket   ||
     |                           |     |         |              ||
     |---------------------------|     |         |  EFS File    ||
                                       |         |      System  ||
                                       |         |              ||
                                       |         |  FSx for     ||
                                       |         |  Windows File||
                                       |         |  Server      ||
                                       |         |--------------||
                                       |-------------------------|
  Exam Tips

    DataSync
      - is a data migration service
      DataSync vs Storage Gateways
      - dataSync is for a one-time migration
      - for hybrid architecture and a continuous sync, think Storage Gateway
      -  Note: Storage migration can be used for a 1 time migration
      Datasync Use Case
        - primarily for one-time migrations
      Agent Based
        - an agent needs to be installed on the architecture on your end to copy the data to AWS
      Datasync Endpoints (for sending data to)
        - S3, EFS, and FSx are all supported locations for your data

20.4 AWS Transfer Family

  AWS Transfer Family
    - allows you to easily move files in and out S3 or EFS using Secure File Transfer
      Protocol (SFTP), File Transfer Protocol over SSL (FTSP), or File Transfer Protocol (FTP)

  How does AWS transfer Family work
     - keep the endpoints that the legacy applications are using but instead read/write from
       S3 or EFS
     - supports SFTP and FTPS from outside AWS in to S3 and EFS
     - Only supports FTP transfers from internal from inside your VPC for security reasons
       (FTP is just not secure)

                           |-------------------------------------------------------------|
                      |->  | AWS Transfer for SFTP       |--->  S3                       |
                      |    |                             |      transferred files stored |
    Legacy Users   ---|->  | AWS Transfer for FTPS ------|      natively has objects     |
                      |    |                             |                               |
                      |->  | AWS Transfer for FTP        |--->   EFS                     |
                           | - Only supported within the         Transferred files       |
                           |   VPC and not over public internet  stored natively         |
                           |-------------------------------------------------------------|
  Exam Tips

     AWS Transfer Family
       - excels when you have a collection of older applications using protocols that cannot
         be changed. You can easily "trick" these applications into using S3 or EFS storage
       - FTP/SFTP/FTPS related, think Transfer Family
       Legacy
         - this tool excels with bringing legacy application storage to the cloud
       Protocols
         - FTPS and SFTP from outside AWS
         - FTP only from inside your VPC
       DNS
         - the DNS (endpoints) stays the same, but the location for the storage becomes S3

20.5 Moving to the Cloud with Migration Hub

  Migration Hub
    - gives you a single place to track the progress of your application migration to AWS
    - It integrates with Server Migration Service (SMS) and Database Migration Service (DMS)
    - GUI based Service that lets you record, schedule, track and manage SMS and DMS

  Server Migration Service (SMS)
    - converts your VM architectures to AMIs
    - VMDK (Virtual Machine Disk) -> EBS Snapshot -> Creates AMI -> Launching EC2s

  Database Migration Service (DMS)
    - Oracle Database (on-premises, EC2, or RDS) -> AWS Schema Conversion Tool -> Aurora or RDS Database
    - SQL Databases (on-premises, EC2, or RDS) -> AWS Schema Conversion Tool -> Aurora or RDS Database
    - AWS Schema Tool primary job is to convert Oracle Databases and SQL Server Databases and convert them
      to Aurora Databases

  Console Demo
    -> AWS Console -> Migration & Transfer -> Migration Hub
      -> Options: Get started with Discovery OR Get started Migrating
           Discovery Steps:
             1 import data or use use AWS discovery tools
             2. View discovered servers
             3. Group Servers as applications
           Migrate Steps:
             4. Connect migration tools to Migration Hub
             5. Migrate using the connected migration tools
           Track
             6. Track status of Migration

         -> Discovery Tools
           - Import
             - Use import template to upload data about servers and applications (group of servers)
               from existing data sources
           - Discovery Connector (replaced with Agentless Collector)
             - Deploy an agentless Discovery connector in each VMware vCenter
             - connectors collect specification and summarized performance about your VMs
           - Discovery Agent
               - install discovery agent on each of your VMs or physical servers
               - discovery agent collect running processes, packages installed, and network connections
                 besides specifications and summarized perfornance

  Exam Tips
     Database Migration Service (DMS)
       - used to migrate databases
       - Oracle Database (on-premises, EC2, or RDS) -> AWS Schema Conversion Tool -> Aurora or RDS Database
       - SQL Database (on-premises, EC2, or RDS) -> AWS Schema Conversion Tool -> Aurora or RDS Database
       - AWS Schema Tool primary job is to convert Oracle Databases and SQL Server Databases and convert them
         to Aurora Databases
     Server Migration Service (SMS)
       - used for moving VM servers to AWS
       - converts your VM architectures to AMIs
       - VMDK (Virtual Machine Disk) -> EBS Snapshot -> Creates AMI -> Launching EC2s
     Migration Hub
       - gives you the overview you need [for DMS, SMS, etc]


     Migration Tools
       Magic
         - on exam, these tools will solve all application migration needs
       Schema Conversion Tool
         - used to convert SQL and Oracle databases to Aurora or RDS
         - Note: can also be used to convert data wharehouse database to redshift
       Oracle Pump
         - There are many tools for migrating an Oracle database to an Amazon RDS for Oracle database.
           The most commonly used tool is Oracle Data Pump

20.6 Migrating Workloads to AWS Using AWS Application Discovery Service or AWs Application Migration Service

  AWS Application Discovery Service
    Service Overview
      Plan
        - helps plan migration to AWS via collection of usage and configuration data from on-premises servers
      Simplify
        - integrates with Migration Hub, simplifying migration and tracking migration statuses
      Track
        - helps you view discovered servers, group them by application, and track each application migration

  AWS Application Discovery Service Types
    - offers 2 types of discovery
    Agentless
      - You install the Agentless Collector as a virtual machine (VM) in your VMware vCenter Server environment
        using an Open Virtualization Archive (OVA) file.
      - Agentless collector is an OVA (Open Virtual Appliance) file [used to store various files associated with a VM]
         within the VMWare vCenter
      - Once you deploy the VM/OVA, it:
         - Identifies hosts and VMs in vCenter
         - tracks and collect IP and MAC addresses, resource allocations [memory and cpus], and hostnames
         - collects utilization data metrics [peak CPU, RAM, and disk I/O]
       What is an OVA file:
         https://www.lifewire.com/ova-file-4144357
    Agent Based
       - Deploy AWS Application Discovery Agent
       - install Agent on each of your VMs and each physical server
       - installer for Windows and Linux
       - installer collects static config data, time-series performance info, network connections, and OS processes

  AWS Application Migration Service (AWS MGN)
    Lift and Shift
      - automated lift-and-shift service for expediting migration of apps to AWS
    Flexible
      - used for physical, virtual, or cloud servers to avoid cutover windows or disruptions
    Replicate
      Replicates source servers into AWS, and automatically convers and launches on AWS to migrate quickly

  AWS MGN RTO (Recovery Time Objective) and RPO (Recovery Point Objective)
     RTO
       - recovery time objective: typically just minutes; dependent on OS boot time
       Note: RTO is the duration of time and a service level within which a business process must be restored after a
           disaster in order to avoid unacceptable consequences associated with a break in continuity. In other words,
           the RTO is the answer to the question: "How much time did it take to recover after notification of business
           process disruption?"
     RPO
       - recovery point objective: measured in the sub-second range
       Note: RPO describes the interval of time that might pass during a disruption before the quantity of data lost
            during that period exceeds the Business Continuity Plan’s maximum allowable threshold or "tolerance."

  Exam Tips

    AWS Application Discovery Service
      Application Migration
        - to easily and quickly migrate applications to the AWS cloud
      Agentless Discovery
        - You install the Agentless Collector as a virtual machine (VM) in your VMware vCenter Server environment
          using an Open Virtualization Archive (OVA) file.
      Agent-Based Discovery
        - installable agent for windows and linux to collect detailed information on the VMs and physical host


    AWS Application Migration Service [AWS MGN]
      Lift and Shift
        - automated lift-and-shift service for expediting migration of apps to AWS
        Replication
        - replicates source servers (VMs, physical, or cloud servers) into AWS for non-distruptive cutovers
       RTO (Recovery Time Objective) and RPO (Recovery Point Objective)
         - recovery time objective: typically just minutes; dependent on OS boot time
         - recovery point objective: measured in the sub-second range

---------------------------------------------------
20.7 Migrating Databases from On-premise to AWS with AWS Database Migration Service (AWS DMS)

  AWS Database Migration Service Overview
    Migration Tools
      - allows for easy migration of relational databases, data warehouses, NoSQL databases, and other
        data stores to AWS
    Cloud or On-premise
      - migrate data between AWS and on-premise - either into or out of AWS
      - one of the endpoints must be in AWS so either to AWS or from AWS
    One-time or Ongoing
      - you have the option to perform a one-time migration or continuously replicate ongoing changes
    Conversion Tools
      - the Schema Conversion Tool (SCT) allows for translating database schemas to new platform
    AWS Benefits
      - Since it is a cloud service, you gain the advantages of AWS: cost, efficiency, security, and more

  AWS Database Migration Service - how it works
    1. DMS is a server running replication software
    2. Create a source and target connection for loading from and to
    3. Schedule tasks to run on the DMS server to move data
    4. AWS creates the tables and the primary keys (if they do not exist on the target)
    5. Optionally, you can create your target tables beforehand, if desired
    6. Leveage the SCT for creating some or all of your tables, indexes, and more
    7. Source and target data stores are referred to as endpoints [within in this service]

  AWS Database Migration Service - important concepts
    migrate to Same Engine
      - Easily migrate between source and target endpoints with the same engine types
        (e.g. on-premise MySQL to RDS MySQL)
    migrate to Different Engines
      - Easily migrate between source and target endpoints with the different engine types
        (e.g. on-premise Oracle to RDS PostgreSQL)
    AWS Endpoint
      - You must have one endpoint live within an AWS service
      - It cannot be used exclusively outside of AWS

  AWS Schema Conversion Tool (SCT)
    Convert
      - Leverage the SCT to convert existing database schemas from one engine type to another engine type
    Engine Types
      - convert many types of relational databases, including OLAP (OnLine Analyical Processing)
        [use data warehouse databases - e.g. redshift] and OLTP (OnLine Transaction Processing)
        [use relational databases - e.g. RDS]
      - It even supports data warehouses
    Supported Targets
      - Converted Schemas can be used for RDS Engine types, Aurora, or Redshift
    EC2 and S3
      - you can even use the converted schemas with databases running on EC2 or data stored in S3

  Migration Types to Know
    - 3 different migrations you need to know
    Full Load
      - all existing data is moved from sources to targets in parallel
      - while conversion is in progress, any changes made in your tables are cached on you replication server
    Full Load and Change Data Capture (CDC)
      - Full load plus CDS captures changes to source table during migration
      - CDS guarantees transactional integrity of the target database
    Changed Data Capture (CDC) Only
      - only replicate the data changes from the source database

  Migrating Large Data Stores via AWS Snowball
    Terabytes
      - Some data migrations can include terabytes of data
    Bandwidth Throttles
      - Large migrations can take up bandwidth and cause network throttles
    Snowball Edge
      - leverage certain Snowball Edge devices and S3 with DMS to migrate large data sets quickly
    SCT
      - you can still leverage the SCT to extract data into Snowball devices and then into S3
    Load Converted Data
      - DMS can still load extracted data from S3 and migrate to the chosen destination
    Change Data Capture (CDS) compatible
      - still leverage CDS for capturing changes when extracting data to store in S3

  Exam Tips

    AWS Database Migration Service (AWS DMS)
      Easy Database Migration
        - allows for easy migration of relational databases, noSQL databases, data warehouses, and other
          data stores to AWS
      How it works
        - DMS server runs replication software that executes specified tasks that you assign
      Migration types
        - know the difference between, full-load, full-load with CDC (Change Data Capture), and CDS-only migrations
      Must include AWS endpoint
        - one of the endpoints must be with AWS
        - you can not leverage for external-only migrations
      Schema Conversion Tool (SCT)
        - SCT allows you to convert databases schemas to new or different target database schema
      Snowball
        - you can migrate massive databases to AWS more efficiently by using Snowball devices with DMS

---------------------------------------------------
20.8 Replicating and Tracking Migrations with AWS Migration Hub and AWS Server Migration Service

  Migration Hub
    Single Pane
      -Singular place to discover existing servers, plan your migration efforts, and track migration statuses
    Visualize
      - visualizes connection and server/database statuses that are part of your migrations
    Options
      - options to start migrations immediately or group servers into application groups first
    Supported Tools
      - integrates with AWS Application Migration Service (MGN) and AWS Database Migration Service (DMS)
    Leverages Other Services
      - only discovers and plans migrations, and works with the mentioned services to actually do the migration

  Migration Phases
    1. Discover
      - find servers and databases to plan your migrations
    2. Migrate
      - Connect tools to Migration Hub, and begin migrating
    3. Track
      - follow migration statuses and progress

  Server Migration Service (SMS)
    Automate
      - Automatest migrating on-premise servers to AWS Cloud
    Supported VMs
      - supports VMWare vSphere, Windows Hyper-V, MS SCVMM (System Center Virtual Machine Manager)
        (or just VMM), and Azure VMs
    Incremental
      - incremental replications of server VMs over to AWS AMIs that can be deployed on EC2

  SMS Use Cases
    Simplify Migrations
      - manages all complex parts of a migration to AWS
      - processes like volume replication are handled for you
    Multi-Server Migrations
      - schedule replication intervals however you please
      - track progress for chosen server group
    Wide Support
      - In addition to supporting many types of VMs, it also supports a majority of common operating systems
    Incremental Testing
      - SMS incrementatal migrations allow for quick testing of your migrated servers
    Say No to Downtime
      - Using SMS minimizes downtimes that occur during traditional migration efforts


  Migration Hub Exam Tips
    - scenario-based questions related to tracking and planning migration efforts from either on-premises or
      another cloud vendor could involve Migration Hub
    - it does not do the migration for you, but instead works by integrating with other Migration services

  Server Migration Service (SMS) Exam Tips
     - is perfect for scenarios like simplifying migrations of critical VMs from on-premises, easily migrating
       VMs from other cloud providers, and minimizing downtimes during cutover
       - for migration different types of VMs (VMWare VSphere, Hyper-V, ...)


    https://aws.amazon.com/server-migration-service/
    Product Update: AWS recommends AWS Application Migration Service (AWS MGN) as the primary migration service
       for lift-and-shift migrations. If AWS MGN is not available in a specific AWS Region, you can use
       AWS Server Migration Service (AWS SMS) APIs until March 31, 2023


    https://aws.amazon.com/server-migration-service/faqs/
    Q: What are the main differences between AWS Application Migration Service and AWS Server Migration Service?
    A: Application Migration Service (MGN) utilizes continuous, block-level replication and enables short cutover
       windows measured in minutes. Server Migration Service (SMS) utilizes incremental, snapshot-based replication
       and enables cutover windows measured in hours.

20.9 Migration Exam Tips

   4 questions to ask in the Exam
     1. Where are we going?
        - on-premises to AWS, another Cloud Vendor to AWS, AWS to on-premises?
     2. How do we get there?
        - Do we use snowball devices to do a bulk upload to AWs, migration services like AWS DMS to migrate databases
          in chunks?
     3. Is it all at once?
        - Do we use a snowball device to move all our data, migrate in increments using replication servers to AWS?
     4. Or is it a partial Migration?
        - Use a basic S3 upload, multi-part upload, server replication service, or lift-and-shift everything at once?

   Migrating data with AWS Snow Family
     snowball choices
       - snowballs are perfect for moving many terabytes of data
     when do we use snowball
       - best used in situations where you have slow or no internet. Snowball is faster and more secure in
         these situations

     Snow Family
       - use cases
         - know the general use cases for each snow family member
       Snowcone
         - small, robust edge computing
         - 8 TB of storage, 4 GB of memory, and 2 vCPUs
       Snowball
         - Storage
         - 50 TB (42 TB usable) US regions only and 80 (72 TB usable) in storage
       Snowball Edge
         - Storage, Compute, and GPU
         - 100 TB (80 TB usable) in storage
       Snowmobile
         - up to 100 PB in a very large truck
       Data transfer to and from AWS
         - The Snow Family works to get data into AWS, as well as shipped back to you
       Timing
         - generally, turnaround is a week, but that mostly depends on the customer

    Storage Gateway
      hybrid
        - anytime on-premises storage is brought up, think "Which version of Storage Gateway could complement this?"
      Out of Space
        - file gateway is perfect solution if your local network-attached storage is full
      It's a VM
        - Storage Gateway is run locally as VM on-premises
        - you deploy a template file, connect it to your AWS storage solution (e.g. S3, EFS), and connect
          to locally through your Storage Gateway VM

      Storage Gateway
        - Storage Gateway excels at being a Hybrid Storage solution for data migration
        Use cases for File Gateway, Volume Gateway, and Tape Gateway
          - know the general use cases for each storage gateway type
          Storage Gateway
          - e.g. NFS service is out of on-premises storage, you can use storage gateway
          Volume Gateway
            - back up drives
          Tape Gateway
            - replace tape backups

    DataSync and Transfer Family
      DataSync
        - an agent-based solution that excels at one-time migrations of file shares into AWS
      EFS and FSx
        - both viable locations for DataSync to transfer content into
        - EFS for Linux and FSx for Windows for Windows
      Transfer Family
        - allows you to use legayc transfer protocols (SFTP, FTPS, and FTP [FTP is limited to AWS internal])
          to give older applications the ability to read and write from S3

      DataSync
        - is a data migration service
        DataSync vs Storage Gateways
        - dataSync is for a one-time migration
        - for hybrid architecture and a continuous sync, think Storage Gateway
        -  Note: Storage migration can be used for a 1 time migration
        Datasync Use Case
          - primarily for one-time migrations
        Agent Based
          - an agent needs to be installed on the architecture on your end to copy the data to AWS
        Datasync Endpoints (for sending data to)
          - S3, EFS, and FSx are all supported locations for your data

      AWS Transfer Family
        - excels when you have a collection of older applications using protocols that cannot
          be changed. You can easily "trick" these applications into using S3 or EFS storage
        - FTP/SFTP/FTPS related, think Transfer Family
        Legacy
          - this tool excels with bringing legacy application storage to the cloud
        Protocols
          - FTPS and SFTP from outside AWS
          - FTP only from inside your VPC
        DNS
          - the DNS (endpoints) stays the same, but the location for the storage becomes S3

    Moving to the Cloud with Migration Hub
      Migration Hub
        - an organization tool that gives you a way to organize all your migration steps
        - you'll need other tools to complete the migration through
      Database Migration Service (DMS)
        - go-to tool for any sort of database migration
        - it works for on-premise to the cloud, or for moving data between different RDS databases
        - provides you the AWS Schema Conversion Tool (SCT) for porting and converting databases
      Server Migration Service
        - the tool you'll want to use to migrate VMs out of the data center and into AWS


      Migration Hub
        - scenario-based questions related to tracking and planning migration efforts from either on-premises or
          another cloud vendor could involve Migration Hub
        - it does not do the migration for you, but instead works by integrating with other Migration services

      Server Migration Service (SMS)
         - is perfect for scenarios like simplifying migrations of critical VMs from on-premises, easily migrating
           VMs from other cloud providers, and minimizing downtimes during cutover
           - for migration different types of VMs (VMWare VSphere, Hyper-V, ...)

        https://aws.amazon.com/server-migration-service/
        Product Update: AWS recommends AWS Application Migration Service (AWS MGN) as the primary migration service
           for lift-and-shift migrations. If AWS MGN is not available in a specific AWS Region, you can use
           AWS Server Migration Service (AWS SMS) APIs until March 31, 2023

        https://aws.amazon.com/server-migration-service/faqs/
        Q: What are the main differences between AWS Application Migration Service and AWS Server Migration Service?
        A: Application Migration Service (MGN) utilizes continuous, block-level replication and enables short cutover
           windows measured in minutes. Server Migration Service (SMS) utilizes incremental, snapshot-based replication
           and enables cutover windows measured in hours.

      AWS Database Migration Service (AWS DMS)
        Easy Database Migration
          - allows for easy migration of relational databases, noSQL databases, data warehouses, and other
            data stores to AWS
        How it works
          - DMS server runs replication software that executes specified tasks that you assign
        Migration types
          - know the difference between, full-load, full-load with CDC (Change Data Capture), and CDS-only migrations
        Must include AWS endpoint
          - one of the endpoints must be with AWS
          - you can not leverage for external-only migrations
        Schema Conversion Tool (SCT)
          - SCT allows you to convert databases schemas to new or different target database schema
        Snowball
          - you can migrate massive databases to AWS more efficiently by using Snowball devices with DMS

    Application Discovery Service
      - quickly migrate entire applications to AWS
      Agentless Discovery
        - can be used via OVA deployment for VMWare vSphere VM
      Agent-based Discovery
        - install a agent on all VMs and phyical server.
        - agent collects detailed information on the VMs, both Linux and Windows OS's

      AWS Application Discovery Service
        Application Migration
          - to easily and quickly migrate applications to the AWS cloud
        Agentless Discovery
          - You install the Agentless Collector as a virtual machine (VM) in your VMware vCenter Server environment
            using an Open Virtualization Archive (OVA) file.
        Agent-Based Discovery
          - installable agent for windows and linux to collect detailed information on the VMs and physical host

    Application Migration Service [AWS MGN]
      Lift-and-Shift
        - automated lift-and-shift service for migrating infrasture to AWS
      Replicates
        - replicates source servers (e.g. VMs, physical, or Azure VMs) into AWS for non-distruptive cutovers
      RTO and RPO
        - RTO of minutes (based on OS boot time) and RPO of sub-seconds

      AWS Application Migration Service [AWS MGN]
        Lift and Shift
          - automated lift-and-shift service for expediting migration of apps to AWS
          Replication
          - replicates source servers (VMs, physical, or cloud servers) into AWS for non-distruptive cutovers
         RTO (Recovery Time Objective) and RPO (Recovery Point Objective)
           - recovery time objective: typically just minutes; dependent on OS boot time
           - recovery point objective: measured in the sub-second range

21.2 Quick Deploying Web Apps with Amplify

  AWS Amplify
    - offers tools for front-end web and mobile developers to quickly build full-stack applications on AWS
    - it handles deploying the underlining infrastructure so they can focus on the code

  Amplify offered Services
    - offers two services for developers
    Amplify Hosting
      Frameworks
        - Support for common single-page application (SPA) frameworks, like React, Angular, and Vue
        - also supports Gatsby and hugo for static site generators
        Note: SPA: a web app implementation that loads only a single web document, and then updates the body content of
              that single document via JavaScript APIs such as XMLHttpRequest and Fetch when different content is to be shown.
      Environments
        - allows for separate production and staging environments for the front-end and backend
      Server-side Rendering (SSR)
        - support for Server-side Rendering (SSR) apps, like Next.js
        Note: SSR:  rendering on the server. When you do this, you'll create an HTML file with all of the content
                    of the site and send it back to the user. The user will then get an fully rendered HTML page
                    that contains all of the necessary information for them to see your site without having to wait
                    for any JavaScript or CSS files to load.
         - Next.js supports (but React, Angular,..., do not support) both static sites and SSR sites
         - can not do SSR with a static application in S3, so think Amplify

    Amplify Studio
      Easy Auth (Authentication & Authorization)
       - Easy authentication and authorization for implementation within your application with the tools provided
      Simplified Development
        - Visual development environmemt to simplify creation of full-stack web or mobile applications
        - can do everything within the AWS Console
      Ready to Use
        - Offers ready-to-use components, easy creation of backends and automated connections between the front-end
          and backend

  Note: AWS Amplify
        -  offers developers a set of tools for easily deploying full stack applications to AWS.
        - It offers Git-based workflows with automated deployments for web applications, and it is especially
           suited for developers not as familiar with AWS who need to also leverage server-side rendering (SSR)

  Exam Tips

    Amplify
      - could be the answer for scenarios like: managed server-side rendering in AWS, easy mobile development, and
        developers running full stack applications with minimal knowledge of AWS

---------------------------------------------------
21.3 Testing App Services Using AWS Device Farmn

  Device Farm
    - an application testing service for testin and interacting with Android, iOS, and Web Apps
    - it's usable on actual phones and tablets hosted by AWS (uses actual phones/tablets hosted by AWS)
  Way to use Device Farm to Test
    - allows for 2 primary testing methods
    Automated testing
      - upload scripts or use built-in tests (provided for you) for automated parallel tests on mobile devices
    Remote access testing:
      - swipe, gesture, and interact with devices in real time via web browsers (interactive with the device but
        via a browser)

  Exam Tips

    Device Farm [testing app service]
      - exam scenarios that requires application testing on mobile devices in AWS (in particular, needing
        actual phones or tablets ro automated testing), think Device Farm

21.4 Engaging Custommer with Amazon Pinpoint

  Pinpoint
    - enables you to engage with customers through a variety of different messaging channels (e.g. email, SMS,
     and other push notifications)

     Note: SMS (Short Message Service): , is a form of text message that’s sent from one device to another. Notably,
          it’s a text-only format, meaning it doesn’t encompass texts that also relay information through images or videos.
          SMS is used promotional test messaages, appointment reminders, etc.

     Note: Push notifications: clickable pop-up messages that appear on your users’ browsers irrespective of the device
           they’re using or the browser they’re on. They serve as a quick communication channel enabling companies to convey
           messages, offers, or other information to their customers.

  Who Uses Pinpoint
    - primarily intented for marketers, business users, and even developers

  Pinpoint Features
    Projects
      - collections of information, segments, campaigns, and journeys
    Channels
      - the platform where you intend to engage your audience segments
      - how are you engaging your customers, e.g. email, test messages, push notifications
    Segments
      - dynamic or imported
      - designates which users receive specific messages
    Campaigns
      - Initiatives engaging specific audience segments using tailored messages
        e.g. a campaign could be a release of a new feature that you want to market to your customers
    Journeys
      - Customized, multi-step engagements
        - within a journey, you might have multiple steps where you are reaching out to multiple segments
          with different types of messages, and they all make up one or more campaigns
    Message Templates
      - content and settings for easily reusing repeated messages
    Machine Learning
      - leverage machine learning models to predict user patterns

  Where to use Pinpoint
    Marketing
      - promoting products and services via email, SMS, or push notifications
    Transactions
      - messages to customers after transactions (e.g. order confirmations or shipping notifications)
    Bulk communiction
      - messages targeted to millions of people


  Exam Tips

    Amazon Pinpoint
      - questions related to marketing campaigns, user engagements, and sending email to target audiences,
        or scenarios leveraging machine learning with user engagement predictions, think Amazon Pinpoint

    - tools for simplified front-end web and mobile development

    - Leverages machine learning models to predict engagement interactions

22.2 Analyzing Text Using Amazon Comprehend, Amazon Kendra, and Amazon Textact

  Comprehend
    - uses Natural-Language Processing (NLP) to help you understand the meaning and sentiment in your text
    - For example, you can automate understanding whether people are saying positive or negative things
      about your service
    - you can also pick up on key phrases
    - Comprehend is a way for automating comprehension at scale
    - e.g. could use it in twitter conversations about your company to see if they are talking about it
      positively or negatively
    - e.g. used in election cycles to determine if what people are saying and if it positive or negative

  Use cases for Comprehend
    Analyze Call Center Analytics
      - Automatically detect if your customers are having a positive or negative experience with call
        center staff
        - It can be used in conjunction with Amazon Transcribe
    Index and Search Product Reviews
      - Create index and automate your product reviews to detect whether people are happy with your products
        or services or not
    Legal Briefs Management
      - Automatically search all your contracts and for example recent court case decisions
    Process Financial Documents
      - Insurance companies can automate insurance claims and process where common claims are coming from

  Amazon Kendra
    - allows your to create an intelligent search service powered by machine learning
    - Enterprise search applications can bridge different silos of information (such as S3 buckets, file
      servers, and websites), allowing your enterprise to have all the data intelligently in one place
    - can index the data from multiple sites (such as S3 buckets, File server, & web sites) facilitating searching
      these multiple sites

  Use Cases for Amazon Kendra
    Accelerate Reseach and Development
      - previously research papers by scientitsts may be scattered all over the place, such as in S3 buckets,
        databases, and text files.
      - With Kendra, you can consolidate this into one place
    Improve Customer Interactions
      - Use Kendra to better understand what your customers are asking, and the return more relevant answers
        and experiences
    Minimize Regulatory and Compliance Risks
      - you can use machine learning to automate the research of new regulation that may impact your business,
        such as new ISO requirements, HIPAA, or PCI-compliant policies

       Notes:
            - ISO (International Organization for Standards) standards represents best practices across a range of
             operational areas like quality management, IT security, service quality and environmental impact.
             - PCI: Payment Card Industry
             - HIPAA (Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act) - governs how to store US medical records
    Increase Employee Productivity
      - By having your data searchable in one place, you can increase your productivity by ensuring your staff
        are not spending hours trying to find the right data

  Amazon Textract
    - uses machine learning to automatically extract text, handwriting, and data from scanned documents
    - goes beyond OCR
    Beyond OCR (Optical Character Recognition)
     - using machine learning and OCR, Textract can process text, handwritting, tables, and more with no
       manual intervention
     - you can quickly turn text (such as receipts or handwritten letters) into data, which can then store
       in the AWS cloud in databases or S3

  Use Cases for Amazon Textract
    Financial Service
      - your customers may fil in mortgage applications by hand. Using Textract, you can convert their
        handwritten applications into completed forms that readable by a computer
    Health Care and Life Sciences
      - most health insurance claims can involve an element of handwriting (e.g. doctors' notes)
      - automate this into data you can then quickly process
    Public Sector
      - Tax returns completed by accountants or general members of the public using handwriting can
        be quickly converted into machine-readable documents

  Exam Tips

    Comprehend
      - uses Natural-Language Processing (NLP) to help you understand the meaning and sentiment in your text
      - understanding meaning behind the text: In-depth and used for analysis
      - implement sentiment analysis
      Use Cases:
        - index and search product reviews
        - manage legal briefs
        - process financial documents

    Kendra
      - using machine learning to build an intelligent search service using unstructured text
      - allows your to create an intelligent search service powered by machine learning
      - Enterprise search applications can bridge different silos of information (such as S3 buckets, file
        servers, and websites), allowing your enterprise to have all the data intelligently in one place

    Textract
      - used to convert text, handwritting, and data from scanned documents into text
      - uses machine learning to automatically extract text, handwriting, and data from scanned documents
      - using machine learning and OCR (Optical Character Recognition), Textract can process text, handwritting,
        tables, and more with no manual intervention

22.3 Predicting Time-Series Data Using Amazon Forecast

  Time-Series Data
    - data points that are logged over a series of time, allowing you to track your data
    - Examples could be temperature readings from weather stations around the world, on the hour,
      every hour for years

  Time-Series Data Examples
    IoT
      - IoT sensors relay thousands, millions, and billions of points of information depending on the setup
      - one use case is for agriculture (measuring soil water content)
    Analytics
      - Large websites such as Netflix serve millions of users per second. Need to analyze incoming and
        outgoing web traffic
    DevOps Applications
      - applications that change in response to users needs may need to be monitored continuously so they
        can scale correctly

  Amazon Forecast
    - a time-series forecasting service that uses machine learning and is built to give you important
      business insights
    - you can send your data to Amazon Forecast and it will automatically learn your data, select the
      right machine learning algorithm, and then help you forecast your data

  Where to use Amazon Forecast
    1. IoT
    2. Analytics
    3. DevOps Applications

  Exam Tips

    Amazon Forecast
      - if you have a scenario-based question where you need to analyze time-series data and make predictions
        based on that analysis, think Amazon Forecast
      - if you need to store your time-series data, then think Timestream
      - a time-series forecasting service that uses machine learning and is built to give you important
        business insights
      - you can send your data to Amazon Forecast and it will automatically learn your data, select the
        right machine learning algorithm, and then help you forecast your data

    Note:
    Amazon Timestream
    - A serverless, fully managed database service for time-series data
    - you can analyze trillions of events per day up to 1000 times faster and at as little as
      1/10th the cost of tranditional relational databases

---------------------------------------------------
22.4 Protecting Accounts with Amazon Fraud Detector

  Amazon Fraud Detector
    - an AWS AI service that is built to detect fraud in your data
    - create a fraud detection machine learning model that is based on you data
    - you can quickly automate this process

  Amazon Fraud Detector Use Cases
    Identify Suspicious Online Payments
      - train the model to identify suspicious online payments based on previous cases of fraud
    Detect New Account Fraud
      - create a model where you can distinguish between genuine new users or accounts and high-risk
        accounts, and then add additional security checks to the high risk accounts
    Prevent Trail and Loyalty Program Abuse
      - Stop users from automating "free trial" accounts so they get indefinite free services
    Improve Account Takeover Detection
      - automate the detection of account takeovers where an illegitmate user hijacks a legitmate
        user's account

  Exam Tips

    Amazon Fraud Detector
      - look for exam scenarios where you are asked to build a fraud detection machine learning model
        that is highly customized based on your data, then think Fraud Detector
      - an AWS AI service that is built to detect fraud in your data
      - create a fraud detection machine learning model that is based on you data
      - you can quickly automate this process

22.5 Working with Test and Speech Using Amazon Polly, Amazon Transcribe, and Amazon Lex

  Amazon Transcribe
    - used to convert speech to text automatically
    - you can use this service to generate subtitles on the fly
  Amazon Transcribe Use Cases
    Convert Audio and Video Files to Text
      - turn your audio and video files into text in a fast and automated process
      - one specific use case is to generate subtitles

  Amazon Lex
    - allows you to build conversational interfaces in your applications using natural language models
    - chances are that when you talking to an automated bot online, you are interacting with the Lex
      service on the backend

  Amazon Lex Use Cases
    Build Virtual Agents and Voice Assistents
      - create virtual agents to arrange refunds, flight changes, password reset requests, and more
    Automated Information Responses
      - automated the frequently asked questions on your website so users can ask them directly
    Improve Productivity with Application Bots
      - Create chatbots that can interact with your customers and improve the productivity of your
        customer support teams
    Automate Your Contact Center Transcripts
      - automate your existing contact center scripts as chatbots

  Amazon Polly
    - turns your text into lifelike speech an allows you to create applications that talk to and
      interact with you using a variety of languages and accents

  Amazon Polly Use Cases
    Content Creation
      - Polly allows your to create applications that speak rather than just display text, making your
        content more accessible.
      - if you visit the AWS blog, you can see an example of Polly converting blog posts into
        lifelike speech

  The Trio in Action

               Alexa             Amazon Transcribe             Lex                    Polly
     Person -> talks to Alexa -> converts speech to text  ->  provides response -> converts text reponse to speech

  Exam Tips

    Amazon Transcribe
      - exam scenario using machine learning to convert video and/or audio to text (e.g. generating
        closed captioning), think Transcribe
      - used to convert speech to text automatically

    Amazon Lex
      - exam scenario where you need to build a conversational chatbots, think Amazon Lex.
      - this is the service that powers the logic behind A-Lex-a
      - allows you to build conversational interfaces in your applications using natural language models
      - chances are that when you talking to an automated bot online, you are interacting with the Lex
        service on the backend

    Amazon Polly
      - use Polly to convert text into natural speech in multiple languages
      - turns your text into lifelike speech an allows you to create applications that talk to and
        interact with you using a variety of languages and accents

  Amazon Rekognition

22.7 Leveraging Amazon SageMaker to Train Learning Models

  SageMaker Overview
    - a way to build and deploy machine learning models in the AWS cloud
    4 areas/stages:
      Ground Truth
        - set up and manage labeling jobs for training datasets using active learning and human labeling
      Notebook
        - where you write/create your ML models
        - Access a managed Jupyter Notebook environment (where you are going to write your machine learning
          algorithm in python)
      Training
        - Train and tune models [that were developed in Notebook]
      Inference
        - Package and deploy your machine learning models at scale

  SageMaker Two Different Deployment Types
    Offline Usage
      - Usage: Asynchronous or batch
      - Output Format: Varies depending on algorithm
    online Usage
      - when ML model needs to respond in real-time
      - Usage: synchronous or real-time
      - output format: JSON string

  SageMaker Stages
     1. Create a Model
       - this is the place that will provide predictions for your endpoint
     2. Create an Endpoint Configuration
       - where your specify the model to use, inference instance type, instance count, variant name, and weight
       - this can sometimes be called a production variant
     3. Create an Endpoint
       - This is where the model is published, and you can invoke the model using the InvokeEndpoint() method

  Model Training:
         Training data:
           - can come from a variety of sources include AWS Console, SDK, S3 bucket

          AWS console --------------->|                  |----------------------------|
                 Training             |                  |Behind the Scenes           |
                                      |                  |                            |
          SDK ----------------------->|--> Amazon        |                            |
                                      |    SageMaker ------> Container Registry       |
          Training Data (S3) -------->|                  |         |                  |
                                                         |         v                  |
                                                         |      ML Training           |
                       Model Artifacts <----------------------  Container             |
                         (S3)                            |----------------------------|
  Model Creation:

          AWS console --------------->|
             CreateModel              |
                                      |
          SDK ----------------------->|--> Amazon
                                      |    SageMaker ------> Model v1
          Model Artifacts (S3) ------>|
                                      |
          Inference Container  ------>|

  Create A Model
     - specify model artifacts generated via the training process
     - use a model package from the AWS Marketplace

  SageMaker Neo
    - allows you to customize your machine learning models for specific CPU hardware, such as ARM, Intel, and
      NVidia processors
    - it includes compiler to convert the machine learning model to an environment that is optimized to execute
      the model on the target architecture

      Compiler Optimizing Software
      ----------------------------
      TensorFlow (by Google)   |                                              Specific Hardware/CPUs
      Mxnet                    |
      Pytorch (by Facebook)    |       Amazon           Architecture           G3
      Onnx                     |-----> SageMaker  ----> Optimized    --------> MVidia Platform
      dmic                     |       Neo              Binary                 (or ARM, Intel)
      XGBoost                  |

  Elastic Inference (EI)
    - EI speeds up throughput and decreases latency of real-time inferences deployed on SageMaker hosted
      services using only CPU-Based instances.
    - It is much more cost-effective than a full GPU instance

  Automatic Scaling SageMaker Models
    - Dynamnically add and remove instances to a production variant based on changes in workloado
    - you define and apply a scaling policy that uses a CloudWatch metric and target value, such as
      "InvocationsPerInstance"

  High Availability with SageMaker
    - if using SageMaker only 1 AZ, if it goes down, your SageMaker setup will go down
    - AWS recommends setting up SageMaker in multiple-AZs
    - using CloudWatch to tell if AZ is down for SageMaker to automatically provision it in another AZ

  Exam Tips

    SageMaker
      - exam scenarios about training and deploying machine learning models, think SageMaker
      - a way to build and deploy machine learning models in the AWS cloud
      4 areas/stages:
        Ground Truth
          - set up and manage labeling jobs for training datasets using active learning and human labeling
        Notebook
          - where you write/create your ML models
          - Access a managed Jupyter Notebook environment (where you are going to write your machine learning
            algorithm in python)
        Training
          - Train and tune models [that were developed in Notebook]
        Inference
          - Package and deploy your machine learning models at scale

    SageMaker Stages
       1. Create a Model
         - this is the place that will provide predictions for your endpoint
       2. Create an Endpoint Configuration
         - where your specify the model to use, inference instance type, instance count, variant name, and weight
         - this can sometimes be called a production variant
       3. Create an Endpoint
         - This is where the model is published, and you can invoke the model using the InvokeEndpoint() method

    SageMaker Neo
      - allows you to customize your machine learning models for specific CPU hardware, such as ARM, Intel, and
        NVidia processors
      - it includes compiler to convert the machine learning model to an environment that is optimized to execute
        the model on the target architecture

        Compiler Optimizing Software
        ----------------------------
        TensorFlow (by Google)   |                                              Specific Hardware/CPUs
        Mxnet                    |
        Pytorch (by Facebook)    |       Amazon           Architecture           G3
        Onnx                     |-----> SageMaker  ----> Optimized    --------> MVidia Platform
        dmic                     |       Neo              Binary                 (or ARM, Intel)
        XGBoost                  |

    Elastic Inference (EI)
      - Uses CPU inferences instead of GPU inference
    Automatic Scaling
      - supports automatic scaling
    Highly Available
      - can be configured to be Highly Available

  Amazon Translate
    - a machine learning service that allows you to automate language translation
    - using deep learning and neural networks, Amazon Translate allows you to translate
      from one language to another

  Amazon Translate Use Cases

      - translate service is very accurate, and Amazon is continuously innovating to improve the accuracy of it
    Easy to Integrate into Your Applications
      - you can very easily integrate the translation service with your applications using APIs
    Cost Effective
      - extremely cost effective - especially when compared to the cost of hiring human translators
    Scalable
      - whether a few pages of text or a library of novels - Translate will scale with your demands

  Exam Tips

    Amazon Translate
      - exam scenario where you need to translate from one language to another using deep learning / neural networks,
        think Amazon Translate
      - a machine learning service that allows you to automate language translation
